# B = Brand
# Creating Your Brand
### Building your Brand
*There is so much more to having a strong brand than a pretty logo and website… A brand is what people say behind your back. Broadly speaking this is any consistent element across all interactions people have with your business. *
*You need to have consistency of colour scheme, font, logo, imagery, copywriting style etc. You want your brand to have a distinctive look and feel. It is about communicating the visual identity of your business. It leaves a strong impression and makes it easy for your online and offline content to feel unified and purposeful. (There is a list of places your branding will be visible at the end of this training).*
*Mostly importantly your branding needs to reflect you, your school and your target audience.*
*You can download the exact branding document we use for our design work here in BAM, above.*
* *
*1. Clarify your brand identity*
*Starting this process can be overwhelming but the best place to start is to brainstorm the words/feelings you want attached to your business e.g. professional, fun, approachable, trustworthy.
If you have trouble finding these, here are some questions that will help you get to the core ideas.*
*What is important to you?*
*What type of school are you building?*
*What is your school about?*
*What do you want your students to feel? Do you want it to attract young kids but equally not turn off teenagers?*
*How do you want your parents to feel? Do you want parents to relate your business with trustworthiness?*
*How do you want your teachers to feel? Do you want them to be proud of how legit the organisation is that they work for?*
*These words will be helpful when you need to communicate to designers and team members. *
*Having a clear vision for your brand and being able to give a detailed brief will be essential for getting the right branding for you school.*
* *
*2. Build a LOGO bank*
*You will need to use your logo across numerous channels online and offline, it is important that these are consistent and of a good definition. 
To make this process as fast as possible, create one folder that is filled with your logo in different variations of size, colour, grayscale, white on black, black on white, white on transparent etc. Even adding a few on different backgrounds that will be easily sent to a designer as examples of how you normally use it. *
* *
*3. Choose your Brand Colours*
*Getting your colours right is going to be absolutely essential to building your brand.  Make yourself easily recognisable by being cohesive and consistent.  There is nothing that more quickly makes a brand feel cheap and just thrown together than having a million different colours all over everything. *
*Choose a primary colour, secondary colour and off-set (POP) colour.  
 *
*You can view our BAM brand colour guidelines below as an example. You can also use the following brand colour guidelines to help you get started on choosing the right colours for your school.*
![image224.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image224.png)
![image249.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image249.jpg)
*
*
*Choose whether you will use mainly dark tones, light tones, vivid, muted etc*
*Create a **list of colour hex codes** and include gradient variations for variety.
You can use colour generations to help you out such as Coolors found here: ** *
![image251.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image251.png)
* *
*4. Choose your Brand Typography*
*Typography is so much more than just choosing a couple fonts. Although its important that you do that for consistency, your brand typography should as a golden rule make any written information readable. This includes the font type, the character spacing, the line spacing, the colour of text on backgrounds, the use of underlines, borders etc.  When working with a designer, make sure you outline what this is for your brand. *
*Again, here is an example of the typography document we've created for our "Build a Music School" brand.*
![image250.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image250.png)
* *
*Typography is crucial. Judge for yourself, which of the below options draws your eye to the key information first? *
*Option 1 *
![image256.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image256.png)
*Option 2*
![image252.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image252.png)
*Option 2, but why?  Because option 2 has implemented a few typography tricks so that your eye goes to the intended place first.*
*Using comparison.  Although the typeface and capitalisation is the same, the heading is significantly larger than the body text. If you want something to pop, try making the body text smaller.  If everything is large and equally important than nothing is. *
*Using white, blue and orange text on a black frame and then black text on a coloured background.  The heading is therefore easier to read and more eye-catching. *
*The text is grouped and has sufficient empty space around it.  If you're skimming any text, you instantly know the pieces of key information which entices you to read more.  The longer you can keep someone engaged and reading the more likely you are to convert them into a customer or get them to understand whatever you are trying to communicate.  *
*Option 1, although good, it has not made the text easily readable. *
*The spacing between lines is too large, and space between each section too small (not enough empty space).  This makes the sections not as easily definable. *
*This is white text on a busy lighter colour background, to make this easier a simple black overlay over the picture specifically the side with text would make the text pop up.  *
* *
*5. Build a bank of design assets, e.g visual elements of texture, patterns, icons, illustrations, backgrounds*
*A music school, no matter the size, does not have time to spend fussing over making everything pretty.  Save yourself time without compromising your brand by building or getting your designer to build you a stash of your backgrounds, repeating icons and templates.  Next time you need a poster, a sign, a web page, even a mug, you won't have to worry about it not being cohesive or eye-catching because you will have what you need.   *
*NEVER compromise on purpose in order to make it pretty.  Good design brings the copy to life.*
*Here are some examples of our BAM brand elements:*
![image192.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image192.png)
*Some examples of music school brand elements:*
*Epic School of Music : the arrow, the handwriting typeface, the fact the picture is in a poloroid frame, the child being outlined in a white border and the musical instrument icon....these are all brand elements that will repeated over numerous channels and make this music school branding cohesive.*
![image194.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image194.png)
*Monster Music : the red line under the word lessons and the repeating illustration of a monster playing music are awesome brand elements that make this music school easily recognisable. *
![image193.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image193.png)
*Build a music school : the blue/purple gradient, the collage of real-life images of members of the #BAMSQUAD, the black overlay, this is a repeatable background that encapsulates BAM branding.  *
![image196.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image196.png)
*
6. Build your 'Top 100'*
*Real life images of lessons, concerts, summer camps etc are the goal of any music school - because a parent wants to know they can trust you with there child and what they are walking into.   We always encourage to get these where possible and use them all over your branding (with permission obviously).  However if you are anything like us, you end up with thousands and thousands across numerous years and all you need for a website or flyer is just one...one really good picture.  Solve this problem or start off the way you want to finish and create a file of your top 100 pictures.  For every event/photoshoot, filter it down to 5-10 great pictures and add these to your top 100 file.  This will make it easier and faster when dealing with a designer or creating things yourself. *
*Side note : if you haven't got any photos yet there are websites that have free stock images e.g. Pixabay, motion array... this will help you get started and can be swooped out for your own studio pictures eventually. *
* *
*7. Define your brand voice/tone*
*A big part of your brand is how you communicate, the messaging, the tone of the copy you use! Some people will use copywriters for this, some will use chat gpt to get good copy and some will come up with this themselves. Either way here are a few things for you to consider. *
*Brainstorm what you want your brand to stand for and make sure your business is living that vision *
*Be clear on how you’d like your customers to feel about your brand e.g. what do they say about your brand when you’re not in the room?*
*Know the problem that you help people solve*
*Decide on consistent language that can be used across your website especially the 'about us' section, your social posts, your flyers etc.*
*Decide on the hierarchy of information.  We live in an instant gratification society, people scan things rather than reading them or more accurately they scan and try to ascertain as quickly as possible if this is worth there time to read.  To help them, we want the most important, the most enticing copy to be the first thing they look at, and then the next thing to be secondary information and so on and so on.  If you try to communicate too much or make everything the hero, you are forcing them to run a marathon to decipher your offers.   We want to spoon feed them and guide them to what is the most important - what is the most benefit driven part (where they are the hero not your business).  Then we make that part pop - by using good design concepts - colour, contrast, empty space, even capitals and underlining.  *
*Ensure your website name matches your business name *
*Have your own domain email*
*Have branded email signatures*
*Link your email signature to your social media*
*Have a linkedin, FaceBook Business, Instagram business and other social media pages *
* *
*8.  Apply your Brand to your business*
*Here is a list of some the places your branding will be visible:*
*Website: The school's official website is a crucial platform to showcase the brand's logo, colours, typography, and overall design.*
*Social Media Profiles: Facebook, Instagram, Twitter, YouTube, and other social media accounts will feature the brand's logo and visual elements.*
*Physical Signage: The school's name and logo will be displayed prominently on the school's building, entrance, and directional signs.*
*Brochures and Flyers: Printed materials used for marketing and promotion will carry the school's branding.*
*Merchandise: Branded merchandise like T-shirts, hoodies, bags, and accessories can be sold or given away to students and supporters.*
*Advertisements: Whether in newspapers, magazines, or online publications, the school's brand will be present in advertisements.*
*Online Advertising: Digital banners and ads across various websites and platforms will carry the school's branding.*
*Email Newsletters: Branded templates will be used for sending out newsletters and updates to students and subscribers.*
*School Vehicles: If the school owns or uses vehicles for transportation, they can be branded with the school's logo.*
*Outdoor Banners and Posters: Placed strategically around the community to advertise events, concerts, or open enrolment periods.*
*Classrooms and Interior Spaces: Branded posters, wall art, or displays can be placed inside the school's premises.*
*Event Tickets and Programs: Branded tickets and event programs for concerts, recitals, and performances.*
*Community Events and Festivals: The school can participate in local events and festivals, displaying their branding on booths or banners.*
*Local Print Media: Advertisements and articles about the school in local newspapers and magazines.*
*Google Maps and GPS Services: Ensuring the school's correct branding and location is visible on map services.*
*Social Media Ads: Branded ads on Facebook, Instagram, and other social media platforms.*
* *
![image195.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image195.png)
*One of the best things you can do is start observing.  Start observing marketing materials and branding that you like - on webpages especially the ones that cause you to buy, on signs, on flyers, on ads, on everything! Take a snapshot of the ones you like, the ones that work, and add it to a 'swipe' file (a file of inspiration).  It will go 10 times faster if you can communicate using examples of what you want....and will mean time isn't wasted on the back and forth of the design process...and thus be 10 times cheaper to execute.  Most design is not original, it is a compilation of other work put together. *
* *
*8.  Up-skill and Outsource*
*As business owners we need to know enough about the design process and how to communicate what we want in order to **not waste money or our precious time!**  We often know what we like but can't explain why or which part...thus upskilling is important because you may never be the designer but at some point you will need to hire one and they will need to know what is in your brain.   This list should help *
*Read Storybrand*
*Read copywriting secrets*
*Set very clear deliverables e.g. i want three poster templates built in canva.com by x date.*
*Ensure all deliverables are on a platform that you can access and edit easily.  You do not want to be in a situation that you have to go back to the designer every time you need a simple change. *
*Set very clear guidelines on the process e.g. i want a few concepts first, then first draft and then the right to two post-review edits. *
*If you are creating the designs yourself, remember the first draft is supposed to suck. But it is a necessary evil and part of the creative process - the faster you get that one out of your system, the closer you are to your asset. *
*Utilise template generators, remember don't waste time or money on pretty, the most important thing is if it is fit for purpose e.g. Canva.com, motionarray.com*
*Ideally use one person for your design work so that you don't have to keep communicating who you are and what your brand is over and over.  It also gives the designer an ability to get to the root of what you actually like, making it quicker to produce it. *
*However if you prefer, there are many freelance designers out there on websites like these: 
**   
**  
** *
*Ensure brand compliance across your team by communicating clearly what your brand guidelines are.*
*Create or have created for you brand guidelines. These are also known as brand standards or brand manuals, they are a set of rules and guidelines that define how a brand should be presented and represented in various contexts. They serve as a comprehensive reference for maintaining visual and verbal consistency across all brand communications, ensuring that the brand identity remains coherent and recognisable to the target audience.  At minimum create an a4 document of brand guidelines for yourself and your team include hex colour codes, font types, repeating design elements, examples of there application and unique messaging.  *

# Naming Your School
*Whether you have a school or you’re teaching on your own under your own name, we will try to convince you to not name your teaching business after yourself, no matter how big or how small.*
*Here's why: *
*Be taken seriously:*
*Tim's Piano Lessons versus Rockstar Music Academy. Which competition would be more intimidating if they moved in next door to you? We need to create a confident, professional image. Professionalism spells out safety and loyalty for your customers, especially parents who are entrusting their children to you. A strong, professional image will mean that your terms and conditions are taken more seriously too. *
*Branding:*
*It's a lot easier to build a brand around a Music School name rather than your own name. What do you think would sell better; a bunch of Jonny Wilson t-shirts or a bunch of Goodtime Music Academy t-shirts? People want to feel part of something and a brand name helps to achieve this. Maybe you are working toward a music school but currently only teaching one instrument? I started out as Goodtime Drum Academy so that when it came time to add more instruments, I only had to change 1 word; the logo and brand name didn't change.   *
*Be the founder, not the hero:*
* You need to give an impression to your community that your whole school, your team and your staff are fantastic. If it's all about you, people will assume that everyone else is subpart. They will only want lessons with you and your school won't be able to grow to it's full potential. Make yourself the founder of the school - don't make yourself the hero. *
* *
*If you need to change your name:*
*It needs to be cool but it also needs to describe what you do,  fits within your brand and describes your values*
*Try to include Music School or Music Academy within the name of your school to show how professional you are and get taken more seriously*
*Don't be scared if you need to change your name and you've already got students... It's highly unlikely you'll lose students over a name change*
*Don't delay - the longer you wait, the harder and more expensive it will be to change*
*Communicate with your students / customers; you may even want to involve them in the process!*
*Research the name to make sure it's available in your country*
*Search the domain and any variables/abbreviations to make sure they're available*
*Think about if your name explains what you do?*
* *
![image155.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image155.png)
*Why not run a competition with all your students and parents to name your studio then pick your top 3 for everyone to vote on. Jonny’s name actually came from a student that became a tutor: ‘He came up with something close to what we're called (can't remember what it was) and I tweaked it and it was set. He even drew logo ideas on scrap paper (which were awful haha, but it's about bringing people along for the ride).’*
* *

# Creating Your Schools Tagline
*So you’ve got your music school name locked in. Now, let's look at getting you a tagline. A good tagline gives your families and staff the language you want them to use to tell your brand story e.g. they are all about the kids, they really specialise in getting kids performance ready etc.*
* *
*Tagline Examples from the #BAMSQUAD: *
*Creating Tomorrow's Rockstars One Note at a Time*
*Awesome Music Lessons for Awesome Kids*
*Music Lessons and Band Programmes That are as Cool as Your Kids*
*Life's Better with Music*
*Taking you from the practice room to the stage*
*We help you LOVE music*
*Encouraging confidence and creativity through music*
*Where kids come to ignite their passion*
*Using our musical gifts to help grow yours*
*Where young musicians flourish*
*Love. Passion. Prodigy.*
*Where music is meant to be shared*
*Making Music Lessons Fun For All Ages*
*LOVE making music.*
*Out of this World Music Lessons*
*Discover your creativity*
*Join the Sound*
*Dream. Transform. Inspire.*
*Let the music move you*
*Let your creativity take centre stage*
*Discover YOUR voice. With Us.*
*Learning to play, playing to learn*
*Creating a more music world, one student at a time*
*Fun unites, learning excites*
*If it's not epic, it's not worth it and Everything we do is Epic (for Epic music academy)*
*Not your average music school*
*Life is busy... why miss a beat? (for busy beats music school)*
*We Make Musicians *
* *
*Goodtime Music Academy's Tag-lines were:*
*'Life's better with music' - We used this tagline For our in-school programmes*
*'Taking you from the practice room to the stage' - We used this tagline for on-site lessons*
![image343.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image343.jpg)
* *
* *
*Here's a few reasons why you should consider one.*
*Adds another layer of professionalism to your brand:*
*The more professional we appear, the more perceived value we have at our school. *
*It provides additional clarity to why you exist:*
*What you couldn't include in your name can be included in your tagline. *
*It taps into the emotions of your customers:*
*Naturally, customers want to know what's in it for them. How will you bring happiness to them?*
*They're memorable:*
* A tagline helps reinforce your brand in your community.*
* *
*Reasons why most people's taglines are pretty bad:*
*They make it all about themselves:*
*People don't care as much as you do about how great your business is. They want to know what's in it for them.  *
*They don't tap into the mind or emotions of the customer:*
*How does this make them happier? What win are you bringing to their life? What will they achieve with your help? *
*The brand name and tagline together still don't paint a picture of what they actually provide/sell*
*They're too weird or use technical or poetic language people don't understand*
*Don't try to be overly creative with your tagline - get rid of the fluff. It needs to clearly explain what you do and how it will affect someone's life. *
* *
![image155.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image155.png)
*Use Chat GPT to help brainstorm different tagline ideas!*
* *

*Website*
*Flyers*
*Business cards*
*Use when training staff*
*FaceBook page*

# Your Logo
* How visible is your branding? Does your logo pass the logo test?*
*Your logo is going to be everywhere so it need to be great and fit with your branding. Check out Goodtimes logo evolution below:*
![image347.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image347.png)
![image346.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image346.png)

*Remember - You don't need to pick a logo for life, you can always update it in the future.*
*In this training we are going to go through how to design your own logo, what makes a good logo and tips for writing your design brief…. Plus loads of logo examples from the #BAMSQUAD!*
* *
*Tips for making a great logo:*
*Keep it Simple: A simple logo is memorable and versatile*
*Be Memorable: Make your logo stand out and leave a lasting impression*
*Timeless Design: Aim for a logo that remains relevant for years to come*
*Reflect Your Brand: Ensure your logo conveys your brand's values and personality*
*Versatility Matters: Design a logo that looks great across different platforms*
*Scalability is Key: Your logo should work well at any size*
*Stay Appropriate: Create a logo that suits your industry and target audience*
*Be Unique: Avoid confusion with other logos by being distinct*
*Balance Colors and Fonts: Use harmonious colours and readable typography*
*Thoughtful Design: Take your time and plan carefully for a successful logo*
*Work in Black and White: Ensure your logo is effective even without colour*
* *
*How to get your own logo:*
*Unless you're a great designer, don't do your own logo…Research the best freelance design site (e.g. **,**)** then submit your design with a great brief, explaining how you want to be perceived.   *
*Here are some guidelines of what to give as a design brief:*
*5 - 10 logo styles you like from a google search (from completely random brands outside our industry)*
*A short brief about what you want the logo to convey e.g. fun, warm, family, friendly, trustworthy*
*Colour palettes you like (You can google these to get the codes, plus screenshot the colours to attach with the brief)*
*Who the target market is, a logo will look very different if you’re targeting kids ages 7 - 12 Vs adult students only*
* *

*Logo test: Line up all the logos you collected from your competitors and see how your logo measures up against them. You may want to paste them into a document together so you can easily and clearly see how they look next to each other, like this:*
* *
![image317.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image317.png)
* *
*Think objectively and ask yourself questions like:*
*Does our logo pop out more?*
*Is our logo more exciting?*
*Is it simple & uncluttered?*
*Would it go well on a t-shirt or other merchandise?*
*Does it convey the message, feel and culture that we want to have at our school? *
*Feel free to post your logos in the ** to get some feedback! *
![image319.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image319.png)
* *
![image155.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image155.png)
*Once you’ve got your logo (or new logo) it needs to be changed EVERYWHERE. Keep a list somewhere of all the places your logo exists such as: email signature, website, vehicle, merch, books etc... so if you change it again in the future it’s easy to update!*
* *

# Introduction and Jonny's Story
Coming up in this course we're going to have a look into everything to do with your environment such as:
Optimising your teaching space
Gear recommendations
Different types of teaching spaces
Lesson room set ups
Waiting rooms/ Lobby area
Decorating and storage
Renting commercial property
Studio Tours

Jonny's Story
We’re going to go for a drive and I’m going to show you my journey with my teaching spaces and you’ll see that it’s been a long and messy journey.
*1. First up, door to door lessons:*
Door to door lessons are a lot of fun and a great place to start. It's easy to grow fast but you spend time and money travelling between lessons. One great benefit is hospitality; bonus tea, coffee and sometimes a meal!
*2. Hiring a space part-time:*
* *I stumbled across a space where I door-knocked and was able to rent a room where they charged me per half hour that I rented the space for a lesson. This was great because it worked out a cheap option but because the space wasn't just mine, it meant a lot of set up and pack down. Not every student was happy to come in so I still did in-home lessons for some students but my travel time was reduced so I was still able to grow.
*3. Hiring a full-time space:*
 The place was hideous to begin with. I ran working bees and received lots of donations from friends. This was a great location and once I hired this spot, I completely stopped visiting people's homes for lessons; all my students had to come to me from that point on. I grew to a point where I couldn't grow any further and had a waiting list. This is when I decided to begin Goodtime Drum Academy and hired a drum teacher to take on my waiting list. We built another space for the other teacher and began to grow.
*4. Multiple spaces & additional lessons for other instruments:*
* *After filling all the drum lesson slots and growing a waiting list, we hired a 3rd space at the same location. I lived in one of the rooms and in the other 2 rooms, we held lessons for piano, guitar and singing.
*5. Larger building:*
 Moving into one building was exciting and this is when we switched from Goodtime Drum Academy to Goodtime Music Academy. Previously we had lessons happening in 3 different locations on one site, but now we had one building with 8 rooms, 2 offices and a waiting area. The atmosphere was great and people started to get to know each other. We started to build a community which meant people had a sense of belonging; we began to build traction with our students and in our community.
*6. Multi-site model:*
* *We starting leasing a commercial building on a main road with high visibility, which became our 2nd location. The 2 locations were running reasonably well but this did dilute the culture of the team and we found that staff wanted to work at the location that was closest to where they lived. From here, we opened 2 further locations; 4 locations in total with 2 of them leased and 2 of them on a pay-as-you-go basis.
*7. One main location:*
Our 4 locations were strategically placed within the main parts of our city. Our 2 full-time sites had around 600 students and the 2 part-time locations may have had 50-70 students at each. The full-time sites were great and they lived up to this image we were portraying of this energetic, fun place to go, but the part-time sites were not there yet. After some staffing changes and receiving notice from our 1st main site and the huge stress of running multiple sites, we made the major decision to cut down from 4 sites, to 1 amazing, quality site! This was a massive leap of faith... But it worked! Within 6 months, we shot up to 900 students and continued building up from there. The culture of this site is amazing and it was one of the smartest decisions we ever made.
…………………….

# Waiting Room / Lobby / Front Desk Ideas
*Having a fun looking, interactive and professional looking waiting room or entrance space can give an amazing first impression to your school. Below we have got loads of ideas and examples from the #BAMSQUAD of what you could do with your waiting room.*
* *
*Waiting Room Games and Boredom Buster Ideas*
*Interactive Chalk Board*
*Set up a chalkboard or paint a large wall area with chalkboard paint*
*Keep a bucket of chalk, dusters and wipes for dirty hands after they've drawn*
*Write prompts on the chalkboard that can be changed fortnightly. Such as*
*"Favourite ice cream" or*
*"Draw your favourite music teacher" or*
*"What song are you learning in lessons right now?" or *
*"Draw something that makes you smile" *
*Encourage students to add to the chalkboard each week and then come back the next week to see what else was added to the board *
![image12.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image12.jpg)
![image9.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image9.png)
* *
* *
*Create an Arcade Games Area*
*If you have the space, a really awesome way to entertain your waiting area kids, is to create an arcade area for them! This will guarantee your school is filled with laughter and lots of joyful students who can't wait to come to your school every week for lessons. *
![image8.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image8.jpg)
![image7.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image7.jpg)
![image6.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image6.jpg)

![image5.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image5.jpg)

![image2.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image2.png)
* *
*Large Roadmap Vision Board *
*As studio owners, we want to be setting a clear vision and destination for our students as much as possible. A great way to do this is to have a clear, visual roadmap of the journey your students will take when learning with you. Display this large-sized poster somewhere beautiful in your waiting area! *
![image26.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image26.jpg)
* *
![image25.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image25.jpg)
* *
*Wall of Fame*
*Alison Ervin and Chris Lance have a wall of fame in their waiting area. The wall was started in 2017 when we had a teacher move out of state. We asked her to sign the wall just because our school is an old theatre. With our theatre background, we knew it’s a common tradition in many theatres to have cast/crew sign the theatre walls backstage after a production has ended. We figured why not have students sign the wall! Students sign the wall after finishing a lesson book or accomplishing anything musical - such as auditioning college music programs, getting a solo in their ensembles, and more. *

# Storage Solutions
This lesson is going to give you some great storage solutions/ hacks for your school!
Here's a list of clever storage solutions for music instruments and sheet music that a music school owner could use in a small space:
Wall-Mounted Instrument Hooks: Install sturdy hooks on the walls to hang smaller instruments like guitars, violins, or even brass instruments. This keeps them off the floor and easily accessible.
Instrument Shelves with Pegs: Create shelves with built-in pegs to hold instruments securely in place. This works well for wind instruments and smaller percussion items.
Foldable Instrument Racks: Invest in foldable or collapsible instrument racks that can be expanded when needed and easily stored away when not in use.
Vertical Instrument Storage: Utilise vertical space with tall cabinets designed to hold instruments vertically. This saves floor space and makes instruments easy to reach.
Under-Seat Storage: Use benches with built-in storage compartments to keep sheet music or smaller instruments conveniently accessible. This is especially useful for pianists.
Rolling Carts: Utilise rolling carts with multiple shelves to store instruments and sheet music. These can be easily moved around the space as needed.
Customised Shelving Units: Design shelving units with adjustable shelves to accommodate instruments of various sizes. Include drawers for smaller accessories and sheet music.
Pegboard Organisers: Install pegboards on walls and use hooks to hang instruments, such as guitars, ukuleles, and even drums. Attach baskets or containers to hold smaller items.
Hanging Shoe Organisers: Repurpose clear hanging shoe organisers to store small instruments, accessories, and sheet music. Hang these on the back of doors or walls.
Ceiling Hooks: Install hooks on the ceiling to hang larger instruments, like cellos or double basses, using strong ropes or chains.
Sheet Music Binders: Use labeled binders to categorise and store sheet music. This makes it easy to flip through and find the right pieces quickly.
Floating Shelves: Install floating shelves above workspaces or along walls to display and store instruments or decorative music-related items.
Rolling Drawers: Incorporate rolling drawers under benches or tables for easy access to sheet music and smaller instruments.
Accordion File Organisers: Use accordion-style file organisers to store and categorise sheet music. They take up minimal space and keep music neatly organised.
Instrument Wall Racks: Install specialised wall racks designed to hold multiple instruments in a compact arrangement, turning instruments into decorative pieces.
Piano-Top Storage: Attach small shelves to the top of pianos for storing sheet music, metronomes, and other frequently used accessories.
Cubby Storage: Install cubby-style storage units with different-sized compartments to accommodate a variety of instruments and sheet music.
Drawer Dividers: Use drawer dividers to create compartments for smaller instruments and accessories within larger storage units.
Multi-Functional Furniture: Invest in furniture pieces that serve dual purposes, such as a storage ottoman that holds sheet music or a keyboard with built-in storage.

*Use the Instruments as a Wall Feature*

# Renting Commercial Property
 In this lesson we will be talking about renting commercial property. It will go through:
What you should consider before renting
Interviews - one with a #BAMSQUAD member on his journey to renting his first commercial space and one with someone who owns commercial property and a realestate agency
New Commercial Space Expenses Checklist
FAQ's from the #BAMSQUAD

*What to Consider Before Renting a Commercial Space*
*
*Renting a commercial property for an independent music school requires careful consideration to ensure the success and sustainability of the school. Here's a comprehensive list of things the music school owner should consider before finalising the rental:
Location: Choose a location that is easily accessible, safe, and convenient for students and parents. Consider proximity to residential areas, schools, and public transportation.
Size and Layout: Ensure the property has enough space to accommodate classrooms, practice rooms, administrative areas, and waiting areas. A well-designed layout can enhance the learning experience.
Acoustic Environment: Check the building's acoustics. Sound isolation is essential to prevent disturbances between rooms and to maintain a conducive learning environment.
Zoning Regulations: Confirm that the property is zoned for educational use, and check any restrictions that might affect the operation of a music school.
Cost: Assess the rental cost and ensure it fits within the budget. Consider additional expenses such as utilities, maintenance, insurance, and potential rent increases.
Lease Terms: Carefully review the lease agreement. Pay attention to the duration of the lease, rent escalation clauses, early termination options, and any restrictions on modifications.
Parking: Sufficient parking space for students, parents, and staff is crucial. Check if the property has dedicated parking or nearby public parking options.
Accessibility: Ensure the property is accessible to people with disabilities, complying with relevant accessibility laws.
Safety and Security: Evaluate the safety of the area and the property itself. Adequate security measures, such as lighting, alarms, and locks, are essential.
Local Competition: Research existing music schools in the area. Consider the competitive landscape and identify what makes your school unique.
Future Growth: Think about the potential for growth. Is there room to expand as the music school grows, or will you need to find a larger space in the future?
Amenities and Infrastructure: Check for amenities like restrooms, heating/cooling systems, and internet connectivity. These are essential for a comfortable learning environment.
Community Engagement: Evaluate the community's interest in music education. Consider the demographics and cultural preferences of the area.
Local Regulations: Research local business regulations, permits, and licensing requirements specific to operating an educational facility.
Local Noise Ordinances: Ensure compliance with local noise ordinances to prevent disturbances to neighbours.
Renovation and Customisation: Check if the property requires any renovations or customisation to meet the specific needs of a music school.
Exit Strategy: Plan for the possibility of outgrowing the space or needing to relocate. Review the lease terms regarding subletting or early termination.
Insurance: Obtain appropriate insurance coverage to protect the music school from potential risks, including liability and property damage.

*Learn from a Real Example!*
To help you with the process, download the business plan for Art & Soul Music Studios. This plan shows how they grew their music school and expanded to a new location. It’s a great template that can help you build and grow your own music school, just like they did.

*Software Options for Hosting Lessons*
* *Melissa made this great video to help her students fix their sound on zoom:

* *Here is what Annie has to say about her software choice:
*We use Zoom. I am aware it is not the best platform for music and they change the audio settings quite often which causes us all a bit of a headache however as far as I am aware, the breakout room function is not built into any of the other software specifically designed for musicians  (yet… we have hope one day this will come!) and we very much depend on this for our hybrid practice class structure which gives the students their own privacy and ability to practice in their breakout room without distraction of the others for most of the session and then the ability to come together at the start/end of class. We have a tech trouble-shooting guide we send to all students:*

*Recital Ideas*
![image107.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image107.png)
Here's what Annie's school, Play Well does:
*I set a date for our annual online concert each year and get students to select a piece according to a theme- eg. This year was Screensounds where we all played music from movies/tv series, video games and I get them all to create a recording of the piece as well as submit a short scene that reflects what they are playing and artwork for the cover of each piece. We held a circus themed concert too where everybody submitted a trick (we even had some of our 60 yo students doing the splits in their ugg boots!) Check out the links below if you want a good laugh!). We post out a packet of popcorn to all performers and tell them to share the link with their friends and family and we all tune in together and watch it as a premiere event on Youtube. We used to hold a Zoom party beforehand where they tuned in wearing funny hats and there were prizes for the best hats. This year we had prizes for best costumes, best ham acting and people’s choice where they all have to vote for their favourite of each category by the deadline and then we distribute prizes. Students practice like crazy for it and create their best recordings which we then repurpose for marketing for years to come. *
*You can see links to our past concerts here:*
More Wisdom from Annie below:
*What set up or requirements do you have from the students? *
*As for this, we ask students to set up a device to the side of their pianos so we can see their hands and they can see the screen. We discourage use of mobile phones (simply as the screen is too small to be truly effective and the audio is shocking!) so laptops with bigger screens are much better. Some students have two devices going to present multiple angles simultaneously but this depends on their Internet speed which may be glitchy if their connection isn’t fast enough and the student’s tech-savvy factor (we work with a lot of older adults so this isn’t always feasible without causing serious frustration on their end). We also recommend they have an iPad in addition for reading any music that isn’t in their books or if we need to flick a score across to them, or if we want them to play along to some backing tracks in the Supersonics app.*
*What tips and tricks have you got to make online lessons feel like in person lessons?*
*Online lessons do feel different to in-person lessons but you can make them very enjoyable. *
*Treat the student the same was as if you were in the room with them. *
*Be personable and respond to things you see in their environment- pets, people/family, use emojis, show them things like your own pets if you have them.*
![image733.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image733.png)
*Mix up camera angles and move around, keep it dynamic and don’t just stay in one spot all of the time. *
*What’s one thing you know now that you wish you knew when you started online lessons?*
*That they are not for everyone and that’s ok. I know a lot of people still resist wanting to learn online, especially if they have had experience previously of learning in-person as they will always compare to their prior experiences which is human nature, however believe in yourself, believe in the power of learning online and communicate that with conviction to those who are on the fence about giving it a go. Prepare for rejection yet prepare for success as well. I assure them they will be surprised and delighted at how effective this style of learning can be and it only takes a handful of students to share great testimonials to support your mission. *
![image721.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image721.png)

### C = Compelling Offer……………
# The Annual Calendar Concept
An annual calendar is a yearly routine where you have certain weeks of the year that are different and you are offering something such as workshops or concerts INSTEAD of normal lessons. For example GTMA held something called 'concert week' where the lesson fees were put towards the concert and helped to pay staff for the concert time and helping the staff feel fresh ready for the concert and for them to do concert prep. Students wouldn't get a refund for that week as it was all built into the school fees.
This lesson is going to go through:
Benefits of an annual calendar for you
Planning your annual calendar - including a template for you to use
How to transition to an annual calendar
LOADS of theme ideas
Additional examples from the #BAMSQUAD

*Benefits of an Annual Calendar for you*
Get you more time for money
* *Give a better student experience and help them develop their musicianship
Increase your value stack
Save you money on staff wages in certain weeks
Give your teachers some variety in their weeks
Puts you into an annual sign up situation where you can account for missed lessons within your weeks and get rid of missed/ catch up lesson policies
Easier to incorporate concerts and save you some time
This plan will help you even out your monthly payments throughout the whole year so you don't have months with big drop offs

*Planning your Annual Calendar*
Here are some guideline steps to help you plan your annual calendar:
Write out the months you want your year to run from e.g. September - August or January to December and include weeks 1 - 5 for each month
Write in any public holidays or weeks you will be shut (e.g. Christmas)
Write in your concerts, this will mean no lessons that week as it will all be prep for the concerts
Add in theme weeks or months (see below for some ideas)
Make sure themed weeks and concerts are spread out enough to allow preparation time if needed.
 Here is a template example you can use to help you out:
![image712.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image712.png)
You can access and make a copy of the template here:
* *
#BAMSQUAD member Jacob Burrows made his annual calendar using the template above and he'd laid it out like this:
![image727.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image727.jpg)

*How to transition to this model*
You need to communicate to your students and parents that you are planning next years annual calendar. You want them to give you feedback about all the things that they would like through an ideas wall (using post it notes), surveys, suggestion box etc while sneakily feeding them some of your ideas when you're encouraging them to give ideas. Having this done somewhere that people see other people doing it will help!
After this you can thank everyone for their input and ideas then introduce your new calendar concept and clearly outline all the experiences they will get. Something important to add in is as clause such as: *We have built into our price knowing that certain things you may opt out of, we want you to come and try everything and we are making it available to everyone... Everything is going to be amazing and might push you out of your comfort zone. But this is our annual calendar and if you chose not to come along to something there are no refunds as we have accounted for some people not coming to something or missing weeks within our billing process.*
Remember - people don't always buy in unless they've had a chance to weigh in.
 Below is a video made my Kathryn at West Coast Music Academy where she is introducing the new changes that are coming, check it out below:
 Here is another from Brejoya Smarr at Daytona Beach Music Academy:

*Presenting Your Annual Calendar*
 Here we have some examples of how #BAMSQUAD members have presented their annual calendar to their students and parents.
![image725.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image725.png)
![image724.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image724.jpg)
![image711.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image711.jpg)
![image1003.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image1003.jpg)
![image999.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image999.jpg)

Theme Week/ Month Ideas
*Concert week*
* *It can be exhausting for your teachers and other staff if they have a normal teaching week followed by putting on a concert at the weekend (or other time) and cost you a lot more to the pay the staff for the extra hours. Instead of running lessons have a concert week or weeks built into your annual calendar where the lesson fees go towards the concert and paying your staff to be there for the whole concert day/evening. Additionally you can also get the teachers to be prepping anything that's need for that concert week too if needed.

Here's an example of a concert week poster - Notice that the reminder is on the poster about the concerts replacing lessons.
![image988.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image988.jpg)

*Band Week/ Month*
 Put all the students into bands for a month, having already learned their parts in the lessons in preparations. Instead of normal lessons for the week or month the students are put into bands that you organise and they rehearse the songs together.
This can give a new challenge for the students and improving their skills as well as giving the staff some variety to their weeks.

*Guest musicans performing with students*
Find a local popular covers band to come in to the school or another venue to perform with the students. Find out what songs from their set list they do in the original key so students can learn those songs in their lessons, along with the backing tracks. You can then pay the band to come for the day and students can play along side the band. The guest musicans could either play alongside the students or step out if the student was confident enough to hold the part on their own.

*Video/ YouTube Week*
 You have a room set up with lights, cameras, sound equipment and the students come in to film a professional video of a piece of music that they chose (could also be something from a concert.) In the lessons the students are prepping for their video performance then book in a slot to come and film in that video week in groups. Students who are not performing can also learn some other skills such as the lights and camera etc. The students get two takes to record their performance and get their recording.
Once they have their recording they can set up their own YouTube channel and you give them a sheet with guidance on how to create the channel, set up canva to make a thumbnail for the song, how to load in all the parts. It can be part of a competition for all the different videos with awards such as: the best dressed, most improved, best thumbnail, most views, best performance, best title etc.

![image985.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image985.png)
![image982.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image982.png)
![image979.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image979.png)
*Coaching Call Week*
Instead of having a normal lesson that week students can have access to 10 hours (or a different number) of calls with the teachers where they can ask questions and get instant feedback with lots of opportunities to to go, which would equal more time than their usual lesson time. You can pair the teachers up to do this and students drop in to ask their question. Record the videos too and let the students have access to them.
You can then cut down the teachers time of teaching a lot and use the other time they should be teaching to put back into the school, as well as giving them a break from teaching.

*Workshop Week*
Offer a week where students can attend 2 workshops during the week (included in their current fees) and then give a charge for any attended after that. From working with your teachers and their strength have all these different workshops lead by the teachers over the week in their normal lesson time. They should be planned out so that students of different levels (and maybe instruments) can attend to try something different.
Teachers can use their time gained from other weeks (e.g. coaching call week above) and prep their lessons, workbooks, playlists and resources to deliver a really well planned workshop.

*Mega Practice Challenge Week*
Something to consider - as your building your annual calendar think about if there are times where you need a bit more of a break e.g. week after a concert.
Design a really cool gamified week where students get some sort of challenge of how long they practice for each day with a self and what they worked on or how many minutes then there are some epic prizes up for grabs. Everything for this could be prepped before hand and so you get some hours back that week.
Additionally there can be more videos done by teachers with practice tips or how people can get better that you give out during the week. You just need to find ways that add value to the students in that week.

*Other ideas:*
Adult choir
Songwriting/ composition
Jam nights
Improvisation sessions
Songbook sessions (e.g. Taylor Swift)
Rare music (rare chords etc)
Ukulele club
Intro to different instruments
Film and game scores
Performance skills
Confidence skills
Acapela
Battle of the bands
Musical theatre
Pitch off
Music production
Recording skills
Instrument making
Intergenerational weeks (e.g. parent week)
Music theory and musicianship
Music history and musicology
Music and wellness
You can find even more ideas for this module 4. We have a lesson called 'Themed Weeks' under 'A = Additional Programmes.'

# Your Core Offers
This lesson will be focusing on creating your core offer and value stack. We will be covering:
What is a core offer?
Value stacks
Other examples of value stacks for different types of offers

*What is a Core Offer?*
This is something that you promote after a student has taken your compelling offer e.g a free trial or an ‘intro to music’ course and you’re now making another compelling offer into your core lesson program which will provide them with recurring lessons and you with recurring revenue.
The idea here is to present the core lesson program with a value stack.
*Value Stack*
A value stack is a list of EVERYTHING that is offered for students who sign up for lessons and all the elements have a price attached to them. You want to include the feature, the benefit and the value. For example:
Feature = All access video library with unlimited videos
Benefit = This means you're never stuck at home with your learning, speeds up progress and gives you 24/7 support
Value = $400
You then list your whole stack, in an easy to read format and total that price at the bottom. You  give a new cheaper price which is what students will pay. This will make your customer feel like they’re getting so much more for their money and is a great way to present your offer. Here’s an example of the value stack method:
36 x 45-min Group Lessons ……………………….  Value $1,500
Free curriculum workbooks and PDF’s …………..  Value = $200
All Access Video Library …………………………...  Value = $400
All access app……………………………………….  Value = $100
Unlimited Q&A support……………………………… Value = $200
$0 Registration Fee………………………………….  Value = $100
4 x Epic Performance opportunities p/a…………… Value = $200
1 x Rock Band experience programme p/a ………. Value = $200
Rockstar photoshoot…………………………….…… Value = $50
Regular competitions and prizes……………………  Value = $150
Priority placement on holiday programmes……….  *Bonus
Awesome waiting room experience……………….   Priceless
Making loads of new friends………………………..  Priceless
Access to GTMA referral refund plan…..…………   Priceless
TOTAL VALUE = $3,100
YOUR PRICE = ONLY $45 per week (*billed monthly)

This way the price is anchored by the true value and not by the lesson amount and people feeling self entitled to getting free access to all the other benefits you create for students. Show the smallest number as a weekly amount to make the contrast feel as big as possible.
You can have options for prices. E.G. in this example it could be:
$30 p/w when billed annually,
$35 p/w when billed quarterly
$40 p/w when billed monthly
$50 p/w when billed weekly
* *
The more additional benefits you have, the more you can charge, so every time you add a new feature or experience in your programme you add it to your list. For example, let’s say you create an annual youtube week where students set up their own channels, building videos, thumbnails etc… Maybe you hire a videographer for that week. You then add that experience into your stack as something with a fixed value. E.g. $75 extra per person to cover lesson planning, videographers film / edit time, workbook, competition prizes etc.
![image195.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image195.png)
When you create your value stack you need to include EVERYTHING that goes into lessons, big or small to help increase the value! Remember: Feature, Benefit, Value.

Make sure you charge the prices based on 52 weeks. So if you’re saying $40 per week billed monthly, the maths looks like this:
52 weeks x $40 = $2,080
$2,080 / 12-months = $173 per month

Don’t calculate your monthly price based on 4 weeks per month as that only adds to 48 weeks instead of 52. You don’t want to sell yourself short of 4 weeks' revenue.
If you have a 10-month annual calendar, you will divide by 10 months, so the maths looks like this:
52 weeks x $40 = $2,080
$2,080 / 10-months = $208 per month

Other Value Stack Examples
Below we have some other value stack examples for different types of schools or offerings. You can use these as a starting point to make your own.

Unlimited Q&A support……………………………… Value = $200
$0 Registration Fee………………………………….  Value = $100
2 x Epic Performance opportunities p/a…………… Value = $100

TOTAL VALUE = $2, 250
YOUR PRICE = ONLY $40 per week (*billed monthly)
*Group Drum Teacher*

Free curriculum workbooks and PDF’s …………..  Value = $200

Rockstar photoshoot…………………………….…… Value = $50
Regular competitions and prizes……………………  Value = $150
Making loads of new friends………………………..  Priceless
TOTAL VALUE = $3,050
YOUR PRICE = ONLY $43 per week (*billed monthly)

### Building A Compelling Offer………………..
# What is a Compelling Offer?
* What is a compelling offer?*
A compelling offer is a great offer that stands out above your competitors and is something that draws people in to trial your services and sets them up for continuing into your main lesson programmes.
Most music teachers offers are so lame they would be better off not using them at all!

*Crappy / Stingy offers are things like:*
$30 OFF a full terms lessons
First lesson half price
Get your 10th lesson free
 None of those get you excited to make me want to sign up, right?
![image1066.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1066.png)
Here's an example of the least compelling offer I've ever received in an email recently - Don't be this guy! 👎 No one cares about 10% OFF ❌ ❌ ❌

*What you have to remember:*
When someone enquires about lessons, they may have actually enquired with 2-5 other teachers and studios. How are you going to stand out amongst those that have just shared their lessons prices and details?
You're going to stand out with a compelling offer that looks better than your competitors.
People love a deal and a great first impression, so give them one.

*What makes a compelling offer?*
Remember, you can't fool people into thinking a bad or average offer is good. It actually has to be great.
Here's some things that make a compelling offer great:
- Great content and experiences at a great price (e.g. 30-days for $30, First month Half price etc)
- Bonuses (e.g workbook, guitar pick, drumsticks, gift bag etc...)
- Scarcity (e.g. Only 7 spots left)
- Urgency (e.g. Register today and save 20%)
- Risk reversal (First lesson absolutely free)
* *
*Compelling Offer Examples*
Free Trial Lesson
First lesson 1/2 price
2 for 1 - They pay for one lesson but book two weeks in a row.
2nd lesson free - A different variation on the 2-4-1 deal
FREE $30 voucher - If your lessons are $40, give a $30 voucher away so the first lesson is only $10. Make the vouchers less than they need to buy a full lesson, or a full month of lessons so they have to pay the rest.
First lesson free - we only charge if you don't turn up. This way you take their credit card at the booking and only charge if they 'no-show'.
First month half price
$0 Registration + First month half price
Free studio tour / interview
First month only $99 (or any number you think sounds good)
Double your lessons for the first (x weeks/month). E.g double lessons for 2 weeks
Get a free instrument when you join today on our quarterly plan (they pay 3-months in advance and get a free beginner guitar / keyboard etc - works well if you also have a registration fee to help with some of these costs.
Free instrument hire for X months
Family Freebies. Get an additional X lesson vouchers free for other family members for the next X days (e.g. 30-days) Sign up for one instrument, get a 50% discount on the second instrument’s lesson for X-months.
* *
*Our two best performing compelling offers were:*
FREE TRIAL LESSON
INTRO TO MUSIC PROGRAM
I'm going to talk about the free trial lesson first, then in the next video i'll give you everything you need to build an intro to music programme that's going to grow your school like mad!
First, let's talk about free trial lessons because it's always a hot topic and even here in the #BAMSQUAD there have been a few debates on it over the years.

*The Great Free Trial Debate of... Forever*
There's been so much debate in the music teacher / music school community about whether you should do free trials or not. The arguments from the 'negative' side centre around not wanting to work for exposure or for free.
If you're a local teacher with a full schedule and no room to grow, then you probably don't need to offer a free trial.  But, if you don't just want to own a job, but want to own a real business and grow it fast, then a compelling offer is one of the fastest ways to grow your business.
A free trial is one type of a 'compelling offer'.

*Ask yourself this...*
If FREE TRIAL Lessons (or a variation of a free trial) brought you in 5-20 new students p/w and 60% of them converted into paying customers. Would you care about the 4 people that you had to give a free trial to that didn't sign up?
Your answer should be no!

*How we could afford to grow using FREE trials:*
When our teachers were contractors, we set a deal up with our team that when doing a free trial lesson, your pay was  'Double or nothing'. If the free trial signed up for lessons afterwards, you got paid double your normal rate for that lesson. If they didn't sign up, then you got paid nothing.
This removed the risk for us entirely, so we could give away unlimited free trials. Teachers worked hard and learned tips and tricks to convert students into regular lessons.
One day we realised that free trials were converting at over 80%, so we ended up removing the double or nothing policy and just paying people to teach the lesson normally. The double or nothing policy worked best with contractors and would not be legal here for employees.

*If you have trouble with no-shows for free trial, do this... *
We were lucky in that 'No-shows' were very rare when it came to FREE Trials.
If you are having trouble with no-shows and you've already implemented things like reminder emails and texts on the day to the customer, then you should change your compelling offer so that people have some skin in the game (e.g they pay something). Here's some great examples you can test:
 First lesson $1, $2, or $5
2 for 1 - They pay for one lesson but book two weeks in a row.
2nd lesson free - A different variation on the 2-4-1 deal
FREE $30 voucher - If your lessons are $35, give a $30 voucher away so the first lesson is only $5. Make the vouchers less than they need to buy a full lesson, or a full month of lessons so they have to pay the rest.
First lesson free - we only charge if you don't turn up. This way you take their credit card at the booking and only charge if they 'no-show'.
First month half price
First month only $99 (or any number you think sounds good)

*So, remember. The argument is NOT, should you give a free trial or not!!! *
The point is, a FREE TRIAL is a type of compelling offer.
AND, your business needs a compelling offer.
You can test a free trial or any of the above variations.
Here's what some experiences members have shared:

*Converting FREE TRIALS into regular students:*
Here's an example of a flyer we used in free trial lessons. Teachers would fill this out with the students in the lesson so they could walk away with something tangible showing what they learned etc.
The student and parent would then be instructed to fill in the final section and either go talk to the reception desk right then or scan/photo it through via email that night if they wanted to have a chat about it first.
Our team then calls every trial the next day to check in how it went and to close the sale and enroll them into regular lessons.
![image1025.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1025.png)
![image1022.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1022.png)

*Still not** converting Free Trials into regular students?*
If you're getting leads who are coming in for free trials then not converting to lessons, it's most likely not the free trial that's the problem.
The problem could be:
- Your lesson / teaching wasn't good enough and they weren't inspired to continue.
- The on-going price was too much
- There was something else. E.g. It was too far to drive, the time didn't work, the parking was tricky, the waiting room was freezing etc...
You have to ask people why they didn't continue. Sometimes you'll have to chase them for an answer.

# Our #1 Compelling Offer - Intro To Music Programme
What I'm going to show you today is the offer that broke our record and signed up 155 students in one day.

*What is an intro to music programme? *
A short term programme / package where students can learn with you for a set number of weeks. The programme is designed to be super fun and give students some big wins and awesome experiences.
When packaged right it's a super compelling offer for beginners. But the principle of it can be applied to any course you want to run for any age group.

*Why it's awesome:*
You can create an ITM programme for any instrument (E.g. intro to guitar), a group of instruments (Intro to music) or a niche course (E.g. Intro to Jazz piano).
Chances are, no one else is doing it in your area
It stands out from the competitors so if people have enquired with 3-4 other studios, you've got a much better shot of being selected.
Not a long term commitment
Tests to see whether kids want to stick at it (risk reversal for parents)
Perfect for the child that isn't sure what instrument they want to learn
Very easy to market and get huge student growth
Very easy to up-sell into regular lessons afterwards
People are happy to learn in groups, then stay in groups
You can easily launch new classes anytime.
Very easy to train any team members to be able to deliver

*Here's what the offer looks like: *
*Please Note - I've improved the offer to how I would promote it today

INTRO TO MUSIC PROGRAM
Looking for the perfect beginner music lessons for your child? Goodtime Music Academy's 'Intro to music' programme is the perfect place to start. In just 5 weeks you'll learn the basics on 5 of the most popular instruments, PLUS you'll get to experience playing them in all in a band. Here's what you'll get access to:
5 x 45min lessons with a small group of new friends
Your very own intro to music workbook
Free membership to our 'All Access' video library to support you with additional learning from home
Rock band week, where you'll get to experience rocking out in a band and doing a rockstar photoshoot
Every Student also completes the course with:
An 'intro to Music Certificate' and graduation photo
A 'Goodtime Music Academy' Wristband
A video of themselves performing with the class
Photos from their Rockstar photoshoot
### Register this month and ONLY PAY $99
### Enrolments are now open for our next intake - Fill in the form below to apply
**Please note: Spaces are very limited, so get in quick*
[INSERT REGISTRATION FORM]

See how much more compelling that is compared to regular lesson?

# Fast-track Marketing For Your Compelling Offer
The goal of this lesson is to show you how to rapidly fill your ITM classes with the best marketing strategies that work

*Coming up in this lesson:*
Create a lead/ landing page for your Intro to Music Classes
Elements to include on your lead page
Headline example for your lead page
Great landing page examples from the #BAMSQUAD
Promotional video script example for your lead page
FAQ's to cover on your lead page
Video testimonials
Create a FaceBook/IG campaign
Share on all your socials
Email your list
Community Facebook Groups
Local Schools
Student Tik-tok Strategy
Cross promotions
Posters for internal use
Train the team to promote the programme
Extra ideas, feedback and examples from the #BAMSQUAD

A few things to note:
It’s easier to schedule when you have a number of students lined up and ready to start at the same time. This means your class will be more fun, will run profitably and will allow those students to an easier transition into your ongoing programs afterwards. To do this you need to pick a realistic launch date and a great marketing plan leading up to that date.
We want to get your marketing up and running first, and then you can focus on perfecting your curriculum once the marketing blocks are in place. If you try to perfect your curriculum first, you may never fill your classes as it can always be “better” but you don’t want perfect - You just want “done”
It’s motivating to have students ready to start…that will force you to just pick a curriculum and start (more on curriculum soon)
We’ve included a list of 10 great marketing ideas here to fill your intro classes fast. There’s also hundreds of great ideas in *Module 5* that you can use for marketing for new students. A lot of these ideas can be used without spending money on ads.
In the next lesson we’ll go through a bunch of ideas for how to market your program on Facebook using paid ads. Which is still an incredibly effective form of advertising

*ITM Marketing Plan:*
If you want to get 20, 50, or 200 ITM students fast, then if you execute this marketing plan, you will smash your goals.

1. Create a Lead/Landing Page for your intro to music classes
*Here are the elements you want on included in your lead page: *
Headline
Short promotional video
Programme details and benefits (use the copy from the offer)
Photos of the programme in action (use placeholders from BAM library until you get your own)
Price
Location and contact details
Call to action. E.g Register below
Opt-in form or info pack download
FAQ's

*Headline Example for your lead page: *
Our 5-week 'Intro To Music' course is the perfect first music lesson experience for your child!

*Promotional video script example for your lead page:*
Here's is an example promo video I whipped up. This example is just a basic face to camera example with some B-roll behind it. This can be done very simply with photos if you don't have video footage available.
(*I forgot to add background music - make sure you do that in your video so it's more enjoyable to watch)

*Here's a rough script you can edit for your own video:*
Are you looking for the perfect beginner music lessons for your child?
My name is Jonny, Founder of Goodtime Music Academy and I want to tell you quickly about our brand new 'Intro to music' programme that has been designed to give your child an incredible musical kick-start in the most fun way.

Here's how it works...
In just 5 weeks you'll learn the basics on 5 of the most popular instruments, PLUS they'll get to put it all together playing them in all in a band.
This is a great way to let your child give music a go without needing to commit to on-going lessons right away.

Here's what you'll get access to:
5 x 45min lessons with a small group of new friends
Your very own 'intro to music' workbook so that your child can remember all the amazing things they learned in lessons.
Free membership to our 'All Access' video library to provide additional support and learning from home
Rock band week, where you'll get to experience rocking out in a band and doing a rockstar photoshoot

Every Student who completes the course also gets:
An 'intro to Music Certificate' and graduation photo
A super cool 'Goodtime Music Academy' Wristband.
A video of themselves performing with the class
Photos from their Rockstar photoshoot
So, it's the perfect collection of souvenirs to show off to friends and family!
If your child hasn't experienced our intro to music programme yet, then click the button below and get them registered today for our next in-take.
We can't wait to meet you all soon!
*(*Don't include the price in your video, or you won't have the flexibility to change it)*

*Here's an example of how Tim copied this video for his school:*

FAQs to cover on your lead page (or info pack)
Make sure that you have a clear understanding of how the following things work with the program, so you can answer any questions confidently. These are things that you and your team should be able to answer on the phone, in your info pack / on your lead page.
Curriculum - What will the cost of the book be, is it included in the program? What materials will they need at home… do they need an instrument for the 5 weeks? What will the child be able to know by the end of the program? In the books provide in this Intro to Music course, we’ve included a checklist of everything that students will learn in the 5 weeks. You could have these checklists saved for easy access when handling parent enquiries
Feedback - How will the teachers keep parents updated about weekly homework or progress in the class? Can parents contact the teacher? Will there be notes sent home? Can they sit in to watch the classes?
Costing - What is the full fee? What is included… a gift bag? Books? Drum sticks? Picks? How many weeks is the program, how many lessons etc? Make sure you also include what the students will GET from the program when talking about pricing with parents (see the “curriculum” heading above)
Missed lesson policy. What is your policy for missing a lesson? Don’t offer refunds, makeup lessons or credits for a set x-week program. As an alternative you might be able to offer them to join a different class for a week to catch up a lesson. Remember this is all part of training your customers how things work at your school if they are to continue.
How do they sign up? This cannot be overlooked! Make sure the timetable of the classes starting is easy to access, before handling these enquiries. Have a way of signing them up immediately on the phone or an easy to fill out form plus a follow-up procedure to make sure they fill out the form!

Video testimonials
You may like to include some testimonial quotes or short videos on your lead page.
If you don't have any good testimonials, here's a simple way to get great testimonial videos from students.
Give them the following sentence starters below, then film their answers one by one and edit it together later.

*Video Testimonial Outline for Students:*
My name is _____ and i've been learning the drums for _______
My favourite food is __________
My favourite part about drum lessons is __________
My teacher is _________
A musical goal of mine is to _______

2. Create a Facebook/IG campaign
I would do this first because it can be running in the background while you work on more time intensive marketing tasks.
I've recorded a video showing how I'd set up the campaign in the next lesson. There is also Ad copy examples and creatives you can use.

3. Share on all your social channels
Map out a series of posts you can release to build hype for the programme.

4. Email your list
Email your entire student database and leads list to promote the classes.
You can ask people to spread the word by sharing a post from your FB/IG page.
You can also boost the incentive with a reward for anyone that signs up based on their referral. E.g $20 lesson credit, a movie voucher, a shopping voucher, Candy etc

5. Community Facebook groups
Find 10-20 Facebook community groups to join. Make a schedule to post in 1-2 groups a day with a promotional post. (Don't post in all of them at once)

 *Here's an example of what to post in FB community groups: *
Attention [your town/city name] Parents :)
Looking for the perfect beginner music lessons for your child?
[Your Music School's name] 'Intro to music' programme is the perfect place to start.
In just 5 weeks your child will learn the basics on 5 of the most popular instruments, PLUS experience playing in a band.
Here's what they'll get access to:
5 x 45min lessons with a small group of new friends
Your very own intro to music workbook
Free membership to our 'All Access' video library to support you with additional learning from home
Rock band week, where you'll get to experience rocking out in a band and doing a rockstar photoshoot
Every Student also completes the course with:
An 'intro to Music Certificate' and graduation photo
A 'Goodtime Music Academy' Wristband
A video of themselves performing with the class
Photos from their Rockstar photoshoot
Enrolments are now open for our next intake
Click here to learn more: [INSERT LINK to Lead Page]
**Please note: Spaces are very limited, so get in quick*

*Images to use:*
Test a range of images with the different posts into different groups. If you don't have your own, then you can grab pictures from the photo library in module 5.
The best images are going to be pictures from the actual programme.* *

6. Local schools
This is our number one to get an insane amount of students:
We talk more about this in module 5 in the show teams course, but the best ways to promote with local schools are to:
Ask to run a blurb in the school newsletter - pay for it if you have to.
Get a guest spot in assembly to do a short demonstration and pitch
Do a school show - this is the ultimate promo. learn more in module 5 in the marketing with show teams course.
Start by making a list of every school within a 10-15min drive from your studio. This is your list to promote to. Refer to the show team course to get phone scripts, email templates, performance ideas, promotional flyer examples and LOTS more.

7. Student Tik-tok Strategy
Give your students with tik-tok a reward (e.g candy) to do a tik tok with them on their phone and account in lessons.
Record a quick video with them at their instrument telling their fans who you are and about the ITM programme and how they can sign up.
This works best if you have something else to give away. Movie tickets have always been our number 1 gift. Just add the cost of the tickets to the programme cost and give them away free.
So in your tiktok video you can say 'if you join us both you and [students name] will also get a free movie ticket.

8. Cross promotions
We had strong networks with other businesses that attracted our target market. E.g Dance schools, theatre schools, gymnastics clubs, sports clubs, libraries, after school programmes etc
Make a list of all these businesses nearby and network with the owners / managers.
Make a deal to have them promote your programme to their email list / social media and in return you'll do the same for them and promote their next big event.

9. Posters for internal use
Get posters printed to promote the programme. Put them up in your lesson rooms, bathroom, front door and waiting area etc.

10. Train the team to promote the programme
Teach your entire team how to promote the lessons to students, parents, via tik tok, on the phone to enquiries etc
Set a team goal to enrol a certain amount of students and there will be a reward if you hit the target. Check out all the staff thank you ideas in the rockstar teams module if you want to see 50+ reward ideas you could use.

# Building your ITM curriculum
Now it's time to build the curriculum for your intro programme.
*Firstly, we need to remember:*
1. You're not trying to make students proficient in 5 weeks. We just want to learn some basics, get some quick wins they can be excited about and have a really great time, so they want to continue into regular lessons after the programme.
2. The point of your intro to music curriculum is that it’s simple and easy to implement for any teacher running the program. Fleshing out the curriculum with full lesson plans each week means that teachers don't have to do any preparation and you can be confident about the quality of the programme being delivered.

*In this video we'll go through:*
Some examples of intro to music workbooks (You'll see some attached here for you too)
