# Linking Social Media Consistently
How do you link your Facebook, YouTube, Instagram and other social accounts to your website? These platforms should be "talking" to each other regularly, to increase awareness and ultimately sales. Access this lesson to learn about how to make the most out of the connection between your website and social media content.

*Contents:*
Overview
Similar content that links to your site
Similar content that doesn't link to your site
Examples - Facebook page cover photo section followed by relevant posts
* *
*1. Overview:*
Using your social media platforms to link to your website is an easy and important practice. Everything about your business online should be interlinked so people that discover you on social media can then explore your website. It also improves your website's SEO (search engine optimization), which basically refers to your website's discoverability. Your Facebook post should match your website in two aspects (which you'll see in the examples section).

*2. Similar content that links to your site:*
Your website should link to your Facebook page, Youtube channel, Instagram page, and any other platforms you use, and those platforms should link back to your website regularly.

*3. Similar content that doesn't link to your site:*
You can use the examples in this post as inspiration so that you can link to your website on a regular basis in your Facebook posts. There will also be Facebook posts in the examples below that will be similar or the same to content on your website without linking to it. This is still useful for your website because it lets you test how people engage with that content and because all your Facebook posts shouldn't link to your site.

# Individual Instrument Pages
Whilst some people make the mistake of simply saying "we offer guitar lessons" and outlining all the boring details on these instrument pages, we're going to help you turn your instrument pages into sales machines. This lesson will help you create lead pages out of your instrument pages, so you can easily get more leads from your Facebook ads and other marketing channels.
*Contents:*
Individual instrument pages and landing page elements
Examples

*1. Individual Instrument Pages and Landing Page Elements:*
Individual instrument pages are more effective if they contain elements that you would normally find on landing pages. Just as we suggested in our post on blog posts, taking the lead from landing pages can be effective on many other types of pages.
A landing page (or a lead page) is typically a standalone page that a person lands on after clicking on an ad. It doesn't usually exist in the main menu of your website. Everything on it is built to generate leads or sell a specific offer.
It has social proof section sections.
It has feature/benefit sections.
It has a hero image and a call to action.
It has elements you would typically see on a home page, except in this case they are here to support a single offer.

These are the types of sections that shouldn't only exist on your home page. They are just as important to have on your individual instrument pages. There's no reason to stop using these elements after someone has gone from your home page to one of your instrument pages. You can see these elements being used in context in the full examples below.

# Unique Features (Integrations, Widgets, Plugins and Other Web Tools)
There are a few tools that can help improve the user experience on your website and help potential customers feel connected to you and your music school. We address some great ideas in this lesson, with examples to see from other websites.
*Contents:*
Overview
Embedded Google Maps
Back to top button
Chat windows
Text/call buttons
Calendly booking forms
Embedded Google Reviews

*1. Overview:*
This section is dedicated to different ways music schools have used web tools and services that they've integrated into their website to improve it.
These are all different strategies worth testing to improve the performance of your website.

Automatic chat windows are probably the most common of these. The second most common of these are the online shops either integrated into the website with a checkout function, or in an off-site location. We won't go into those here since we are planning to give them their own post in the future. This post is more about smaller integrations that can be added quickly to your site.

*2. Embedded Google Maps Section:*
This is commonly seen in footers or contact pages. It's an easy installation, just a block of code that you copy into your site. People can zoom in or out of it to see where your school is in relation to them and like most of the unique features here it doesn't take up much room.

![image138.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image138.png)

*3. Back to Top Button:*
Worth having on every page of your site. It makes using long pages much more manageable and will mean people are less likely to get frustrated and leave your site. They are almost always a fixed element on the bottom right of the screen.

![image135.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image135.png)

*4. Chat Windows:*
This is another tool that can be installed by copying and pasting code. It's another way of encouraging communication. The Facebook chat window is connected to your Facebook page which is often a more familiar way for people to reach out. This is only really worthwhile if you have someone who can respond to messages quickly as they come in.

![image132.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image132.png)
![image151.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image151.png)

![image148.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image148.png)
![image146.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image146.png)

*5. Text/Call Buttons:*
Giving people multiple ways to contact is always a good idea. This feature doesn't need to take up much space but it's worth having.
![image144.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image144.png)

*6. Calendly Booking Forms:*
Calendly is a popular appointment booking system that integrates with Google calendar. It lets you control when appointments can be booked and prevent double booking.

![image259.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image259.png)
![image257.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image257.png)

* *
*7. Embedded Google Reviews:*
This an excellent form of social proof. Using reviews that can be publicly submitted by anyone also has the added benefit of appearing transparent, since it's obvious the actually reviews haven't been paraphrased at all.

![image254.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image254.png)

# Testimonials
Having testimonials on your website from students and parents reinforces to people that they can be confident that your studio is a great place to learn. It’s always better when you let others brag for you, rather than talking yourself up.

 Most people just put up testimonials that say generic nice things. The smarter way to do this is to use testimonials that speak to the most common questions and objections customers have.
We’ve done the work for you below and listed the most common objections and questions people have about lessons.
Your job is going to be to generate relevant testimonials that actually address these objections. Below you will also find an email template that you can send to students/parents to request testimonials that will speak to these issues.
Common objections and questions:
*Money / Value:*
Are lessons worth the cost?
Is it worth it if their child quits?
Is it worth the drive there each week?

*Will it work for me / my child:*
Will it be fun / will I enjoy it?
Will lessons progress too fast or be too hard to keep up with?
Will it be boring?
Should I wait till they’re older?

*Quality Teaching:*
Are the teachers good teachers?
Will my child develop good skills and techniques?
Will it all just be fun and games without much progression in musical training?

*Culture and support:*
Are people nice and encouraging there?
Will my child get individual attention and support?

*Email Template:*
Here’s an example email template you can copy and send to students/parents.
If you have your "Google My Business" set up then you should change the request in this email to ask them to write it directly as a google review.
Once customers write you a review on Google directly, you can go back and copy/screenshot the best ones to post onto your website.

*Hi **[NAME]*
*[insert personal comment / greeting] *
*Quick question - I’m in the process of updating our website at **[INSERT MUSIC SCHOOL NAME]** and I would love to include a few testimonials. *
*Would you be open to sharing a few words about your experience learning with **[me/us]** that I could use on the website? *
*Just a short paragraph or even a one-liner about what you love most would be amazing. *
*It would be amazing if you could include something about any concerns you had before starting with us, and how you feel now that **[CHILD'S NAME]** is enjoying the lessons.*
*To jog your memory, some common concerns include wondering if **[they/their child]** will get enough personal attention, hoping the lessons are worth the cost, and hoping they find the lessons fun!*
*You can just reply to this email with your testimonial OR here is the link to write your testimonial directly on Google** [ADD GOOGLE LINK].*
*Thank you so much! *
*[personal sign off etc]*
…………………

# Fast Ways to Get Leads
Below we have got loads of ideas on how to get leads FAST... we will continue to add to this list with more ideas as well. Most of the ideas won't cost you anything to do, or won't cost you too much to implement! Here is a list of what's coming up:
Do more of what’s already working
![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
Posting in community FaceBook groups
![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
Win back campaign ideas
![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
Emily's Instagram hack for increasing student numbers
Optimise your Google My Business account
Run a competitions on social media
Posters with QR code / Business cards in local music stores
![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
Ask students/ parents to post on social media about you and tag your business page
![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
Posting in school newsletters
Nicole's tactical yard signs Idea
Ask students to deliver fliers to houses on their street
Bring a friend week
Referral competition ideas
![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
Student stories / testimonials
Facebook / Instagram ads
Google ads
![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
Take your students busking in a popular area
![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
Cross promotions with other kids activity businesses
Run an open day
Make a compelling offer campaign / program
School concert / assembly presentation
If you have any ideas that we're missing please comment your idea in the*#** *so we can add it to this lesson!

* 1. Do more of what’s already working*
The easiest way to get leads quickly is to do more of what's already working. Ask your customers how they heard about you and double down on those marketing strategies. Make sure you’re tracking these details using the tool we’ve built you in Module 3. You can find this in module 3 under 'Your Leads' called 'Tracking Enquiries and Cancellations.'

![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
*2. Posting in Community FaceBook groups *
Community FaceBook groups can be a great way to target the right type of people in your area. Make a spreadsheet of all the local FaceBook groups in your area so you can track the last time you have posted in the group, you then want to get on some sort of schedule where you can put your latest offer or post into the group.
![image195.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image195.png)
Get a few friends / family / staff to comment / like the posts so it drives engagement and post different photos / videos and post copy to different groups.
Here’s how Nicole got over 100 students free with this strategy:
Nicole went on FaceBook and looked at all of the neighbourhood associations in my area and joined them. 
Her first posts were always questions in the group, for example:
How are parents feeling about in person lessons?
Do you prefer online or in person for music lessons right now?
Are you looking for something new in 2021?
Nicole asked questions and got answers (which gave leads) and then followed up and got a large amount of students this way. Then she posted a short course she was running which was a new adult online ukulele class. Nicole used marketing phrases such as:
Try something new this year
It's never too late to learn something new
Do something just for you!
A Poster was included with the course dates and a Registration Form.
There was an Early Bird Price Structure one time, and the other time the first 10 people receive $25 off the course.  (The Early Bird was easier to manage, as you list the Early Bird Price and then they just pay it, whereas, with the "FIRST 10", there was more back and forth to see if they were actually one of the first 10 before they sent payment).
These two things gained her over 100 students this past year and it cost nothing except time. If you get right down to it, it only takes about an hour to get it up and running on many associations.  And you get other leads from it as well, so you can build up a contact list for future.
![image502.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image502.png)
![image501.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image501.png)
![image512.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image512.png)
Here are some other examples of what you could post:
One off offers e.g. *We’d like to give away a $100 music lesson voucher to a child that would love to learn** [list instruments you teach],** message us at **[tag your business page]** if your child or a child you know would like this! *Give away as many as you’re happy to give away. For every other lead apologise that the $100 vouchers have been snatched up but would they like a $30 or $50 voucher? Then walk them through the booking process and give them your info packs about lessons etc.
Showcase videos of your tutors on your website and social media. Sometimes it can be as simple as saying:* ‘Steve has 3 spaces available for guitar lessons, message us asap if you want to snatch one before they go!*
Short form Ad example:
*Looking for the perfect beginner music lessons for your child? 🚸*
*Let your child experience the magic of music with our amazing *[programme name]
*👉 Click to learn more*
4. Medium form Ad copy example:
*⛔️ Attention** [Your town/city/area]** Parents! 👨‍👩‍👧*
*We know that all kids love music, but not all kids are ready for weekly music lessons!*
*Looking for the perfect beginner music lessons for your child? 🎹 *
*Let your child experience the magic of music with our amazing **[Your programme name]*
*[Snapshot of your programme] **e.g In just 5 weeks they'll learn the basics on 5 of the most popular instruments and experience playing them in in a band with new friends. *
*This is a great way to let your child give music a go without needing to commit to on-going lessons right away. *
5. *👉** [Your town/city]** Parents! 🎉 Check out this brand new programme for children ages **___** to **____*
6. *We all know our children should learn an instrument...BUT*
*It can be hard to find lessons they'll enjoy... at the right price! *
*Until Now... INTRODUCING...*
6. *Why your child is luckier than you! Because, you never got an opportunity like this... Learn to** [e.g play the guitar] **in **[x days/weeks]** with our brand new** [programme name]*
*7. Got a child that loves to sing, or who drums on everything? Then here's what you need to know... 👀*
8. Ad Headline Ideas:

![image514.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image514.png)
*3. Win back campaign ideas *
Remember that your ex students maybe be interested in coming back, therefore you want to have a regular offer that you send to them every 3-6 months to keep you top of mind! We have some examples you could use below.
 Here’s an example that got Marcus 72 students from one email:
![image510.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image510.png)
*SUBJECT: You’re receiving this because you gave Nook Nook Music School your email at some time in your life *
*Hello **[name]**,*
![image510.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image510.png)
*I just want to let you know that our school is filling up! We will stop accepting new students and have a waiting list until we’re able to move to a bigger space. Something like a farm. Seriously! Imagine a huge space dedicated to creative activities for children. A music school, art school, dance, a cafe, a performance space . . . sorry, back to the topic *
*There are some spots open for bands - for all ages. Plus private lessons for various instruments. Contact us by replying to this email to learn more.*
*OFFER: Free trial lesson. No registration fee. Get in before prices go up!*
*P.S. Offer expires October 31st 2021.*
*Kind regards,*
*[Your Name]*

Here’s a shorter email example:
*Subject line: **[First Name]*
*Hi **[First Name]**, Are you still interested in **[instrument]** lessons? I have a few spaces available and would love to offer one to you first before advertising it publicly. *
*:)*
*[Your Name]*

Another cheeky email Idea you can send is the following email, then just give them the offer they say is their favourite. You now also have the data on what’s a better compelling offer to run ads with and scale fast.
*Subject line: Quick Question **[First Name]*
*Body text: *
*Hi **[First Name]**, Hope you’re well. Just a quick quick question… *

*4. Emily's Instagram Hack for Increasing Student Numbers *
Head to Instagram and search for schools that are local to you, follow everyone who follows their page and they will see that they have a new follower and are more than likely to click on your page.
Emily gains about 5 students every time she does this.

*5. Optimise your Google My Business account*
Make sure you've got your google my business account optimised. In this video Jonny shows you all the key elements of google my business and what elements you need to collect to optimise it.
Optimising your Google My Business (GMB) listing is crucial for improving your local search visibility and ranking higher on Google. Here's a list of instructions to help you maximise your GMB listing:
Claim or Verify Your Listing: If you haven't already, claim your business on Google My Business. If it's already listed, verify your ownership through a postcard or other verification methods.
Complete Your Profile: Fill out every field in your GMB profile, including business name, address, phone number (NAP), website URL, hours of operation, and category.
Choose the Right Category: Select the most relevant primary and secondary categories that accurately describe your business. Be specific; this helps Google understand your business better.
Add High-Quality Photos: Include clear, high-resolution photos of your business exterior, interior, products, services, and team. These images build trust and help potential customers understand your offerings.
Write a Compelling Business Description: Craft a concise and engaging business description that highlights your unique selling points and important keywords. Be informative and customer-focused.
Upload Posts Regularly: Use the GMB posts feature to share updates, promotions, events, and news about your business. Posts can enhance engagement and provide valuable information to users.
Collect and Respond to Reviews: Encourage customers to leave reviews on your GMB listing. Respond promptly and professionally to both positive and negative reviews to show your engagement and commitment to customer satisfaction.
Add Attributes and Amenities: Include relevant attributes such as wheelchair accessibility, outdoor seating, Wi-Fi availability, and more. These details help customers find exactly what they're looking for.
Set Up Messaging: Enable messaging to allow customers to contact you directly through the GMB listing. Respond promptly to inquiries to improve customer experience.
Publish Q&A: Address frequently asked questions in the Q&A section of your listing. This can help potential customers find essential information quickly.
Use Google My Business Insights: Regularly review the insights section to gain insights into how customers are interacting with your listing. This information can guide your optimisation efforts.
Geo-Tagged Images: Ensure that your images have geo-tags, which help reinforce your business's location and improve local search relevance.
Local Citations and Consistency: Ensure your business's name, address, and phone number are consistent across all online platforms, directories, and social media. This boosts your credibility.
Website Optimisation: Link your GMB listing to your website and ensure that your website is mobile-friendly, fast, and optimised for local search keywords.
Use Posts for Events and Offers: Utilise GMB posts to share time-sensitive information such as upcoming events, special offers, discounts, and sales.
Respond to Messages Promptly: If you enable messaging, respond quickly to customer inquiries. This responsiveness is valued by potential customers and can positively impact your ranking.
Optimise for Voice Search: Use natural language in your description and posts to align with how people use voice search on devices like Google Assistant.
Regular Updates: Keep your GMB listing up-to-date with any changes to your business hours, services, or contact information.
Remember that optimising your Google My Business listing is an ongoing process. Regularly monitor and adjust your strategy based on changes in your business and customer behaviour, and keep up with any updates or features Google adds to the platform.

Get lots of google reviews!
You'll want to have the customers that give you loads of great feedback to leave some awesome reviews. You'll also want to get team members, staff, friends and family to also leave an awesome review - Aim for 5 star reviews. This is going to help your google search ratings to go up. You'll want to make sure to not bulk email asking people for reviews & don't ask for reviews on social media as there you have people that don't even know you leaving reviews.
Loria has made a video talking about "How to Get 5 Star Google Review" and how having Google Reviews and posting on her Google Profile have helped her studio show up on the MAP when people are doing a Google search for piano or music lessons. Loria gets new leads every week that say they found her studio from Google Maps.

*8. Ask students/ parents to post on social media about you and tag your business page*
Sometimes it’s as simple as asking parents to help you... Always ask individually. The best option is just to ask everyone in person at their lessons. The second best is a personalised email - NOT a bulk email where they can tell it’s been sent to a group.
You can use simple mail merge tools to do a short email in bulk. We use ‘Yet another mail merge’ which is a cheap gmail plug in tool/add on. Write something simple like…
*Hi** [NAME],*
*You know how awesome people like you, know other awesome people…*
*I was wondering if you would be comfortable sharing a little social media post about **[SCHOOL NAME]** and tagging us? *
*We have a few spaces available, but only want them filled with awesome people like you. *
*[YOUR NAME]*

![image560.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image560.png)
*9. Posting in school newsletters *
Connecting with your local schools is a great way to get an offer out there. Go and visit all the schools near by and go in to talk to them about this. Have the blurb drafted on your phone to send right there and then and/ or some flyers you can immediately hand out to them.
Example of a blurb for a school newsletter:
*Interested in learning an instrument? Student's at **[school name]** are being offered a free $50 dollar voucher/ First month at half price at **[music schools name]**. Go to **[specific landing page for the offer link]**. Contact us and mention your school to get this deal.*
Note: You want to make sure you track how effective this is by using a voucher code, if it’s online, or ensuring that they need to say the school they’re from to get the offer. You will then know if it’s worth repeating in future.

*10. Nicole's Tactical Yard signs Idea*
Yard signs might be old school but they absolutely still work! #BAMSQUAD member Nicole Castro uses them to get students to her school on a regular basis. The time that she shared her idea with us, she was able to sign up 11 students from the signs.
She puts yard signs all around the local school pickup lines, for parents to see as they go to collect their children.
![image557.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image557.jpg)
Nicole mentioned that she doesn't request the school's permission to put the signs up. Instead, she adopts the "Ask for forgiveness, not permission" mentality as only one school has ever asked her to take the sign down. The key here, is to place the signs right by the road and in as unobtrusive places as possible!
She orders her signage in bulk when the sign company is having 50-60% off sales. She'll then order around 200-300 signs to last for quite some time. Her cost per sign is only a few dollars and she knows that they do bring in students, so it doesn't concern her too much if the signs don't last more than about a week in the school pickup line.
Nicole also elaborated as to *when* they put the signs up. Usually, this is done on a Saturday/Sunday or directly after a Spring break when school is about to come back into session.
Here is the sign that she uses, and the map showing which schools they target in relation to their music school (marked as the pink dot).
![image117.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image117.jpg)
![image121.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image121.jpg)

*12. Bring a friend week*
* *This is a relatively easy way to get new potential students through your door and set you up with a lot of leads! Advertise to have your current students bring a friend to lessons week. Everyone that does it goes in the draw to win a range of cool prizes. (Ask the students before hand what their dream prizes are e.g. skateboard, iPad, 10 movie tickets etc.)
When they bring a friend, let them both know that if they sign up for lessons you both get a movie ticket. Give them a registration form/flyer with instructions on how to register and give a time limit on them registering.  Make sure you follow all leads through until you get an answer and add them to your mailing list!

*13. Referral competition ideas: *
Pretty much all we want to do is to find a friend of a student, that wants to sign up for lessons. They fill their details & you fill out your part, then they get to choose a free prize. This could be something like:
Movie ticket
Food voucher
Swimming pool voucher
Box of chocolates
The reason this campaign works so well is we have talked about how music school margins are pretty small and giving away discounts on our lessons can be a bit lame for a kid, even free lessons are too. It's the kid that's getting the prize here, they want something fun. Parents love this as well as their child is getting something out of it.

*14. Student Stories / Testimonials*
Powerful stories will be one of the number one ways you are going to leverage marketing in your school.
Here are 3 huge reasons why we want to leverage stories:
Stories tap into people's emotions. They draw people into a place where they have good emotions about a story, where they will more likely purchase most of the time. Stories are far more engaging than a list of information or some facts about your school
Stories don't come across as 'salesy' but they sell
People are always asking, will this work for me or my child? When you showcase stories of ordinary people experiencing lessons at your school, developing & having a great experience that is more likely to resonate with what the potential student/parent is questioning
How do we get these stories:
We can ask for written testimonies from people and use those on Facebook, Google and our website
Video testimonies. We are in an age that people are soaking things up via video and people as they scroll they are more captivated with videos than they are text. If you have a good camera or camera person then great you can make professional videos. If not, you can totally do it on your cell phone
Ways we can leverage our stories:
Newsletters
Social media posts
On your website
If you actually have a really big story, say a student wins an award or a student became head boy at high school and they gave you credit for helping them to grow, you can leverage that story in the newspaper. Community newspapers are looking for stories like this all the time
Most of you are going to be amazing at getting students and getting results. Being in a course like this shows you are invested. What we need to work on is getting the stories and leveraging them to close that circle of cash. If you can do that your school will astronomically grow. You want to make those videos cool enough that the student would want to share it themselves and be sure to not make it about you but the student. Of course at the end you will have your information about how people can get involved in your school. Also make sure to make the videos 1-3 mins max. More people are going to watch the whole thing and see your little advert at the end if it is shorter.

Get your team helping with social media by using our 'Social Media Bingo' concept.
You can have a bingo board, which is given to your team members with types of photo or clips of the types of posts you're after (ideas below.) The boards should be up on a wall somewhere so staff can see how well others are doing and adding some social pressure.
You need to incentive your team as well with some prizes up for grabs. Having a personal and a team prize can work really well. Have a personal prize of cash or cinema tickets BUT if all the team members get it then the prize doubles for everyone!
Think about if you will allow people to do more than one board in a month, but then you want to put the people who complete them to put them in a draw to win a prize. This could be the cash that you have essentially saved from not needed to hire someone to do your social media plus getting more diverse posts!

*17. Take your students busking in a popular area*
Take your students out into the community and get them busking! You want a way to collect leads from people seeing you perform, for example you could do a a 'guess the candy in the jar competition.' When people submit a guess make sure you get their name, email phone number and tick interested in lessons or not. You can then chase all these people up for lessons.
At the busking event, you can also have signage, wear your branded t-shirts and give out flyers to everyone listening to the show.

*18. Cross promotions with other kids activity businesses*
Build connections with other studios and set up a cross promotion system where you promote each other's businesses. You email each other lists on the same week and give them all a special deal (make sure it is clear and has a time limit on for them to access). If this goes well you would want to repeat this 2 or 3 times a year on a regular basis. Some other studio ideas are:
Dance
Swimming
Karate
Gymnastics
Any other similar groups

Open days are a great way to get new students. Open up your school and put on lots of fun activities for the community to engage with such as:
Food
Karaoke
Scavenger Hunt
Photo booth
Instruments out for kids to try.

Marlayna hosted an instrument petting zoo at her school. The event was charged at $55 per student and here's how it worked: The concept is SUUUUUPER simple.
Do an all call for instructors to host the sessions, being careful to only have what’s available on the schedule. Advertise. Kids come for two hours and rotate in groups of the available instrument families.
We had a dance party while the staff decided who will rehearse and perform on what instrument. We picked out a very easy song (itsy bitsy spider this time) and chose one or two notes to teach the kids. Rehearsed for about 15 minutes, performed for parents.
Then the kids had pizza while the parents took advantage of our one-time deep discount to get started! I’m more than happy to go deeper with the details. Just let me know. We made about $600 minus $70 for food.
Check out Marlayna's original post below as well as some pictures of the event in action!

Girls Empowered had a table and had people write all kinds of positive messages and display them in creative photos. They will join us for theater and parties. And others.*

Check the video to see the various business and what they did: ex. Exotic animals on display! Baked goods from a local bakery who hires kids with disabilities. So much good stuff!*

One handed me a US flag with certificate that was flown over the US Capital in honor of Nook. All gave speeches. One offered the school a generous grant. They all featured us on their socials.*
*Local celebrities and influencers came and raved about us. Some I didn’t know about, but the younger teachers hipped me to them.*

*Attendance appeared to be split right down the middle of current student families and interested parties. Post fair has brought in an avalanche of new sign ups. We did prepare properly for it!*
*A lot of planning and work went into it, but it cost a lot less than might be expected. An investment well worth the cost and effort. The new sign-ups alone. The exposure. Continued photos, videos, and word of mouth from all those attended. We have so many amazing photos and videos from the photographers that we can use for many things. For retention from current students. Meeting and being introduced to a good amount of people worth knowing. Great for staff morale. I could go on!*

*20. Make a compelling offer campaign / program *
All the details for this are in our compelling offer course is in Module 2 under 'C = Compelling Offer.'
A compelling offer is a great offer that stands out above your competitors and is something that draws people in to trial your services and sets them up for continuing into your main lesson programmes. Here are some things that make a compelling offer great:
Great content and experiences at a great price (e.g. 30-days for $30, First month Half price etc)
Bonuses (e.g workbook, guitar pick, drumsticks, gift bag etc...)
Scarcity (e.g. Only 7 spots left)
Urgency (e.g. Register today and save 20%)
Risk reversal (First lesson absolutely free)

* 21. School Concert / Assembly Presentation*
Running a show team is such an effective way to get leads, however it is a bigger project to implement. By having a show you:
Will have access to the largest number of people in your target market in one place, at one time. You can’t buy that sort of audience.
Don’t have to go through parents to get kids excited about wanting to sign up and learn music.
Can start building a relationship with the schools, so can go back year after year.
Want to have an ongoing relationship with the schools, so you can not only do just a show, but you can also offer more services to the schools later on.
You could offer shows for:
Primary schools
Early childcare centres eg. kindergartens, pre-schools
School fairs
Youth groups
 If you happen to already have some strong connections with any local schools that can make it easier and therefore more achievable. We have a whole section on show teams in module 5 under 'I = Inspire' called 'Marketing With Show Teams in Local Schools.'

### M = Money Management………….
# Billing
There are SO MANY different options when it comes to organising your billing. Instead of going through all the different options we are going to share with you the one that we think is the best, and recommend that you do... Monthly Subscription via direct debit.
As well as talking you through the monthly subscription offer we will be giving you some other thoughts when it comes to billing.

*Monthly Subscription*
A monthly subscription is when students pay a fixed monthly fee for a set number of lessons over the year. E.G. they pay $199 a month to have 48 lessons over the year in their organised time and day slot. The payment should be made by direct debit so it's automatically set up and you don't need to do anything.
The pros to this method are:
Predictable income helping you to budget and plan with your finances
Encourages consistent attendance
Easy to manage for both students and the school, less admin hours needed to manage this payment method
Opportunity to add in a couple of extra lessons over the year and not offer catch up lessons if any are missed as they're planned into the schedule
Use a Student management system to automate all of this (see module 3 for more information on SMS!)
When you're setting your prices for this, consider making different pricing for how often people pay. E.G. monthly, quarterly, yearly. This will help to make up for your processing fees. The idea here is the less often people pay, the less they pay.

One thing that often confuses students and families with this method is when you have school closures or public holidays. It's there for really important to communicate the fact that they are paying an equal amount for a set number of lessons over the year.
Marlyna's school have this great video they send out to help remind their students when holidays are coming up and the question of not getting all their lessons:
Bay Ridge Music school lay out a calendar on their website and a talley of lessons for each day making the number of lessons really clear throughout the year:
![image762.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image762.png)

As with anything there will be people who don't like this method, people don't like change! We suggest you stay strong on this billing method and explain that it cuts down your admin costs to bill this way. If you have people still refusing it you have two options: Let them go, you can easily replace them with someone who will pay this way OR charge them a massive admin fee to process their fees a different way, this should put them off!

*Why You Shouldn't Use Another Billing Method *
Here we have a list of other billing options and explain why we don't think recommend them:
Pay-as-You-Go (students pay for each lesson individually before or after the session) - this leaves you with inconsistent income making it harder for you to plan and budget, admin hours will be taken up by chasing missed payments, people can easily cancel and leave you out of pocket.
Term-based Billing (lessons are billed per academic term or semester, with tuition due at the start of each term) - Limited flexibility for students who join mid-term, potential dropouts mid-term, lots of admin hours needed at the start and end of each term, have to re sell the lessons each term
Tiered Pricing (different levels of lessons (e.g., beginner, intermediate, advanced) are priced differently based on complexity and duration) - Complexity in pricing structure, potential confusion for students and higher chance of things to go wrong when setting up the payment schedule
Block Booking/ Package deals (Students purchase a set number of lessons upfront at a discounted rate) - inconsistent income, have to keep re selling the package deals rather than them rolling on, more admin time needed.
Please note - Package deals/ Block booking can seem like a good idea for Christmas/ Birthday vouchers, however rather than sell them a set amount of lessons just use that as money of the monthly subscription instead!

*Automatic Billing*
Upgrade to automatic billing so you have more time to focus on teaching and not spending hours tracking and processing payments. If you need to upgrade to automatic billing here is an example email you could send out, you will need to change the instructions to match the system you use:
![image761.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image761.png)

*VAT/ GST…*
Most countries have a VAT or GST type of tax on your earnings over a certain threshold. E.g. in the UK is 20%, Australia is 10% and New Zealand is 15%. While it’s exciting to get to the threshold as your earnings are going up, it can be hard to workout how to cover these extra costs.
It is important that once you get to this point you raise your rates so the customer is covering the costs, otherwise you’ll just be subsidising their lessons for them. It is a super easy increase in some respects as it’s government imposed and 100% of the increase if going to the government, so you can blame it on them!

# How To Price Your Lessons Like a Pro
*What we’re going to cover:*
Common Pricing mindset issues
How to price your lessons like a pro
I’m also going to share a major thing I wish i knew when building my school.
Down below you will also see FAQ’s about pricing

Common Pricing Mindset issues
*Be aware of your competitors' prices, but don’t base your price on it. *Music Teachers generally suck at business and so just price themselves relative to what others around them charge and at a number they can comfortably look people in the eye and say.
*Wrong pricing leads to you being underpaid and just subsidising everyone's lessons.* This leads to you feeling like you’re being ripped off and you’re teaching from a place of unnecessary stress. When your finances are under control, it’s this huge weight off your shoulders and you’re given the headspace and energy to be a better and more creative teacher and business owner.
*Remember why you went into business,* probably for reasons like this:
Freedom,
To be your own boss,
To make an impact
To make money,
To provide jobs,
To create awesome experiences for students
That will only truly come from profit.
It can’t come from you undercharging then just working 100hrs p/w to make up the gap.
*You're not going to build something epic by charging peanuts:* You want to build something amazing, not just for you, but for your students and your community. That is worth it! The more you charge the better off your school is going to be and the better of your students are going to be. Check out this image below to show what increases and decreases dependant on your price, and the same from the customer point of view. E.G. the higher your prices the higher perceived value of your lessons from your customers and the higher service level you will have
*With higher prices your students get a better experience.* When you look at my school you’ll see some pretty cool things right. Everything from:
Big flash concerts
Band programmes
Media team
Video Recording rooms
Intern programmes
Foundation programme providing free lessons and mentoring to underprivileged kids.
Music buses
Big waiting room with lots of waiting room activities
And the list goes on…
What you need to know is that, all of that comes from having profit to reinvest into growing the business and making it more exciting for the students.
*Almost no one cares about your masters or PHD in music. *
It’s an amazing achievement that i’m sure will open doors for you, but most beginner / intermediate students will not  pay more for lessons with those with higher credentials. Your credentials alone will never trump the full package experience an amazing music school with a value stack can offer.
Some of my best teachers were intern / Junior level teachers, not professionals.
*Not all problems are solved by your price.* Sometimes:
You’re spending too much,
Not growing enough customers fast enough,
You have retention problems,
Wrong staff problems
Or something else...
So, Always ask yourself ‘what is the root problem i’m trying to solve’.
*Keep your pricing simple and easy to understand.* Some of you have horrendous plans where the price changes whether it’s a group lesson with 2, 3, 4, 5, or 6 people in lessons. That’s not what people want.
*Be patient.* You will always have to balance the tension of what you want to achieve and provide with how much it costs to provide that. but it takes time. Be patient!

*How to price your lessons like a pro...*
What I got wrong about pricing at my school and how I’d do it different today. We didn’t use value stack. We had all the elements, but we never presented the offer this way. Here’s what that would have looked like if i’d used this value stack method:
36 x 45-min Group Lessons ……………………….  Value $1,500
Free curriculum workbooks and PDF’s …………..  Value = $200
All Access Video Library …………………………...  Value = $400
All access app……………………………………….  Value = $100
Unlimited Q&A support……………………………… Value = $200
$0 Registration Fee………………………………….  Value = $100
4 x Epic Performance opportunities p/a…………… Value = $200
1 x Rock Band experience programme p/a ………. Value = $200
Rockstar photoshoot…………………………….…… Value = $50
Regular competitions and prizes……………………  Value = $150
Priority placement on holiday programmes……….  *Bonus
Awesome waiting room experience……………….   Priceless
Making loads of new friends………………………..  Priceless
Access to GTMA referral refund plan…..…………   Priceless

## TOTAL VALUE = $3,100
## YOUR PRICE = ONLY $40 per week (*billed monthly)

This way the price is anchored by the true value and not by the lesson amount and people feeling self entitled to getting free access to all the other benefits you create for students.
Show the smallest number as a weekly amount to make the contrast feel as big as possible.
You can have options for prices. E.g in this example it could be:
$30 when billed annually,
$35 when billed quarterly
$40 monthly
Or $50 weekly
Here is another example of Spark school of Music and Dance's Intro to music programme:
![image803.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image803.png)
*The more additional benefits you have, the more you can charge:*
So every time you add a new feature or experience in your programme you add it to your list.
For example, let’s say you create an annual youtube week where students set up their own channels, building videos, thumbnails etc… Maybe you hire a videographer for that week.
You then add that experience into your stack as something with a fixed value. E.g. $75 extra per person to cover lesson planning, videographers film / edit time, workbook, competition prizes etc.

 *The more professional you look, the more you can charge:*
Everything you deliver leading up to lessons is projecting an image. Every layer of professionalism allows you to be perceived as more professional and therefore, worth more value. Price itself is a communication of value. If you price yourself higher you send a signal that your quality and experience is higher.
I mean everything… How tidy your studio and waiting room is, how quickly you respond to customer emails etc…

 *What are your costs?*
The most important thing to consider when choosing your prices is the fact you need to cover all your costs, plus a little extra for rainy days! Carefully workout your fixed and variable costs to consider when choosing your pricing. If you add more students, will your costs increase?
You can work backwards by taking your projected costs and adding on a margin of 20% then dividing by the number of students and number of lessons they’ll take in a year. This will give you an estimate of what you need to charge to make the amount you would like.
The challenge with this is you can’t always guarantee how many students will join you in a year, so this an exercise to do, but it’s not the only factor to consider.

Does your annual revenue attract any taxes?
Many countries have extra business taxes depending on their revenue amount. You need to be aware of tax increases; know when they are coming and how much they are… Be ready!

*Be aware of what competitors are charging and how their offer compares to yours*
Don’t try to position your price lower,
Position your value higher… way higher!

*Know your area and your customer demographic:*
In some areas, Not everyone is going to be able to afford to pay what you think you’re worth. Other areas you’re undercharging.
This just takes some more research and testing.
You can always add additional offers to be able to up-sell and downsell people.
The best way to test your pricing is by taking calls on the phone and seeing how people respond.

*Increase your prices regularly*
Train your customers to get used to regular prices increases so they are not surprised by them, but they expect them. [Find out more in our ‘Price Increase lesson’].
Two things you need to know now though.
 Pick a date and put the price up on that date every year, people will get used to it and expect it.
 Put it at the bottom of invoices / receipts. So  you can remind people that this is communicated all year round. Use a simple example below like this
****[Music school name]’s prices are adjusted on the [insert date] every year to ensure we do not fall behind inflation or any increased costs of delivering our unique lesson programmes. *
Down below you will see some frequently asked questions about pricing.

FAQ’s Around Pricing
*Should different teachers have different prices?*
You’re selling your school and not the teacher so therefore you should be confident in what your school offers and everyone should be on the same price. However there are some ways this could be used to an advantage:
Some schools use advanced students to help teach brand new beginners on their instrument, this would make those lessons cheaper. It’s a good way for the advanced students to progress their skills, while giving the parents a cheaper way for students to ‘try out’ an instrument
You don’t want to set things up in a way that prevents you being able to step back.
If you have someone that’s got more credentials and you’re both dead set on creating a higher pricing tier for their lessons you are better to create a new service with a new stack for their lessons. It shouldn’t just be based on their credentials alone.

*Should I grandfather my pricing with my students (let old students keep the old price as new students get the increase)?*
You probably want to do it because you’re nervous about the price going up and feel you owe it to long time customers to give them a deal. That’s not the case.
I would do it on a case by case based on individual conversations. E.g Get to the bottom of their concern (is it that they can’t afford it? Or do they not think you’re worth it?)
Always give a time limit on a discount. E.g don’t say you’re going to keep them at the old price, say you’re going to give them a discount for the next 3-6 months. After that it’ll revert to the normal price.
*How should I think about Group Vs Individual Prices? *
Most people think group lessons should be cheaper than individual lessons, however if you charge the same as your individual price, but have a longer lesson it makes the price per minute for the student less and they have more lesson time. It’s essential to show the value of group lessons, so don’t lower the rate.
You can use this pricing tool to work out your price per minute for students based on individual lessons and group lessons. It can also help you work out your current revenue and projected revenue if you swapped to group lessons.
Access the Pricing Tool here:
*Remember to click ‘File’, then ‘Make a copy’
*You need to be logged into a gmail account to access the document.
Hear from some of our #BAMSQUAD members on how they price their group and individual lessons.
Some parents will complain they don’t want group lessons as their child will get less of the attention… Give them this analogy from Jonny: ‘Music lessons aren’t like a loaf of bread, where if you have 5 students in the class it gets split into 5, so your child only get 20% of the teachers attention. Group lessons are like a movie ticket where everyone is going to get the same experience and therefore everyone pays the same amount.’
Don’t forget our tips from above about selling your whole package!

*How should we approach family discounts?*
The idea of family discounts is nice, and I'm sure appreciated by the families, however you are still putting in the same amount of time and effort to each lesson so should you really be charging any less? At the end of the day the choice is going to be down to you however we do have a couple of options to consider:
You could charge a family by time rather than number of lessons… E.G. a family pays for one hour and then 3 children get 20 minutes each, or they rotate and take it in turns. Maybe some weeks one child just needs a 10 minute ‘check in’ and the other two have a longer lesson. Or there might be some activities the 3 kids could all do together such as theory or aural skills. This makes it cheaper than having to pay for three 30 minute slots however you’re still getting your normal hourly rate.
If the lessons are back to back and you have a different price for hour and half hour slots, you may decide to just charge the family the hourly price, rather than two half an hour slots.
You could offer the family a discount in the last month of the year, this gives them incentive to stay in lessons and proves their commitment to lessons.
If you have an admin or sign up fee you could waive this for a family signing up, they get a discount but you still get your regular rate for teaching.
You might offer a family discount to parents to fill a day time or off-peak slot that you usually struggle to fill (e.g. 9am - 2pm)… A great way to get parents involved in music too!
At the end of the day you’re still working super hard for all your lessons, so don’t feel like you HAVE to offer a family discount.

*How do you make sure things in your value stack are things people actually want and are willing to pay for?*
![image809.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image809.png)
*

 Should I have annual registration fees and how much should they be?*

*Should lesson costs vary depending on the day of the week they are offered?*
![image807.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image807.png)

*Should lessons cost more when they are taken by the business owner?*
![image802.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image802.png)

*Do you post your rates on your website? Why or why not? *
There is some debate as to whether you should publicly post your rates on your website. There are pros and cons each way. These are some thoughts offered by our members.
![image801.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image801.jpg)

# How to Raise Your Prices The Smart Way
Increasing your prices can feel scary but in this training we're going to provide you with loads of resources to make it easy and help you feel confident in your right to increase prices.

This is for you if:
You know you need to raise your prices
You’re nervous about the reaction you’ll get if you increase your prices
You’re scared all your students will quit if you raise your prices
You don’t think you’re worthy of raising your prices
You feel guilty about raising your prices
You’re worried people won’t be able to afford your increased price
You’ve kept your prices the same for so long you don’t think you can increase them anymore
You’re worried about what you would increase to and how that would compare to your competitors prices
You've realised you're not charging enough and now need to increase your prices but not sure how

What we will be covering:
How to increase your price without losing students
The Price Impact Projector Tool
How to Implement a Price Increase
FAQ's about price increases
Real stories from the #BAMSQUAD about what happened when they raised their prices
 Some Final Thoughts

 **Reminder - Review the pricing mindset issues in the video where we covered pricing your lessons like a pro. That list addresses the common issues studio owners have about pricing and how they should think about those issues instead.

*Jonny Music Bus Lesson Story:*
Music bus lessons started at $10 per lesson, I was so proud it was affordable. As I record this it has more than doubled!
Every other expense has more than doubled too.
The desire to provide $10 lessons is no longer possible financially unless someone else is making up the extra $10. That's either going to be funding if you're lucky, or most likely out of your pocket.

The Price Impact Projector Tool
I have a tool here that's going to put your mind at ease about price increases.
Hypothetical Situation:
Let’s say you have 10 students paying you $20 per hour. You increase your prices from $20 to $25. Two students now quit. So you’re losing $40 from the two quitters
On the other hand, the extra $40 you make from the other 8 people covers your loss. You’re making the same amount of money for 25% less work.
And then you go and sign up two new students for those empty time slots and you’re now making $50 more per shift for the same amount of teaching time.

Your price should really be figured out based on what you need to hit your income goals. It's best to reverse engineer what your price should be. We are going to show you have to do this using a price impact projector:

 Make a copy of the spreadsheet in order to put your own numbers in
Here is a video to show you how it works:

How To Increase Your Price Without Losing Students:

*Step 1. Do the ground work*
- Train your customers to see price increases as a normal structured part of your lessons. You can have a clause in your enrolment forms explaining the time of year you will have an increase.
Another way to over-communicate this is to load the following text at the bottom of your invoices:
*PLEASE NOTE: *
***Need to cancel? Just a reminder we require 2 weeks notice in writing to **[your email here]** email*
***Lesson Pricing - To keep things easy and consistent, our prices are updated every year on the **[choose perfect date for you]**. This is based on the national CPI and increased costs required to deliver our great lesson programmes. *
****For a full list of our **[terms & conditions / policies]**, please view them on our website here [link]*

*Step 2. Give people advanced warning of a price increase *
Send casual reminders in other communications 2-3months out from your price increase. You don't have to say much, just prime people for the increase.
E.g - Just a heads up, our prices will be updated on the 1st January. We will let you know the updated price 3-4 weeks prior it taking effect. Thanks again for your continued support in our mission to make quality lessons accessible for as many people as possible.

*Step 3. Calendar Date the Process*
List all the things that need to be done and load them into your calendar and to-do list. This will include things like:
Send email #1 to customers about coming increase
Decide if new students will get the old price until the change takes effect, or they will pay the new price from today.
Change prices on website, info pack and any other public facing material
Change prices in billing software etc
Let team know the prices have changed / are changing
Decide if there are any customers that will receive a discount when the price increase takes effect.
Choose the new price
Draft price increase letter and include details of all the new improvements you've made since the last increase.
Send official price increase letter
Measure the impact this makes on your business and how many students leave over it. Share your results with the #BAMSQUAD so others are encouraged to also raise their prices.

*Step 4. Write your official price increase letter to customers*
Below we have several examples of fee increase letters from various #BAMSQUAD members.
If you are doing a significant increase, or it's been a while since you've done an increase, then you would probably be best to use a longer format style like Dereks below. (also - be sure to watch the video clip down below about the response to this letter.)
![image791.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image791.png)
![image790.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image790.png)
![image788.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image788.png)
* Here is Kelly's letter that she sends out:*
To our valued KSOM Community,
From our commencement in 2011, Kennedy school of Music has endeavoured to offer fun and engaging music lessons, programmes and opportunities for our community; giving back as often as we are able, in the form of scholarships, lesson vouchers and community donations.
As a small local business, KSOM have many expense categories, all of which have increased greatly over the past 12 months. I know we are not alone in this, everyone is feeling the squeeze, and as such we have pushed out the decision to adjust tuition pricing for as long as possible.
Please accept this letter as notification of a tuition adjustment, effective Term 2 2023.
We use the word ‘adjustment’ with intention, as this is an adjustment relative to the loss of purchasing power of the dollar (CPI). In order to maintain the high quality of our music education programmes and to continue providing our students with the best possible learning experience, we have implemented this adjustment, and kept this as manageable as we can, adjusting lesson fees by $ (INSERT INCREASE HERE) each as outlined below:
KSOM STUDIO LESSON PRICING
Tiny Tones, Prenotes and Mini Tones: $_ per lesson
Group Lessons: $_ per lesson
Nightingales: $_ per lesson
Private Tuition: $_ per lesson
Full Hour Private Tuition: $_
KSOM IN-SCHOOL LESSON PRICING (Halfmoon Bay and Gore High are a separate agreement).
From Term 2 forward, in-school pricing is calculated as full term rate as opposed to the previous itemised rate. This provides greater flexibility to meet your needs in the event of unscheduled school timetable clashes such as strikes, sports days, swimming blocks etc and any catch-ups that may result as appropriate. Your term rate now includes the lesson fee, any applicable travel rates (these remain unchanged from Term 1) and is inclusive of GST, viewable as one total.
We are committed to ensuring that our students continue to receive exceptional instruction and opportunities from our dedicated team here at KSOM. Our qualified team are constantly accessing professional development and work hard to provide rounded opportunities for our amazing students, including performance opportunities, intern experience, and more. Providing our students with skills and opportunities that benefit them as musicians, in addition to regular lessons is a priority for us.
As a parent myself I know the importance of making every dollar count when it comes to running a household. Many families make use of our instalment plans and you are most welcome to put this in place should this be a better fit for your family. Make contact with Cleo (accounts) by simply replying to this email, and she can easily arrange this for you.
Should you ever find that the cost of participation is not manageable for your family, we encourage you to make contact regarding our scholarship programme. We want music education to be accessible for all children, and our scholarship programme has been making that happen since 2019.
The gift of music lasts a lifetime. Thank you for your continued support of us as a small business, and for choosing Kennedy School of Music as your family’s music school - we truly value you as an integral part of our close-knit KSOM Community.
Kindest Regards,
Kelly Kennedy
Principal
Kennedy School of Music
Click  to find out where your tuition money goes.
![image799.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image799.png)
Price Increase Letter Layout
First paragraph is positive talking about what work you have been doing and opportunities you have provided. This reminds parents and customers of your value and what they have been paying for!
Get straight to the point of your letter, people have short attention spans! Use facts to explain what is happening in the industry and what you are doing to combat it. Show what value you will be adding to your school, business and what you offer.
Use figures and examples to make your point about pricing, make it sound like you are doing your customers a favour. Show them how ‘bad it could be’ and how you’re making it better.
Outline EVERYTHING you offer, most of which parents might not know/ realise/ have thought of. Use prices if possible to show what a good deal your customers are getting.
Make sure your customers know this is happening and when. Ensure that you don’t need to rely on them to do anything if you have automated payments.
Finish off reminding customers briefly that you will be adding more value to your service and you are grateful for them.
Once your annual price change is ingrained in your school culture and customers expect it, you can use a shorter, more to the point style letter.
Remember we are not the only industry who have to increase fee's.
See these other industry examples of a a fee increase email. They use a lot of the same techniques as above, straight to the point with little emotion and fluff.
![image797.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image797.png)
![image795.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image795.png)
![image793.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image793.jpg)

![image784.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image784.png)
![image710.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image710.png)
![image709.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image709.png)

FAQ's about raising prices
*What if my competitors are charging more or less than me?*
You should be aware of what your competitors charge, but your prices should not be driven by their prices.
You should build a value stack for your school to set you apart from your competitors. This is where you list all the things students receive as part of their lessons and you assign a value to it.
Show the total value to give what we call a ‘price anchor’. You write ‘Your Price: ONLY $XXX.’
This makes you incomparable to most competitors, while justifying a much higher price. Look for simple ways to add value without on-going man hours. Ideas include:
Free curriculum
Gamified road map system
More concerts with different themes. Replace lessons with concerts that week. Use staff hours toward the concert to make them more epic.
Add themed weeks: e.g Youtube week, band week, rhythm section week,
Add challenges: E.g 30-day practice marathon, around the world challenge etc
Free month on second instrument (if doing groups), OR an unlimited lesson month (with automated bookings).
Your price should be built on:
Getting the best possible price to fulfil your vision.
E.g. If you’re trying to grow a large team and be able to provide lots of teacher hours then you may want to have a lower price point.
But if you're trying to build the most profitable school and you don't care about how many students you have then you can increase the price to find the point where you are earning the most net profit while still having a decent convert rate for your leads.
There will be so much more that go into lessons than your customers realise, so you need to tell them! We have created a list here with input from the #BAMSQUAD, feel free to make a copy and present it to your parents however you'd like:

![image698.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image698.png)
![image696.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image696.png)

*When is the best time to increase my fees?*
Price increases should be every year on the same date so customers know it's going to happen. They should be communicated with a reminder of the value stack so people are reminded of the true value (a much higher number).
Things to consider when choosing the time of year:
Your financial year
Yearly events such as Christmas, summer holidays
If you have a time of the year where you usually get lots of new students (e.g. start of a school year jan/sept) make sure they get ‘hooked’ in before you increase lessons
Australia/ New Zealand - term 2 can work well
Europe/ North America - Easter time can work well
For our school it was April 1, our school works on the calendar year, so we wanted to increase the price four months into the year, so students were already loving lessons and hooked on all the amazing experiences that they've been partaking in... Then we hit them with a price increase. The reason that we don't do it on 1 January is that our summer break proceeds that date, and so we don't want to give people another reason to rethink about returning for lessons in the New Year
Your year will likely be laid out different so have a think about what would be the most strategic date for you to choose as the day that you will increase your prices every year.

*What could I do instead of increasing my fees?*
Give yourself an additional week off of lessons - This makes your “price per lesson” higher, but it does not increase the actual out-of-pocket expense for a family.
Make other big policy changes - For example, if you know you need to completely overhaul your make-up lesson policy, you might want to focus on making this change instead. Changing some policies like make-up lessons can be a huge stressor to yourself and your families (though it will be less stress in the end). So adding a price increase on top of a change like that might increase the risk of losing students.
Eliminate some of your “extra” offerings - Perhaps you offer an extra performance class every month and that is becoming taxing to you. Perhaps the winter recital in December is becoming too much for you to handle (it *is* a stressful time of year!) and you’d like to take a break from it this year. Perhaps you want to try replacing a week of private lessons with group lessons instead
More information and ideas found here in module two under cashflow: Ways to Increase Your Profit Without Raising Prices

*Should I use the ‘Grandfathering’ method?*
This refers to keeping your original students at a lower rate to thank them for being with you longer.
It can work really well and help them feel valued as part of your school, but it can also be an admin nightmare which creates loads of additional manual work. So be aware of this.
It's best to email to explain the price increase and that you will keep them at a lower rate until a certain date a couple of months in the future to thank them for being a valued member of your school.
Additionally you could give them a chance to ‘buy up’ a block of lessons at the cheaper price before they move onto the increased price.

*What if a customer really can’t afford the price increase for a genuine reason?*
This is a tricky situation and something you will need to look at on a case by case basis.
Having an option in your school for people to donate to ‘scholarship lessons’ in different ways can help in these situations e.g:
If someone quits, could they donate their remaining lessons if they don’t want to come
Add $1 to your lesson fee each week to help fund the scholarship lessons
Raise money for it during concerts or events
If it’s a student you really enjoy teaching and really benefits from lessons you might just be happy to take the ‘pay cut’ for now.
Try to work with the customer on some sort of payment plan. You could keep them at the original rate for longer until a set date to reevaluate, you just don’t want this to go on forever!

*How do I increase my prices when my business gets to the point where I get sales tax? (e.g. VAT/ GST)*
Here's a letter you can use that Chris Hobley and Jonny wrote on a coaching call for his school.
Dear Students / Parents,
Today we've got some really great news and some not-so-great news.
The great news is that thanks to all your amazing support, our mission to help young people through music has allowed our business to grow! We now have 250 incredible students who are all sounding amazing.
The not-so-great news is that this growth has pushed us into a new tax bracket, which means we are now forced to add a 20% VAT tax to all of our music lessons. This VAT tax bracket change is something that only ever happens once. We want to be completely transparent—this tax is required by law, and all of it goes to the government; not a single dollar goes to [INSERT MUSIC SCHOOL NAME].
 We’re only adjusting our prices because we have to by law, not because we want to. We’re so grateful for your continued support and understanding as we navigate these changes!
 If this change causes you significant hardship, we do not want this to be the reason that you have to stop lessons, so please do get in touch, and we'll see if we can help you during this season.
[YOUR SIGN OFF]
![image694.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image694.png)

 Some Final Thoughts from us and the #BAMSQUAD...
Be sure to do a thoughtful evaluation before you raise your piano lesson price too high. Then, continue that careful evaluation before you make a tuition or policy change of any kind to make sure the change fits the goals you have for your studio and your life.
If you students/ families/ customers truely value you and your service, they will want you to do well therefore understanding the rate increase. If they don’t understand it then maybe they don’t value you enough and not worth keeping/ worrying about losing. You can replace them with someone who does value you enough.
Make sure you have a strong marketing campaign in place, therefore IF you lose a couple of students when you increase you can easily and quickly replace them, at your new price too!
