### Cashflow………………
# Money Fundamentals
Money Fundamentals for Running a Successful Music School
*Understand Basic Tax Laws:*
Get familiar with the tax rules of your country. Understand what you can claim as a business expense and what has to be classified as an asset.
Recognise the difference between assets (like pianos) and regular business expenses. Assets may need to be depreciated over time rather than immediately written off.
Be aware of thresholds. Some countries have limits on how much you can claim as an immediate business expense versus what must be depreciated over time.
*Impact of Asset Depreciation:*
Misunderstanding asset depreciation can lead to unexpected tax bills. If you purchase assets but can't write them off immediately, you'll need to depreciate them over time. This affects your taxable profit.
*Know Your Company Tax Rate:*
Be aware of your country's specific tax rate. For example, in New Zealand, it's 28 cents on the dollar.
*Choose the Right Accountant:*
Partner with an accountant who actively assists with tax planning and helps you maximise tax-deductible expenses.
Ensure they file your returns on time, giving you ample notice of any tax bills. This aids in planning and budgeting.
Regularly check in with your accountant. Ideally, meet every 3-6 months for an update on financials and tax dues.
*Stay Updated on Your Country’s Tax Year:*
Understand when your country's tax year starts and ends so you can anticipate tax liabilities.
*Active Financial Management:*
Just hiring a bookkeeper is not enough. It's crucial to actively manage your business financials. Being passive can lead to financial mishaps.
Sometimes, it's worth changing accountants if you feel your business needs are outgrowing their capabilities.
Remember, the goal is to remain in control of your finances, anticipate tax liabilities, and keep your music school running smoothly.
*Separate Business and Personal Finances:* Maintain distinct bank accounts for business and personal use.
*Bank Account Recommendations:*
Checking Account: For daily expenses and income.
Tax Account: Set money aside for taxes. Never use this account for emergencies; penalties for misusing tax funds can be severe.
Emergency Fund: Build it up to cover three to six months of expenses for peace of mind.
*Budgeting and Cash Flow Management:*
Invest in accounting software (e.g., xero.com, QuickBooks, MYOB).
Use cash flow tools:
*Cashflow Challenge (Module 2)*: Designed for music schools to regulate finances.
*HelloCashflow.com*: A software developed for music schools and small businesses. It integrates with accounting software, translating intricate reports into comprehensible visuals. Currently, it only syncs with xero.com, but will soon support others.
*Understanding Financial Reports*:
Profit and Loss Statement (P&L): A report card showing earnings and expenses. Make sure to be aware of factors like sales tax, assets, and debt payments that might not appear on the P&L.
Balance Sheet: Gives a snapshot of your business's assets (what you own) and liabilities (what you owe). This report helps determine if you are solvent (having positive net assets) or insolvent.
Cash Flow Statement: Tracks all income sources and expenses, displaying the net flow of cash in your business.
Budget Tool: Predicts future earnings and expenses. Use the budget tools in Cash Flow Challenge and HelloCashflow.com to anticipate potential cash shortfalls.
*Regular Monitoring*:
Hold a monthly finance review to assess the previous month’s performance and tweak future projections.
Stay proactive and educate yourself on these topics. Instead of handing everything off to an accountant, strive to be the CFO of your business.

………………..
# Ways to Increase Your Profit Without Raising Prices

How do you increase your bottom profit line without increasing your rates for current students?
If you're willing to think creatively, there are many ways to bring in more income for your music school, without asking all your loyal students to pay more per lesson. Of course, raising your rates every year is smart business practice to keep up with inflation and consistency of families expecting your rates to increase. However, this is not the only way to increase your profits!
In the module below, you'll find many practical ways to increase the profits of your school. These have been grouped into categories like getting smart with your finances or expanding what programs you offer to families in your school.

*We also asked the #BAMSQUAD community for their best ideas for increasing their studio profit:*
At the #BAMSQUAD, we're very passionate about being collaborative in the way we help music school owners grow their businesses. We might have lots of awesome ideas, but so does all the members of our BAM community! Collaboration helps every single person as the collective result is 10x greater than any one person could think of, on their own. We reached out to ask what your best ideas were, for this topic. Included in this list is your contributions as well!

Here's the list of ways to increase your profits without raising your rates
*Host Events:*
Run profitable birthday parties. Charge per child or as a package (e.g. $600 for the party, max 10 kids). Include some awesome package options such as recording your own song and having it recorded, a chance to try a range of instruments, a rhythmic percussion ensemble, games, snacks, party bags etc. You can have sponsors fill the party bags with their own supplies to save costs on having these made up.
![image740.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image740.png)
Run holiday workshops! You can do songwriting classes, intro to music, “taste test” instrument program and even ukulele sing-along programs
To expand on this workshop idea, develop exclusive higher priced one-off workshops for students. Encourage your teachers to develop 1-2hr workshops that you can sell exclusively to 5-10 students. Run this outside of normal lesson times and come up with a deal that inspires teachers to want to do this more regularly.
Offer a mini-course online for students who want to improve something like their theory knowledge, basic ukulele chords, intro to piano etc. This is also great for students who aren’t yet ready to sign up for lessons at your school, or for parents who have a sibling they don’t want to enrol in lessons just yet. We have an entire course on this in module 7 of the BAM Library.
Run once-off large events e.g ukulele and wine evening, Adult choir, a teen rock band with free pizza etc.
![image739.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image739.png)
* *
#### Danielle O'Donnell Rose
![image735.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image735.png)
As Danielle says, "Summer camps and more summer camps!!! It’s our favourite time of the year!! "
She fills up to 400 camp slots during the summer period, using a 4-day summer camp program that she specifically designed, called "Uke Crazy"! She also runs  for the camps. We asked her about how she markets her program so successfully and these were some of her best tips:
Email campaigns
Facebook Ads
Handing every member a goodie bag with their summer camp schedule on it, with a QR code to sign up
Flyers to local elementary schools to hand out
![image717.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image717.png)
![image717.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image717.png)
![image717.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image717.png)
Send personalised "BomBom" videos to parents and kids . Just a short video saying something like "Hey Lisa! This video is for YOU! I want to see you at summer camp this year! You have been rocking in it lessons and I can’t wait to jam with you at camp! You get $40 off for being a member! Text me now to get registered!”
Give teachers a little bonus if they get a kiddo signed up for summer camp. This encourages the teachers to talk it up in lessons and get the students excited.
Here's an example of the marketing materials that Danielle uses to build up interest and hype for her massive summer camp program.
![image714.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image714.jpg)
*Expand Your Offerings:*
Expand the types of lessons that you offer. Glee club (singing), band program, "mummy and me" music, composing, audio-engineering, pop-songs only, improvisation group, campfire songs group… the options are endless!
Launch a preschool music program. Don’t miss the opportunity to educate those 3-5yr old kids, who will stay in lessons with your school for a long time if they enjoy their early experiences! There's a great plug-n-play program in the BAM curriculum Library titled 'Musical Foundations.'
Add additional ensemble classes as an upgrade for current students. Call it something fun like “Music Makes Friends” and invite students to participate for a small fee.
![image731.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image731.png)
Put students into group lessons. Not only are group lessons an awesome experience for students, but it can greatly maximise the amount of income your school brings in per hour of teaching. We have a very comprehensive course on how to successfully transition to group lessons in module 4 of the library under 'A = Additional Programmes.'
Make sure you’re asking siblings to sign up for lessons!
Inform current students about the other lesson types you offer and actively try get them into classes that would benefit them. E.g a singer into piano classes, a drummer into band classes, a guitarist into singer-songwriter classes etc…. You may create a “tier” for students who do this, so it’s seen as a normal and acceptable part of learning at your school. Students in multiple lessons may be given VIP access to special events like fancy performance nights or busking events
Get into a routine of trying to upsell students. This could be from 30min to 45 / 60-min lessons or taking a second lesson time each week. Look out for timetable gaps and reach out to students on either side to see if they’d like to claim that spot and double their lesson time. Works best for one-on-one lessons.
Suggest the most expensive lessons first to leads and learn how to down-sell if necessary. Teach your team to do the same.
Create a discount for families signing up for 3 or more classes and promote this to families. Reach out to every family taking 2 lessons and try up-sell them to 3 lessons.
Create lessons programmes to offer in local schools during hours teachers usually struggle to get work.
#### Jessika Whitfield
 Jessika has a successful in-school program that boosts profits for her music school.  She customises each school program for what they're looking for. Her teachers bring all the instruments needed (usually ukuleles, bucket drums, and some sort of choir/singing). They use our BAM uke books and run 1x a week classes either during music class during school hours or in their after school program.
#### Kelly Bennett
Kelly has introduced  programs into her school. Because of the plug-n-play nature of this awesome program, she's been able to employ an advanced high school student to teach it. As the program is so simple to understand and of great quality, it's been awesome for Kelly to offload the teaching of this profitable program, with an affordable teacher.

*Sell Additional Products:*
Sell snacks! Have a bar fridge that students and their families can purchase protein bars, chocolate, soft drink, juice, chips, fruit cups, granola bars and even bottled water
Sell instruments and books. You can sell guitar packs that have a bag, lead, amp, strap, tuner, and picks. A keyboard pack with a keyboard stand and stool.
Develop your own curriculum and sell those books to students. You can find the most affordable way of having those books printed and making purchasing and learning from them a standard learning process at your school. We give you access to a full curriculum of Level 1, 2 and 3 books in the BAM Curriculum Library, titled 'Intro to Music' Books.
Create certificates and gamification that relate to the curriculum you sell. Use this to help you sell more books.
Provide a shopping list for starting each new instrument - stock those items yourself. You can also create an info pack for everything the new student needs to look for when purchasing a new instrument. End with offering a link to the instrument you sell which meets the criteria. Explain how it’ll save them time looking around if they buy from you direct.
Implement an instrument Hireage program for students who aren’t ready to buy their own instruments. Watch the videos about this in the BAM library, module 6 under 'I = Instrument Hireage & Sales' and you’ll be impressed with how easy and profitable this can be.
![image1008.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1008.png)
Sell branded t-shirts and other Merchandise. Sell these with limited time launches as opposed to being available all year round. Use shoplift and build in easy up-sells / add to carts. e.g if they want one t-shirt for $25, offer them an upgrade to 3 x t-shirts in 3 colours for $60. Encourage students to purchase and wear or use them at your school (t-shirts, drum stick rack, sweaters, music book bag, trinkets).
Up-sell resources, merchandise or instruments to customers while they’re excited and in impulse purchase mode. Build this into your induction process.

#### Jacob Burrows
Jacob sells accessories, instruments, stickers and merchandise. They call it their Rock Shop! He's also added a charitable focus with this program. So, funds from the sales of his Rock Shop, go towards music scholarships they can offer for underprivileged students to still learn music. You can hear about his charitable focus here:
This is his Rock Shop:
![image1001.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image1001.jpg)
* *
*Use Your Teaching Space Wisely:*
Maximise the hours you have available for lessons. Make sure that there’s no free timeslot for the prime 3.30-7 pm after school hours and that all your rooms are filled with classes
Find a more affordable location to rent for your music classes. Such as hiring a local scouts, community or church hall. Negotiate an affordable hourly rate and ask multiple places so you have more options available to you. 

PROS:
You pay only when you use the space
They usually do all the cleaning for you, so you can just pack up and go afterwards
There's always great parking (especially if you're teaching groups) because it's purpose-built for large groups
There are plenty of spaces that will let you store equipment on site
No upfront 3-months bond payment is required and no lock-in contract
Less mental stress whilst going through a growth period of the business.
Hire tutors to teach the days/times you don’t want to in order to increase your chargeable hours each week. Run lessons 6-7 days p/w.
If you have a commercial space, lease it out when you’re not using it. Think of groups like meditation classes, yoga sessions, birthday parties, AA meetings, mother’s groups and even paint 'n sip or art classes.
![image990.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image990.png)

*Manage Finances Wisely:*
Implement a 3% credit card surcharge
Charge extra for early arrivals and late pickups for any holiday program/summer camps you run. We would run programs 9am-3pm, then charge for additional time on either side.
Sell tickets to your recitals/concerts that are higher than the cost of putting that concert on. It’s an event and parents will be very happy to pay for an awesome experience and see their child perform on a big stage
Manage your finances better so you’re reducing your expenses and increasing your profit margin. We have a very important course on this, called the 'Studio Profit Formula' in Module 2 under 'Money Hacks.'
Keep the money in your account by making good money choices. Be sure to follow up with debt collection, avoid offering early bird discounts and pay people well but don’t overpay them! Check out our lesson called 'Money Mistakes' in module 2 under 'Money Hacks.
Having a no refund policy. Except for extreme circumstances where we decide a refund would be best, we do not give any refunds. if someone leaves with $x credit they can store it for future lessons or donate it to our foundation. This has saved thousands.
Go through your expenses and try to reduce every possible cost. Do a monthly review of your finances.
Set yourself sales targets and try to beat them every month.
Work with your accountant to reduce taxation expenses. Ensure this accountant is active in helping you grow your business, not just filling in your taxes.
Use payment plans and financing for parents. Set/enforce the terms.
Implement direct debit for billing
Negotiate employment agreements or contract tutor fees
 Charge more for one-off lessons.
Charge a registration fee

*Be A Leader and Look After Your Community:*
Stop working in your business teaching or doing admin and instead hire someone so you can work on growing your business.
Sell merchandise, drinks and snacks at your student concerts as fundraisers towards scholarship programs for kids that can’t afford lessons.
![image987.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image987.png)
Offer parents to donate towards another child learning that cannot afford lessons.
Hire a cinema for a fundraiser for a charitable programme in your studio. Pick a family-friendly movie.
Be the most encouraging music teacher in town. Train your team on how to be the same. Discuss situations where it was challenging to be encouraging and help come up with strategies for how to handle it better in future.
Ensure you (and any staff) always bring your A-game to lessons, your reputation hangs on it. Make professional development and weekly catch-ups part of your culture. Build a culture of continual improvement.
Make your lessons are more than music. Even something simple as starting the lesson with rating your week out of 10 and asking them about what’s on their mind etc, will build that mentoring relationship that not every teacher places importance on. Quitting lessons is then not about quitting lessons, it’s quitting a friend and mentor. That’s a lot harder.
Talk to your team about how you want to come across in all communications. I used to tell my team to always add smiley faces and ensure everything is always framed positively.
![image984.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image984.png)
Have a strategic innovation goal each quarter/year. What new thing will you work hard to implement that makes your customer experience that much better? Train everyone involved in how to implement this so the idea is implemented well.

*Finally, Consider Your Retention Strategy:*
Often, increasing your revenue simply means KEEPING the students you currently have, as you continue to acquire new leads. If you lose 2 and gain 2 (students) you never increase your bottom profit line. However, if you keep 2 and get 2 more, now your revenue will increase by every new student you bring in.

*Teach your Team Retention:*
Track your teacher's retention rates, look for trends and work on improving these rates. Identify what teachers with the best rates are doing and pair them with those with the worst rates and have them sit in on a lesson or two to watch for professional development.
Measure retention rates for the whole studio each month/year and try beat your previous period
Have a retention strategy in place that the team understands. Consider this: How are you improving customer experience, and customer engagement? Are you making the opportunity to learn with you more sticky and therefore harder to quit?
Implement a system for transferring students from one teacher to another more smoothly. E.g a joint lesson with both teachers before the first lesson with the new teacher happens.
Ensure your team is checking with parents to see how their child is talking about lessons at home? Do they enjoy it? Do they feel they’re being pushed too hard, not hard enough? What other helpful info could the parent offer?
 Train your team on how to teach students with different behavioural management problems or diagnosed conditions on the spectrum. e.g ADHD, autism. Etc
Review your whole student onboarding process. Poor onboarding is the #1 reason for a high churn rate.
Personalise lesson content for each student where possible and drip feed out the content so you keep people coming back for more.
![image981.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image981.png)
Set targets for how fast you’ll reply to emails. Also, keep your email list tidy. Address all emails to the customers' names so it feels personal.
When you have tricky time-consuming emails, sometimes it’s better to just send an audio or video email so it’s faster and easier for the person to grasp the context and feel appreciated. We use
Go above and beyond if you or your team makes big stuff up. I once drove 30mins at 8pm at night to drop off a box of chocolates to a family that was mucked around and apologised in person at their door. You don’t have to do that, but you can do something. Most customers will usually be pretty cool after a personal apology.

*Engage the Students for Better Retention:*
Highlight student of the month in your newsletter, make your students heroes.
Share before and after videos of students showing how far they’ve progressed in a year.
Create a student development pipeline so students can see the awesome opportunities that await them as they progress.
Create reward systems with certificates and prizes as students progress.
Always be looking to make your concerts/recitals as fun as possible, but also with new musically challenging performances that every student can be proud of.
Create monthly or quarterly challenges at your studio. E.g Record your first Youtube cover video, or practice 30-days In a row and get it signed off by a parent.
Ensure your waiting room area has some entertaining things to do. Focus on things that will build friendships. e.g two-player games.
Include the opportunity for songwriting and original compositions for students. Host a competition or concert of original material students can work towards.
Run themed concerts e.g Dress like a superhero and give out prizes. Get loads of photos to share on social media etc
Run an annual survey to all students. What’s one thing you’re doing well and what’s one thing you can improve. Review all the improvements you can make and make plans to implement the best ideas.
Provide monthly newsletter highlights to improve your communication and remind people of all the great stuff you’re offering and how hard you’re working behind the scenes for them.
Send the ‘We miss you’ email campaign to ex-students once a year. See if there’s any interest in them coming back and offer them a deal to do so.
![image649.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image649.png)
Give your students experiences your competitors aren’t. Look to implement things they love. A great example is doing video recordings of songs they’ve learned so they have something professional to share and that you can share on social media also.
Run a special mother-daughter lesson night, or father-son night.
Run a massive end of school party event for kids with live band.

# Introduction to the CASHFLOW COURSE
This is one of the most valuable resources in the #BAMSQUAD library. If you run out of money, then your school falls apart! Managing your cashflow is the most important thing to keep your business running and ensuring you succeed!
We have two tools for you to chose from to help you manage your music school finances: Hello Cashflow and the Cashflow Challenge.

*Hello Cashflow*
![image648.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image648.png)
* *
This is a tool that's been made for the #BAMSQUAD that works with the accounting software*.* (More accounting softwares to coming soon!) Hello cashflow will display all your finances in graphs tables and charts in an easy to read display for you, the business owner. Your accountant will be using the information from xero (or other accounting software) to process your taxes.
This is a more automated process as it works alongside your accounting software.

*Cashflow Challenge*
This is similar to Hello Cashflow but doesn't need an accounting software, it works off google sheets and you import all the numbers and information. This method is better if you don't have an accounting software set up... Although we do recommend you do set one up!
The cashflow challenge is laid out in the rest of this module and walks you set by step through the whole process.

………………..

This is one of the most valuable resources in the #BAMSQUAD library. If you run out of money, then your school falls apart! Learn how to master your studio cashflow with the 5-day Cashflow Challenge!
They say the numbers tell a story but when you get a report from your accountant; a Profit and Loss statement, for example, you can't see that story! At the end of this, you'll have some simple, easy to understand reports where you can get a full view, visually see how you're tracking, see where you're doing well and what needs fixing.
*Action Points:*
Take a selfie and post in the #BAMSQUAD Facebook group with the #cashflow challenge.
Download these below:
5-day Challenge Workbook
Your monthly budget checklist
Accountant email template

# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 1 - Q's for your accountant
This video covers the questions you're going to need to ask your accountant. An email template is provided for you to forward on.
(If you don't have an accountant, you should look into getting one).

*Action Point: *Download the attachment below. Delete any questions you are able to answer yourself. Send the remaining questions through to your accountant.
*Note: You can still continue through this course before you've heard back on the answers to these questions. *

*Email Template:*
Hi [name],
I'm working on my budget for the year ahead and I'm wondering if you can answer a few quick questions for me please?
If you don't have accounting software...
I would like to implement an online accounting software solution in my business.Which software do you recommend? Do you get any discounts with particular services?
(remember to take into consideration which software actually plugs into your student management system if you have one... or which one you should chase if you'll move to an SMS software in the future.)
In my online accounting software, how do I export a report that shows all my cash movements for the month? I need the numbers to include sales tax for cashflow purposes? Is there an easy function to access this quickly or a shortcut I can set up?
Do you have a list of dates where my various tax payments are due sol can put these in my calendar and cashflow forecast? If you could send me the dates and the tax types that would be awesome.
Where can I find my un coming tax payments in my accounting software to best budget what the payments are going to be as they accumulate?
What's dollar value does an equipment purchase in my business go from being a fully tax. deductible expense to needing to be classified as an asset?
Do you think there are any particular business insurances I need to look into this year to stay compliant or mitigate potential risks? Keeping in mind the size of my business and my cashflow, what are your recommendations on weighing up the genuine risk of a situation occurring and me needing the insurance vs the savings of not purchasing said insurance(s) ?
I'm looking to simplify my payroll and tax deductions. What payroll systems do you recommend for a business of my size? Do you have any connections for discounts with any particular providers?
What percentage of an employees pay am I obligated pay in superannuation/retirement and health insurance? Are there any other regular fees I should be budgeting for for staff casts?

[insert your personal sign off]
…………………….

2. All the data you enter into the other sections, works together to produce reports. This is what will drive a lot of your decision and bring that clarity. It will give you a pretty accurate picture of what's going to happen in advance. From cashflow forecasts to your adherence to your budget, you'll get monthly, quarterly and year-to-date breakdowns of different aspects of your business and help you stay on track and reach your goals. You'll get simple, informative graphs which will help you know things like retention rates, churn rates and how much it costs to acquire a customer and how much it costs to acquire and retain a customer.
![image666.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image666.png)

3. For example, here's a graph that shows the *customer lifetime value*. You can see how much a customer is worth - trending up is what you want to see!
![image670.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image670.png)

4. Another example is the feasibility study for *adding a team member. *This will help you budget it to make sure you can afford it. You can set up the cost for bringing the person on and find out the return on their role.
![image669.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image669.png)
Below is the *cashflow projection*. Once you've entered in the details for hiring that new staff member, you'll see another line showing the impact on your cashflow and whether it will benefit your business overall.
![image668.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image668.png)

# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 1 - Tax year set up
In this video you will set up your tax year, which will automatically pull into the rest of the spreadsheet.
The set up here is very important - you must not change it once you have set it up.

1. The months that are entered here will pull into the rest of the spreadsheet. The default is from January to December; the calendar year:
![image667.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image667.png)

2. However the months are set up, they will pull through to the rest of the spreadsheet in that order:
![image664.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image664.png)

3. If you prefer to change the tax year, you can type a different month into *Month 1, *then drag it across and the months will update accordingly. In New Zealand, our tax year runs from April through till March, so I could have my months ordered to follow the New Zealand tax year if I wanted to:
![image663.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image663.png)

4. Having updated that, those months pull into the rest of the spreadsheet, so my first month now shows as April and the end month is March:
![image662.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image662.png)

5. In Australia, the tax year begins in July and ends in June. You can enter the months based on the tax year in your country, or you can stick with the calendar year, like I do. Whatever makes sense for your business.
![image661.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image661.png)

6. Once you have finished setting this up, you can hide the sheet if you want to. Once set up, this must not be changed. You need to decide on how you want to budget; when that year should start and end, and leave it as that in the spreadsheet.
![image665.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image665.png)

# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 2 - Bank Balances
Walk through how to plug in your bank balance at the start of each month.

1. The first step is to label your accounts according to the different business bank accounts you keep. I suggest you use your the 3 that I have listed below; cheque, tax and emergency fund. You can add other accounts too if you like, for example, if you're working toward a large business project, or anything where you need to set money aside. The most important thing is that your business account is kept separate from your personal account.
*Note: You'll want to be growing your emergency fund as you can afford to and build it up to 2 - 3 months worth of expenses. *
![image657.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image657.png)

2. Once you've labelled your accounts in the spreadsheet, you then need to load the bank balance as at the 1st of the month for each of these months, for each account that you have. Get your statements and it'll tell you the opening balance for each month. If I was loading in all the way from January to October, I would need to go find the statements for all of those months leading up to October, and load in the 1st of the month balance date.
Here is an example from one of my bank statements for the month of October:

![image654.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image654.png)

3. Take your opening balance and enter it into the correct cell of your spreadsheet:
![image653.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image653.png)

4. If your statement is not clearly displaying an opening balance for the 1st of the month (if it's showing the 20th to the 20th, for example), just scroll down a look at the statement expenses and find the balance for the first of the month. It doesn't matter if it's not completely accurate; if it's $30 or $40 off. This will still give you a much closer picture than just guessing! You will need to do this for all the months leading up to today so that all the months are completed in your spreadsheet, up to the most recent month.
![image652.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image652.png)

From now on, you need to enter this information at the start of each month when you open your bank statement. This will help with producing your cashflow report and only takes a few minutes to do.

# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 2 - Customer Lifetime Value
What is the value of a customer at your music school? What's the most you should spend to gain a customer? Here's how to enter the data in which will be used to calculate the life-time value of your customers.
The most simple form we can use to calculate the value of a customer at your music school is your total all-time revenue of your business divided by the total all-time customers in your business.
![image651.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image651.png)

*Calculating all-time revenue:  *
This is going to be easiest if you have an accounting software. You need to login to your accounting system and view your profit & loss statement from the time you started your business, to the most recent statement. This will show you the total amount you've earnt (you may or may not want to enter sales tax if that's included in that number). If you use a manual system, you will have to add this up manually.
*Total all-time customers:*
This will also be easy if you have some kind of software used to track your student numbers. If you don't, you can give it your best guess. If you're just starting out or you don't want to backtrack this information, just start doing it from here on. You can leave the previous cells blank and enter the data going forward so come next year, you'll be able to calculate this.

 *Note: *Don't worry too much if it's not perfectly accurate; as long as you're tracking accurately going forward, you're getting the benefit of seeing the way that your customer lifetime value is trending.

# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 2 - Loan Injections
This video shows where you will load any money you've received from any loans you may have taken in your business.

* *To enter the loan details into the spreadsheet, write in who you borrowed money from in the left hand column. Then enter the amount you borrowed in the relevant month; the month when the money hit your bank account. If there was money borrowed in a previous year (in a month that is not displayed on your spreadsheet), do not enter it in here. Keep updating this going forward, whenever you receive a cash loan.
![image307.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image307.png)

* *We want to take note of these cash injections so that we can separate them from our net profit. Otherwise, it's going to look like you generated more money from busines than you did. Loans are not income as they are not generated through work that you did. So, it's important to separate these out.
*Note:* If you haven't taken out any loans, you can skip this step.

# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 2 - Get your numbers ready
This video shows you how to gather your historical data so that you can can then plug it into the rest of the spreadsheet. It is essential that you watch this to ensure that you do not load the wrong figures.
All your historical data, including revenue, staff costs, marketing, overhead expenses, debt payments, tax payments, assets you may have purchased and money you may have taken out or put into the business will need to be loaded into the spreadsheet.
![image306.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image306.png)
First off, we need to gather that information and we need to make sure we do that correctly. (I am going to assume that you are using an accounting software).
If you aren't using an accounting software, I recommend talking to your accountant about which one would be best for you to use. Some of the most popular options include; Xero, myob, Sage and Freshbooks:
![image305.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image305.png)
![image311.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image311.png)
![image310.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image310.png)
![image309.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image309.png)

You will need to load the data into the spreadsheets from the previous 3 months; the next few videos will show you how to do that. Before we do that, there is something very important I need to show you so that you gather this information correctly. When you load a profit and loss statement, this will show you your income and expenses for the month, but, when you have sales tax in your country (GST, VAT etc) this sales tax will often be deducted off your profit and loss statement. However, you want to have the actual amount that came out of your account because you want the spreadsheet to align with your bank statements/balances. We need the exact amounts that went into your account and came out of your account, including taxes and we will track those tax payments too. This will mean that the bank balance will be exact in the reports that are produced here. So, you're not going to load your monthly expenses from your profit and loss statement. You'll need to load them from your accounting software.
*Note: You'll see I've ticked 'cash basis' in Xero. Cash basis is the difference between saying to your accounting software, show me the money that's coming in and out in the month based on when I invoiced it or when I loaded it to be paid versus when it actually hit my account. For example, if I invoiced someone for a payment due on the 20th of March, and the customer didn't pay until April, it would show the payment arrived in March if I do not tick the 'cash basis' box. When I do select 'cash basis', then the accounting software will show the payment arriving in April, when it actually hit my account.*
![image308.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image308.png)

What you want to find is your 'cash summary'. If you are using Xero, you need to click on 'reports'.
![image302.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image302.png)

There, you will find 'cash summary'.
*Note: I have highlighted this in my system by clicking the star. *
![image301.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image301.png)

I can't show you my private accounts, but here, it will show you every payment that went in and out of your account. You will see you can tick the box 'include GST', so you want to click this so that it includes the tax as well.
![image300.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image300.png)

You will see that you also have a dropdown to include 'non-operating movements' under 'more options'. You want to to include this as well; these are the movements in your business that are more than just income and outgoings. For example, tax payments, loan payments and asset purchases.
![image283.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image283.png)

You will need to load up all the months you need, so you can enter the most recent month you are wanting and in the 'compare with' drop down, select 'previous 2 periods' so it will then include 3 months in the cash summary.
![image282.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image282.png)
If there's an option to save, favourite or 'star' your cash summary in your accounting software, then you'll definitely want to do this. You'll need to be entering the information in each month going forward.
Remember: It's worth the little time this takes to get the spreadsheet up to date, so push through, persevere and it will pay off!

This video shows how to track your music schools revenue / income streams properly

# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 4 - Tax payments
Don't let your tax payments catch you off guard! Here's how you can track your tax payments.
Tracking tax payments in the spreadsheet Is a very important part of business. We want to know when we're due to pay it and how much it's going to be, roughly, and we want to load it in to the spreadsheet so that our cash flow is accurate.
![image189.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image189.png)
This is where the questions you sent to your accountant are going to come in. You would have asked them what kinds of tax you will have to pay throughout the year; you'll need to enter those into the left-hand column and you can delete the ones that are not relevant. Then you will load in what you're budgeting your tax payments will be in the relevant months. Once the month has passed, you can go back and enter the actual tax bill.
![image176.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image176.png)
*One of the questions that you would have asked your accountant was where you can see your accumulating tax liability (the tax cost that's accumulating for the current period). There is a function on my accounting software Xero where I just click a button to see the tax amount day-by-day. It will tell me my GST (or VAT/sales tax) for this period is currently $5,000 or $10,000 for example, and will also tell me the due date for that payment. *

This is your monthly tracking for your tax payments. It doesn't have a lot of information but gives you an overview of your regular tax payments:
![image174.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image174.png)

 So, go ahead and load in your actual tax payments for the previous months, as well as your budgeted tax. (If you need to get the figures for your actual tax payments in the previous months, get those from your accountant. If you don't know what your budget was, then just make it the same as actual tax paid). Then, budget ahead, if you have an idea of what your tax payments are likely to be.

 ……
# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 4 - Asset purchases
This video explains what asset purchases are and how to track them properly.
In your accounting questions, you were to ask your accountant what your asset limit is in your jurisdiction. This threshold could be $500, $1000, $5000 or maybe $10,000; each country/area has different rules and thresholds. You will need to know this so that you are aware of when a purchase is classified as an expense and when it is classified as an asset.
Expense:
Below a certain threshold, a purchase can be considered a tax-deductible expense, meaning that the expense can be written off 100% in your tax return.
Asset:
An asset cannot be deducted in full in the year that you made the purchase; it has to be depreciated, meaning that you will deduct a portion of it in your tax return each year until it is used up.
Once you've found out what the limit is, you'll know whether to enter a purchase in the overhead expenses section or the asset purchases section. Then you can enter the details of any asset purchases in the left-hand column of this spreadsheet and put the purchase amount in the relevant month. Gather the information for the previous months and enter that in too. When it comes to the end of the year, it will be easy to show your accountant your asset purchases which they will use to fill out a depreciation schedule (where they record all the asset purchases and track the remaining value to be depreciated for each item).
![image172.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image172.png)

Once up and running, you can also budget ahead if there are assets you'd like to purchase; you'll be able to see the affect on your cash flow. There's only this one graph for asset purchases as that is all that is required to understand this section.
![image170.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image170.png)

……
# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 4 - Owner funds in/out
This video explains what owner funds in/out are and how to track them properly so you're not caught off-guard with any unexpected tax payments.
When you put money into your business or take money out of your business, you want to record it here. When you put money in, you are loaning money to the business. When you take money out, it's effectively taking profit in advance, before paying tax on it or making any other deductions. So you need to record this to make sure you don't get caught out when it comes time to pay taxes.
You need to enter the amount you put in or took out in the relevant month. A balance will be displayed showing the amount of cash you've taken out of the business that tax year. If it's money put in, it will show as a negative number. The graph on the left displays these movements with a red bar for cash in and a green bar for cash out. Taking profit in advance is a good thing, as long as the business is still able to run when those funds are removed.
![image183.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image183.png)

Whenever you do take funds out, you want to physically transfer that money. Go to the bank accounts spreadsheet:
![image181.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image181.png)

Transfer the funds from the cheque account to your tax account. This should be done physically too.
![image179.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image179.png)

……
…………….
# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 4 - Reports walkthrough
 Well done! You've made it to the reports section. This is what everything works toward; to show you the reports that give you the overall health of your business.
**Disclaimer: numbers are just made up and do not reflect a real business. *
You will have a budget line and an actual line for most of the graphs. You always want to be budgeting until the end of your current tax year and when the next year is nearing, create a budget for that year too.

*Cashflow Forecast*
Cashflow is the most important thing in your business; it's like the blood or the oxygen of your business. If you run out of money, your business can't function. In the graph below, you can see that the months November, December and January are trending down. If it keeps going down, you're going to lose money, so you need to cut costs and boost revenue.
![image161.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image161.png)

This is where you go through your revenue tabs to see if there's any way to increase revenue. Likewise, go through the expenses tabs to see if there's any way you can cut some costs.
![image160.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image160.png)
It's also important to know what your overdraft is is you have one. If you have no overdraft and see that the line is projected to drop to zero, you need to do something about this months in advance. Make sure you budget as accurately as possible using historical data; not too optimistic, not too pessimistic. Most business owners have no idea what their cashflow is but this allows you to predict what is going to happen.

*Revenue vs. Expenses:*
* *This is a graph for that basic business question; Am I spending more money than I make? You want to make sure wherever possible, that the blue line (revenue) is higher than the orange line (expenses). I often refer to the gap between the two lines as ' your line of contentment'. Your line of contentment is the amount that you're prepared and happy to spend and the bigger the gap, the more profit, the more margin you have, the more money you can put towards cool projects, pay yourself more, pay your staff more or whatever you want to do. As you can see, August was an amazing month for this fake business, but a bit of work is needed in July and December because the lines are crossing over. Time to do some damage control!
![image159.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image159.png)

Total revenue vs. total expenses:
This is a simplified version of the graph above. This shows Year to Date, the total that has been brought in and the total that has been spent. The difference is displayed which is sitting at $42,000. This looks good but there's a few other things to have a look at before we can determine if that really is good.
![image157.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image157.png)

*Year to date breakdown of spending:*
This shows the breakdown of your expenses. When you hover over each section, it will tell you the dollar amount and will also give you a percentage.
![image142.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image142.png)

*Net profit breakdown:*
* *Net profit is what is left in the business after you've paid all your expenses and paid yourself. Whatever is left is your taxable income in the business which you'll have to pay some tax on. This is where you focus should be because the larger the number is, the more successful your business is. You don't just want to be making $100 at the end of the year; you want to be making 10%, 15% or 20% profit. I've set that as a target because that is a reasonable profit but some people aren't even paying themselves so just doing that would be a big step up for them. So first off, you want to set a target; you can choose the percentage yourself and enter this in.
![image139.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image139.png)

The next section is going to show you exactly where you're at and how you're tracking toward your target. It shows both the percentage as well as the cash figure. It also has a prediction for you, showing what you are projected to have if you follow your budget.
![image136.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image136.png)

It will also show you how much you need to either reduce expenses or increase revenue in order to achieve your target. You'll probably aim to do both of those things, and you'll want to take into account how many months are remaining before the end of your tax year. This isn't your true net profit; it's not the number you will be providing to your accountant at the end of the year for filing your return. But excluding the technical things, this gives you an accurate picture of where you're business is at.
![image133.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image133.png)

*Student Numbers:*
* *The next lot of information is showing student numbers. You'll see there are graphs for how your actual student numbers compare to your targets, as well as students enrolled and students cancelled.
Number of students enrolled; target vs. actual:
![image152.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image152.png)
Number of new enrollments gained:
![image149.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image149.png)
Number of students cancelled:
![image248.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image248.png)
Student retention rate:
![image261.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image261.png)
Percentage of students retained:
![image260.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image260.png)
* *Churn rate - percentage of students cancelled:
![image258.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image258.png)
Cost to acquire a customer:
*Numbers are not an accurate reflection of what it would/should cost in reality; they are made up figures. *
Over time, as you get better at marketing, these should start going down.
![image255.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image255.png)

Average cost to acquire (and retain) a customer:
 This is the average cost for acquiring a new student and also the cost of acquiring and retaining a student. The graph above shows you the exact number at that certain point whereas this number is the average over time.
*Numbers are not an accurate reflection of what it would/should cost in reality; they are made up figures. *
![image238.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image238.png)

# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 5 - Introduction
This provides a quick overview of what you will be covering in day 5 of the 5 day challenge.
Well done! You've made it to the Day 5! By the end of today, you're not just going to have all the data until up to today but you're also going to be budgeting ahead. You'll finally have a clear picture of your finances and be back in control, you'll also know how to monitor this and keep it up to date going forward.
*We'll be learning how to do the following:*
Feasibility study for hiring a new staff member
Feasibility study for a new project
 We'll wrap up with some action points at the end of this which include setting some targets and sharing a selfie so that the rest of the #BAMSQUAD can celebrate with you for reaching the end of the 5 day challenge!!

……
# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 5 - Budget ahead for the rest of the year
Now your numbers are loaded up to the current month, this video explains how to budget ahead and provides a checklist for you to use every month to stay on top of your finances and feel in complete control.

![image314.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image314.png)
Budget the rest of your tax year using the *monthly budget checklist* download. That will pull into all the reports and you'll be in a better position to predict what the rest of the year will look like.

# CASHFLOW CHALLENGE DAY 5 - Feasibility study #1 - Budgeting new staff
This video shows you how to figure out if you can afford that new team member you'd like to hire.
The bottom tabs are feasibility tests for if you want to hire a new team member and if you have a new project you'd like to do:
![image235.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image235.png)

You'll start by entering in the set up costs for hiring a new staff member. Make sure you include every cost that you will incur. Then you'll need to enter the details of their pay; hours, hourly rate and number of pay weeks in that month:
![image232.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image232.png)

The next step is to enter the cash return of their role. See the example below where a staff member might do a combination of group lessons, one-on-one lessons and admin:
*Note: No cash return for admin as no-one is being billed for that. *
![image229.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image229.png)

These numbers are made-up but you can see how they move the needle - there is an initial drop because of the set up costs but it does increase the cash flow overall.
*Note: If you see the cashflow line go down, it doesn't necessarily mean that you shouldn't hire that person; this graph just helps you to see the impact on your cash flow and helps you make sure you won't run out of money. There are other benefits to hiring a staff member beyond cash flow - we will cover this in more detail in Module 6.*
![image246.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image246.png)

Here's an example of what it might look like if you were going to start an 'intro to music' programme.
Enter in all of your set up costs:
![image1002.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1002.png)

Enter in your cash return from this project:
