# FAQ #1 - Not all of my employees are using the retirement plan. Should I put a “0” in the Super Retirement Column for them?
The short answer is 'no'. Here's why you shouldn't and what you should do instead.
Let's say, for example, Kevin is not using his retirement scheme. Don't place a zero in his retirement cell because this will clear the formula and you won't be able to get it back if you do that. (Remember that the left hand chart is only a calculator to help you work it out).
![image973.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image973.png)

To calculate Kevin's wages, you should instead enter a zero in the retirement contribution percentage so it displays 0.00% (yellow cell at the top). As you can see, Kevin's wages come up as a total of $1515 which you would then carry over into the relevant month in the right-hand chart:
![image974.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image974.png)

Now that Kevin's wages have been calculated, you can change the retirement contributions percentage back to whatever percentage it should be for the other staff:
![image975.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image975.png)

You will need to manually change this each time when calculating the wages for your staff, so you may like to type 'no super' next to the name/s of those who are not making contributions to their retirement fund, so that you remember to do this:
![image976.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image976.png)

…………………
# FAQ #2 - Tax payments - is this taxes we pay on our yearly tax return? Or is this sales tax we pay on purchases?
Any tax your paying from your company account is what you're wanting to load in to this spreadsheet.
This is the spreadsheet we use to load in taxes. Both your sales tax/VAT/GST as well as your companies income tax. When your accountant tells you that there is tax to be paid, you need to clarify if the tax is for your personal or business account.
![image977.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image977.png)
We strongly recommend using an accountant! Make sure you ask around and get some recommendations on who may be a good accountant for you.

,...........
# FAQ #3 - Do I need to use a payroll service if its just me?
This is what most people do when they are just starting out. It's really up to you whether you want to pay yourself as owner funds or through payroll. But what is the best option? And why?
If you don't have any employees, then you can just take owner funds out to pay yourself.
![image978.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image978.png)

 But you do want to work toward paying yourself because then you'll have a much better record of what's happening in your business, the profit you really have after you deduct the cost of running your business. So you'll want to enter yourself in here, even if you are the only person and you want to pay yourself as you go, like you would an employee. There are some amazing payroll systems that do everything for you which don't cost a lot and are definitely worth it.
![image961.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image961.png)
So I would encourage you to pay yourself as an employee, pay your tax as you go so you don't get stung with a big tax bill at the end of the year and you'll also get a clear picture of what your profit is at the end of the year. So if you aren't doing this already, work toward getting onto that as soon as you can.

………………
# FAQ #4 -I never "budgeted" for most items I purchased, Should I enter "0" in budget column?
 Definitely! You should be budgeting going forward.
I wouldn't enter 0 in the budget; just enter the same amount as what you actually spent when entering your historical data. Otherwise you'll end up with a massive gap between budgeted and actual but you'll want to keep these as close together as possible.

……………….
# FAQ #5 - If I purchase QuickBooks, I assume that would fall under accounting software?
Absolutely!
We separate our accounting software from what you pay your accountant. It's up to you if you want to separate these things out or if you want to keep it simple and just keep your accounting software and all payments in one line in your expenses.
The main thing is to make a decision on whether you'll separate the expenses out and make sure you enter your expenditure in the right category. If you decide to separate out the accounting software from what you pay your accountant, you may also want to separate out your regular payments to your accountant from your end of year tax bills too. It's up to you.

……………….
# FAQ #6 - I bought a new Mac Laptop. Does this fall into an expense category?
One of the benefits of owning a business is that a lot of things in your life are tax deductible. The question to ask is whether a lap top is an expense or an asset.
In my country, something like a lap top would most certainly be an asset - so you will need to check with your accountant what the asset limit is, which was one of the questions in the list to send to your accountant. If it is an asset, it needs to be entered into the spreadsheet as an asset purchase which likely means it would be subject to depreciation (deducted over time) rather than deducted in full in that tax year. Write in the description, your budget and the actual amount spent. Then you can show your accountant the asset purchases at the end of the year so it can be added into their depreciation schedule. These would be deducted in part in that tax year and then again the following year in part, and so on, until there is nothing remaining to deduct.
![image964.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image964.png)

……………………..
# FAQ #7 - I bought my new domain name, had a logo designed, and spent money to start my website. Would these be design related costs? or Marketing?
 Great question! Personally, I would separate them out. Here's how I would do that.
For the domain name, set up a category called 'website costs' so anything like domain names or security certificates you may need to buy, I would record that under website costs.
For new logo design, I would set up a category called 'design fees' and load the cost for the logo in there.
That's how I would do it. Split things out, because you're going to have ongoing costs for your website and you're going to have ongoing design costs. So I'd keep them under overhead expenses and I'd treat the marketing more as ongoing marketing/ads. Whilst website costs is a way to market yourself, it's really an overhead expense to get running, in the same way that you need a piano to teach piano lessons. So, I personally wouldn't classify website costs as marketing.

………………
# FAQ #8 - Not sure where to put Zoom, Spotify, Amazon Prime and BAMSQUAD memberships.
 Great question! I would put them in their own categories, except the #BAMSQUAD membership.
Zoom, Spotify and Amazon Prime can all be made into categories. For the #BAMSQUAD membership, I would create a category for 'professional development' and put it in there.
In my business, we have a professional development category for each team member. When I was running my music school, there was professional development for me and the leadership team; they all had their own small budget they could spend, and then professional development for each of the other staff. So the #BAMSQUAD membership, you may want to put it into a category for your own personal, professional development.
As for Zoom and Spotify, I keep them in their own categories, so that's what I would recommend.

 ……………
# FAQ #9 - A question about categorising printing costs / curriculum
Great question! Anything you use in your business, you get to claim as an expense.
Instead of purchasing ink and paper from your personal account, buy it from your company account. Then you claim it as an expense and if your tax rate is 20%, you essentially save 20% on what you've purchased. If your tax rate is 30%, then you save 30% on that purchase. Why pay extra if you don't have to?
Set up a category for printing costs in your overhead expenses spreadsheet. You can separate it out if you want to; for example, when we were printing out books to sell, we would classify that as curriculum printing. Then we could see exactly what we were selling and how much it cost us to produce and clearly see any profit we made from that. Then for the standard printing expense category, we would put any costs related to printing on site; ink, paper, servicing of the printer etc.

……………….
# FAQ #10 - How do you account for payroll taxes when the % vary each pay period?
 Great question! Lets go to the employee tab and take a look.
We'll take Kevin for example. Say you're going to pay Kevin $25 per hour for 12 hours of work. Set up the insurance and superannuation percentages so that these are automatically calculated. So the amount you're going to use to calculate tax is the amount in the 'total pay for month' column. If there's an additional amount not included in here, you'll need to add it on top. So it there's another hidden expense, let's say 4% for example, you'll just need to add that in to your budget. The chart on the left is just a calculator to help you work things out quickly. You can come in to use this tool every week if it is changing every week, but the idea here is that it is a rough guide to help you work out how much you might be spending each month. And the actual amount may end up being a bit different from the budget but that's fine - as long as it's not hugely off. You aren't using this to calculate your employees payroll taxes - you're just using this as a guide to help you with your budgeting. This calculator doesn't work out the tax - it only looks at the total amount that will be leaving your bank account so that you can budget for it. You will still be relying on your payroll software/staff to accurately calculate the payroll and then enter the actuals into this spreadsheet after the payments have been made to your employees.
![image967.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image967.png)

…………………
# FAQ #11 - Question about where to add husband and wife salary
Great question! You would load your salary into the overhead staff costs and the dividends would go into the owner funds in/out tab.
So the salary that you are paying yourself which is going through the payroll would be entered here:
![image970.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image970.png)

The extra payments you're taking as dividends, you'll need to enter in here. Then you'll need to make sure you're setting aside your tax based on the figure here.
![image972.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image972.png)
If you are taking a lot of drawings from your business, then you might want to consider upping your salary so if you paid yourself $50,000 one year, then maybe increase that to $55,000 the following year. That's how I would do it.

……………….
# FAQ #12 - Question about budgeting pay when it changes every week
The overhead staff costs tab is set up to work monthly. But there are ways around it! Remember, this is just a calculator to help you budget well.
If the hours for your employee/contractor are changing week to week, there are a few options. If you want to be extremely accurate, then you could either enter the details into the calculator for 1 week at a time and then manually add the totals. Or alternatively, you could build your own tab that would work for your situation if you want to go to that effort.
But remember, this is just a tool to help you find the figures to enter in for your budget. So you could be a little more rough about it; maybe find an average to enter in for the month over something slightly above average. Whatever works best for you.
![image943.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image943.png)

…………………
# FAQ #13 - Question about Gross or net pay in payroll
 What you want to show is the money that is going out of your account. Gross is the goal!
Let's say you are paying Kevin $1040. If you are using some kind of payroll system, then $1040 is what is leaving your account. It's straightforward and your ideal situation.
If you are paying the tax at some later date then it complicates it more. You would need to separate the net pay from the tax. For example, maybe you are paying Kevin a net amount of $832 and the tax of $200 or so is going to the tax department further down the track, then you would add the tax portion into the tax payments tab to be paid at a later date. But, you would have to do that manually for every staff member, which is why it would be ideal to use the gross figure and pay their taxes at the same time so the full amount would leave your bank account.
This is why I recommend using a payroll system - it doesn't cost a lot and it's so worth it as it makes things a whole lot easier. So gross is best and that's what you're aiming for if you're not already doing that.
![image944.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image944.png)

…………………
# FAQ #14 - Question about tracking bank loan repayments
This would be entered into the cash injections from loan tab and the debt payments would be entered in the debt payments tab. Any activity outside your current tax year would not be included in these spreadsheets.
In your cash injections from loans tabs, you would enter the amount that arrived in your account in the month that it arrived. If the money arrived in a month prior to what's shown in your spreadsheet, for example, if your tax year is April 2021 - March 2022, and you received the money in January 2021, then you wouldn't include that here. So that's how you handle the money coming in from the loan.
![image945.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image945.png)

For your debt payments, you would enter the amount for the repayments each month, from when are going to start making the repayments onwards. You will keep loading those in each month, each year, until the debt is cleared. You are not managing the debt here, in terms of the interest or the balance. You are simply showing money going out. So if the repayments aren't taking place in this tax year, you simply don't include them here.
![image946.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image946.png)

……………….
# FAQ #15 Question about listing credit card payments and expenses
![image947.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image947.png)

### Money Hacks……………..
# Money Hacks
When it comes to managing your studio finances, REMEMBER THIS:
Cents add to dollars,
Which adds to tens of dollars,
Which adds to hundreds of dollars,
Which adds to thousands of dollars,
Which adds to tens of thousands of dollars.

*What do we mean by 'Money Hacks'?*
Sometimes, one of the smartest ways to increase your profitability as a music school is not to market and just fill your rooms with more students. Instead, there are many other places you could look to increase profits, save money or save time (Which also saves you money!)
Here is a big list of some 'money hacks' that you could implement in your school, to increase your bottom line. This ultimately means you can have more profit left for you to either keep yourself or re-invest into your business.
 We've broken these hacks down into a number of different categories and every time we or anyone from the #BAMSQUAD discover a new one, we will update the list below for you. So please do share your ideas in the comments below or in the.

*Improve your School Billing System:*
Get into a pattern of updating your prices every year on the same date. In New Zealand, we did always increase prices at the start of term 2. This way, any new kids would already be hooked on lessons from term 1 and it also aligned easier with our tax year. You will have your own cycle where you can choose your optimal increase point.
Record any unpaid fees you were unable to collect as an expense, as this reduces your taxable income. Chat with your accountant on how to set this up in your accounting software. It's something you're able to do because services were delivered (lessons taught) but you were not paid in full (for whatever reason).
Move to direct debit billing for all students in the school. You want to be in control of the date lessons are paid. If people get cranky about it and refuse, then hike up the lesson prices by 15-20% and then say 'We have a 15-20% discount for those on direct debit, which card would you like to use?
If you do quarterly/ termly billing, then offer early pay incentives, not cash discounts. For example, we offered movie tickets to the first 50 students to pay their invoices and everyone else that paid by the due date went into the draw to win one of 10 double passes. This way you get the kids excited about lessons and they help encourage their parents to pay on time!

*Clever Tips for Managing Your Staff and Students:*
Have a strong cancellation policy that forces families to give you notice when stopping lessons. This buys you time to replace students from a waiting list or increase your marketing.
Do away with refunds or reschedules. Replace all missed lessons with a “recap” video from the teacher. The teacher can record this video during the normal lesson time that has been missed. Legitimate refunds are replaced with credits that they can use for future lessons or donate towards your charitable focus (e.g kids that can't afford lessons).
Make a plan for missed lessons due to public holidays. Know the dates of all of these in advance, decide how you will handle it within your school and only give families that one option. Depending on how your school operates you may be able to still bill for those lessons but move them to school holidays or weekend slots with lots of notice.
Create multiple contracts for your staff team. E.g 5hrs admin, 10hrs teaching and pay these two tasks at 2 different rates. They don’t have to be paid the same rate for all the work they do. Separate the teaching hours and non-teaching hours into fair rates which may differ.
![image1076.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1076.png)
Remember to always hire for what the business needs, not what the applicant wants.
Invest time into a good retention system so that new students are nurtured and stay for longer. This means that new students will be an increase to your profit margin, and not just what keeps your business stable. The more leaky holes you plug up in your bucket/funnel, the more money stays in your pocket.
Go paperless. For posters that kids hold up in social media photos, have them hold the poster on an iPad. Have a QR code for concert programs at your events for parents to scan.
Charge for things like concerts. Make it worth the money by having good photos, fun decorations, a theme etc. At a minimum, the concert fees should cover the cost of running that event.
Bump the price up of your lessons and include books and video demonstrations to add value
Make Up-sells, Cross-sells, down-sells (when they quit) wherever you can. Examples include things like:
Get students learning a second instrument with you
Entice students to take two lessons p/w instead of one
Get siblings booked in for lessons
Get students to join your band program
Create special workshops
Down-sells are when someone can't afford to pay the full price so you offer a more limited experience e.g attending fortnightly/bi-weekly, being on call during cancellations to pop in for a one-off lesson, joining group classes, joining short 4-6 week challenges than taking a break.
![image1079.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1079.png)

*Get Good With Your Accounting:*
Get set up with quality accounting software as early as possible
 Budget and forecast from the previous year's income instead of predicting that you'll experience x% growth. This way all the growth you do experience is net profit and cash in your pocket.
Set aside money for the quiet months during the year. Especially if you haven’t got a reliable summer program implemented yet. Figure out what you're going to need across that period (e.g $20k for 6-weeks without income) then divide that amount by the number of weeks you have until that period starts to calculate the $ amount you need to set aside each week. Set up a separate bank account for this money so you don't touch it.
Set aside your tax throughout the year, consistently. Especially when you take cash out of the business. Set up a separate bank account for this money so you don't touch it.
 Make sure you are depreciating your assets - this lowers your taxable income. Keep a list of all the assets you buy in your business e.g new instruments, vehicles etc... Get your accountant to help you do this as part of your annual tax filing.
Implement a debt collection system to chase unpaid debts when you know you’re in the right. Especially if these debts are quite high
To add to the debt idea, don’t allow families to fall into debt. No payment = no lessons! Being a nice person is a very different thing from allowing people to get into debts they cannot sustain. You have to watch unpaid debts like a hawk!
![image1082.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1082.png)
Check your subscriptions and cancel anything that is doubling up something you’re already paying for. Also, cancel anything you’re not using. You'd be surprised how many subscriptions you might still be paying for even if you don't even need them anymore.
Track your number (daily, weekly, monthly, annually). Knowing your numbers is power. If you don’t know how to run the appropriate reports, ask your accountant. Bonus tip: Access our Studio Profit Formula and cashflow challenge in module 2.
Sign up for a business fuel card to save on petrol. Every dollar counts.
Review all your expenses regularly. Ring and ask for deals and discounts, especially on items where you could use another competitor as they usually have the most wiggle room on their prices (e.g phone, power and other service industries)
Move expenses to annual billing where you can, to save money. Just remember to space out the different annual expenses over the 12-months so that it’s easier for cash flow. At BAM we even offer up a massive discount for an annual membership, so just ask us how to switch.
Maximise all of your tax-deductible expenses. Look through your personal accounts to see what you might be purchasing there that could actually be legitimately paid by your business.

*Think Smart With What You Purchase:*
Always take the time to find the best deal. Just remember that dollars add to hundreds of dollars, and hundreds add to thousands and thousands add to tens of thousands…
To add to this point, ask for a better deal on almost anything. Even (and especially) if you order it over the phone or in person. Most people never even ask and often the person has the power to offer a better deal. Try it you’ll be surprised.
Purchase and use plug and play solutions where possible. The cost of building programs, curriculums, templates and systems yourself vs tweaking 10-20% of what someone else has already done, will save tens of thousands. At the #BAMSQUAD we have full lesson booklets, email scripts, Facebook advert templates, social media images and hundreds of systems and ideas for you to just copy straight into your own school. We also recommend lots of different software that will cost money but will save you thousands in paid labour (or your own time which can then be spent on more valuable tasks).
![image1053.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image1053.jpg)
![image1054.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image1054.jpg)
Buy quality second-hand equipment where possible instead of brand new.
 Be sure to store equipment in cases or safe storage to protect it and lower damage costs over time
Buy in bulk. Buy your toilet paper, paper, printer ink, stationery, wipes, hand sanitiser etc. in bulk and shop around for the best discounts
Purchase music books online in bulk and pay the one shipping fee. Then sell each book at a higher rate to your customer. They have the convenience of getting the book in class without having to purchase it themselves and you can easily charge 25-100% more on each book.
Use the same suppliers and ask for repeat discounts (car servicing, music supplies etc). This can evolve into sponsorships - see module 8 to learn more about how we get hundreds of thousands in grants and sponsorships a year.

*Network In Your Community:*
Put the word out to studio families to see if they have a connection or even a tech-savvy sibling who would like to do photography and videography for your events and even for social media photos. You save money on stock images, and on a professional photographer and it helps to build your brand.
Ask parents in your music school if they have a business that they'd like to collaborate with you on. You may be able to get discounts on food/baking gifts for concerts, cheaper t-shirt merch for students, handmade book bags, ice-cream store vouchers for prizes etc.
Get venues to pay you to hold concerts (10-pin bowling, bars, cafes, play centres etc).
![image1055.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1055.png)
Offer big birthday party packages and pay a teacher an attractive wage to run the party. Try to book them back to back on the same day. You can make the parties really fun with instrument try-outs, fun music games and show bags filled with goodies sponsored by local businesses
Hire out your space when you're not using it. Ideas include AA groups, mum groups, sewing groups, morning yoga classes etc.
When you need specific skills e.g painting your studio, signwriting a car, repairing some damage or anything outside your skillset - Put the word out to your customer list to see if anyone would be keen to volunteer or offer a special price.

*Consider The Time Cost:*
Look for software that saves time for yourself, your teachers and your admin staff
Consider when it's appropriate to pay your admin staff for a task vs outsourcing it to Freelancer or Upwork. For example, it can be cheaper to pay a freelancer to write email nurturing marketing sequences for you or update website text. Their hourly rate is usually lower but also, they’ll do the work faster than an in-person staff member (thus, lowering your per-hourly payment), as they are more experienced in that field
![image1056.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1056.png)
Invest time into good teacher training (including training on how to engage with parents and increase retention rates) so that you're not running ads for new teachers and putting more money into training them alongside another teacher
Use templates as much as possible, to save time on creating new resources or new emails etc, therefore saving money on wages to create those resources.
Don't be afraid to invest in programmes where the work has been done for you. E.g if you want to add vocal lessons to your studio and don't want to build a curriculum from scratch, then you are best to invest in a great curriculum so you can hit the ground running with high-quality vocal lessons from lesson 1. Apply this attitude to every area of business because your time is valuable and buying plug and play resources is ALWAYS cheaper than your hourly rate to build from scratch. That's why the #BAMSQUAD library is so valuable.

*We also asked the #BAMSQUAD community for their best 'Money Hack' ideas. Here are some of our favourites:*
At the #BAMSQUAD, we're very passionate about being collaborative in the way we help music school owners grow their businesses. We might have lots of awesome ideas, but so does all the members of our BAM community! Collaboration helps every single person as the collective result is 10x greater than any one person could think of, on their own.
We reached out to ask what your best ideas were, for this topic. Here are some great answers you gave us!

![image1034.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1034.png)
   Marlayna Goosby
Marlayna pays someone else to manage her finances and this financial manager approves all purchases over $250. She makes decisions based on forecasting. She has a great grasp of Marylayna's AP, income, savings, and taxes and can decide if the school can “afford” something and still remain financially healthy long term. Allowing a financial manager to handle the spending in this way, frees up Marlayna's time and mental energy.
As Marlayna says, "It’s basic accounting, really. And that is NOT my superpower!".
We love that because these clever money hacks are all about doing things smarter and not harder. If there's someone else who can do it better than you, let them!
#### Sharon Davis
Sharon only buys instruments and books once a student has already purchased them. This way, she doesn't have money tied up in stock. She keeps a very minimal supply on hand.
She has a system to make sure she isn't accidentally over purchasing books, too. So, for group lessons, the students book and pay in advance of the term, so she knows exactly which books are needed.
For private students, they do a half-price trial lesson and use the "teacher" copy or the one copy that Sharon keeps for sale. Her book supplier posts new book orders usually within a couple of days, so if the student books in for lessons, she can usually get a copy delivered by the time of their next lesson. If something unexpected comes up, she'll sell the student the one copy she keeps onsite and immediately orders another copy to replace it.
One extra tip she had, was to check the cost of commercially printing PDF style books as it's usually cheaper per book to purchase a bigger quantity at one time.
#### David Braybrook
David has managed to cut down on the headaches and/or money often lost from lesson cancellations. He has all his tutors use that missed-students lesson time to record a video lesson and send it to that student.
These videos are uploaded to YouTube as an unlisted video and he then sends the link via My Music Staff lesson notes. E.g A 30 min lesson means a 15-20 minute recording and then 10min to upload and send the video in that same 30min lesson time slot.
He has a strict 7-day notice for makeups. So if the student cancels outside of that window, they don't get a take-home video. He said that he keeps this policy strict, and then when he is able to offer that video lesson, the parents really appreciate it. Because his teachers do the whole video within the scheduled lesson time it doesn't add any more work for them, or extra wages being paid by the studio.
![image1041.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image1041.png)
  Shawn Reams
Shawn also follows the same lesson cancellation process as David and says that video lessons at home have been a complete game-changer. No more make-ups or scheduling headaches. At his school, they use Lessonmate and love it. It is a super easy software to use and very smooth. As Shawn says, "Lessonmate is incredibly affordable for what it is and what it can do for your business. They are super nice people as well and the customer support has been very good. Definitely worth checking out"
You can check out *.*
![image1049.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image1049.jpg)
#### Nicholas Anderson
Nicholas highly recommended going through the "Profit First" system by Mike Michalowicz. There is a book that outlines all the steps that Mike suggests for having a profitable business. The main point he makes is that you need to prioritise taking home a profit from your business, even if it's a tiny amount. He works in percentages and helps you organise your business finances and accounts in a way that works for each individual person.
As Nicholas says, "I have figured out some of my own percentages based on what I know I need. For example, I put way less than 15% in taxes because I know I won't be paying that much. I follow the others pretty closely. I always take my profit first though. It has been amazing for my business and peace of mind. I really don't worry about money at all"
You can check out more details on
![image1014.jpg](2. Business Backbones_images/image1014.jpg)

#### Kelly Kennedy
Kelly has saved hundreds of dollars by purchasing the premium  and doing any design work in-house. She no longer has to pay the hourly rate for a graphic designer! As Kelly says, "Because Canva is so user friendly, the product still looks good while I'm learning!"
We agree, of course. Here are some designs she's created on Canva, without paying a cent!

# Money Mistakes
Here are 17 of my dumbest money mistakes:

*1. Early bird discounts:*
* *Music lessons are exchanging of time for money so the margin is already small. And since your expenses don't change, a 10% discount could end up being 30 or 40% of your profit. Plus, 10% doesn't sound like much, leaving you feeling pressure to give a greater discount! Also, this targets the child rather than the parent - a child doesn't care about an early bird discount! They aren't the one paying for lessons. It's better to target the child with something that will benefit them. This could be a $10 voucher for McDonalds or a free movie ticket, for example. The child will then want their parent to pay early so they receive that free gift!

*2. Not setting aside money for quiet months:*
It's a painful time if you haven't set money aside! The quiet season is the same every year; make sure you mark them out on a calendar so you know when they're coming up. It's all about forward planning so make sure you factor this in to your budget and set money aside for those quiet months.

*3. Raking up loads of debt:*
* *There is no faster way to stress out your business life and personal life than raking up lots of debt; borrowing more money and spending more money to try and solve problems. It doesn't work! I stumbled across podcasts by Dave Ramsey and found his live Q & A's very helpful - they literally saved my business life and I recommend you check him out! It's important to do what you can to be debt free. There are times where you will need to use debt when you become a larger school so it's important to have the flexibility of an overdraft as you may have some large bills that all come out at the same time. But the ultimate goal is to have your own self-managed overdraft system with a cash buffer. This takes time to work towards but the main points to take from this is, check out Dave Ramsey's podcasts / radio show and don't build up heaps of debt!

*4. Not putting enough effort into debt collection:*
 This point is possibly the most painful for me and is just depressing that people take the service without paying for it. We lost thousands of dollars through not chasing up on debts and trying to be 'Mr. Nice Guy'. Whatever level you're at, whatever size, you need to have a debt collection process in place and you have to act on it. You cannot afford to let everyone off - it will kill your business. You must follow up! A worker is worth their wage and you deserve to be paid the price you agreed upon.

*5. Using my set aside tax to pay for some new equipment or emergency:*
* *It's important to set aside your tax and know the dates when tax is due... but there were times I dipped into that money for an emergency or to buy new equipment. Then, the due date for taxes came around... and there is no-one for painful to owe money to other than the tax department! Interest and penalties are always extreme; make sure you put money aside for tax and don't use it for anything other than tax! There are other ways to get through those emergency situations.

*6. Thinking hiring 2 average people would be better than hiring 1 awesome person:*
This is so important. It doesn't sound like a money mistake, but it is! Don't hire 2 average people and think they equal 1 awesome person. I had a situation where a staff member had finished up, and she had been absolutely amazing. We had some more resources since we had grown bigger, so instead of hiring 1 person to do 40 hours per week, I decided to hire 2 people to work 25 hours per week. I thought this would be a great idea because then I could get 50 hours in total and really expected it to be awesome. Well, unfortunately the 2 that we hired didn't work very well as a team and it was a disaster. The stronger skilled person saw that it wasn't working and handed in their notice. Then I was stuck with the lesser skilled person who I had to stick it out with her. I then had to have a tough discussion with her and let her go before hiring 1 person to fill the admin role. 2 average people do not equal 1 awesome person. The stronger skilled person seems to lower their standards to the lowest common denominator and it does not work well.

*7. Stretching my budget to make jobs better suit someone's needs:*
 Putting an individuals needs above the business's needs is not a good idea. We want to be nice, they've got the skills and you really want them but want things adjusted to better suit them... Maybe they want the hours changed, they want to be paid more etc.. Don't do it! It stresses you out and costs you so much more time and money. You don't need to bend over backwards for everyone, especially when your starting out and still small. Be very careful of this! Know what you need and hire for that.

*8. Not budgeting with a buffer:*
We strive to budget the next year on 90% of the income we earnt from the previous year. We know we are going to grow the next year but by budgeting on 90%, this gives us a buffer for other projects, for growth etc. I used to budget on growth, so I would look at how much we grew the previous year and factor that in! For example, if we made $200,000, I would estimate that we would grow by $40,000 and base our budget on $240,000!! But remember, growth takes the whole year! So in week one, you aren't going to have the revenue immediately, but would still have expenses coming out based on your budget. Always have a buffer!

*9. Not budgeting for breakages:*
 I remember how annoyed I was when things got broken. People just wouldn't take care of our stuff! You can try push your team to be careful and encourage them to be respectful of everything, but the truth is that not everyone is going to be as careful with the equipment as you are. When things break, you feel the sting, not them! Because you're the one that has to pay for it as in most cases, you can't force them to pay for it. So, you need to budget for damage to instruments, knocks to walls, vehicle damage etc.. This is just a cost that most businesses have to budget for. Nothing seems more uncool than sending a bill home to the parents of a kid who has broken something. In some cases you might, but usually it falls on the business to cover the cost, so make sure you're prepared for it!

*10. Not taking the time to find the best deal:*
* *Every dollar adds to ten dollars, which adds to hundreds, which adds to thousands and then tens of thousands. So we want to make sure we always get a good deal on things. Sometimes the best price isn't the cheapest price. There is sometimes pressure, we're in a rush to get something quickly. But make sure you take the time to find the best deal because every dollar counts! When it comes to doing your accounts at the end of the year, you see how much money is wasted. So, always take the time to get the best deal.

*11. Not having the guts to ask for a better deal on almost anything:*
In many cases, you can actually ask people for a better price. Ask them, is there any chance of getting a better price on this? Is there any room to move on this? Do you do any deals for education and young kids?.. I run a little music school and we help lots of kids - we're just a small company. Most times, you will save something! In many stores, even the youngest store clerks have the power to drop the price a little. Just be bold, don't be afraid to ask. Even if it's just 5% - it all makes a difference.

*12. Over ordering on stuff:*
I've often over ordered stuff by being too optimistic. I've ordered too many business cards, or too much equipment or a project that I think will have a huge number of sales but it doesn't actually do that many. Be careful not to be too optimistic and over order because it's just a waste of money and you end up with all these extra things that you will never use. Funny story: I once heard of someone who was trying to order 700 umbrellas but accidently added another zero and ended up with 7000 umbrellas! I haven't made any mistakes that dumb! But always check the little details and make sure you're ordering the correct amount of stock.

*13. Overpaying people:*
 We want to be generous and create job, but often we overpay people because we haven't taken the time to set them up for success and often you've given them more hours than it takes to complete a task. Always think about the output that you want for something. Name every dollar ie. I'm paying you this amount to achieve this output. Whether it's teaching some students, producing some admin results, marketing results, a number of promotional concerts. Whatever it is, you need to think about paying for the results. So in all your agreements, you want to be thinking about the outputs and making them very clear. Otherwise, it's very easy to spend a whole lot of money that you didn't need to spend.

*14. Pouring too much money into new projects:*
* *This stresses out the core of your business. Keep the main thing, the main thing! The main thing for running a music school is music lessons. Even for Build A Music School; we've got the resources to build this for you, but if we tried this 5 years ago, it wouldn't have happened; it would have stressed out the core of our business. You will get a lot of awesome, cool, exciting ideas, especially as you go through this blueprint. But it can't stress out the core of your business, and that's where you need to check out our *Strategic Planning* Lesson. Make sure that you have the resources and the time for what you want to do and that it's the next logical step to bring the most value to your school. Don't stress out the core of your business with new, fun and exciting ideas.

*15. Launching new programs without researching to see whether people actually want it:*
* *The amount of time I've launched projects without even doing an email to see if customers want it! Such a dumb money mistake that can cost you so much. Whenever you are thinking of a new product or service, make sure you survey people first! It might be a great idea but you have to make sure people actually want it and are willing to pay for it. Do your research and make sure you check that people want it before launching; do a soft launch before investing all that money!

*16: Sticking with people I've outgrown:*
This is a tough one because it relates to people and relationships that we have built. As you get bigger and better, if people don't grow with you, you are going to move to a new level of professionalism and they are not going to keep up. You will want to hang on to them because of the relationship but if they don't grow with you, you are going to have to have those tough conversations. If they don't decide to grow and step up, they need to be moved on. They are just going to cost you money and pain in mistakes. This could be a business mentor, an accountant or any of those . I'm really sorry; I love you as a person but I'm going to need to move on to something new because we've reached a new business level. Be encouraged; you're not a bad person if reached a new level and you can't take everyone with you. Just make sure that you keep raising the bar and keep putting the right people with the right skills behind you to support you and grow.

Remember:
Pay attention to the tension
All the tensions you are feeling through your business/finances are happening for a reason and they are problems that can be solved. Pay attention to the tension! Figure out the root cause of what's going on and spend your time fixing them.
Everyone makes mistakes - it's okay! Just remember to walk towards the mess and face your financial problems head on and as quickly as you can.

![image314.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image314.png)
Comment down below with one of your biggest money mistakes and what you learned from it. You never know who it's going to help prevent making the same mistake.

# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - Introduction
Welcome to the studio profit formula mini course. This course is really going to highlight what's going wrong in your teaching business. Most people don't go into teaching business with money as the main factor but perhaps one of the reasons down below.

*They usually start a teaching business for one of these reasons:*
*Meaning in their work: *A job that makes a difference which you enjoy and is meaningful when you are working hard day to day
* A job you love and enjoy: *Find the job fun, enjoy music and are talented. Want to work doing something they enjoy
*Freedom: *Want to be their own boss. Choose your own hours, holidays etc
*Larger impact than if you were working on your own: *You know if you are working on your own you are only working a certain amount of hours a week and you can only impact a certain amount of people where as if you grow a bigger business to impact more people

*Here are the problems:*
*A job you love & enjoy: *People start doing a job they love but it can very quickly turn into a job with one thing you love and loads of other things that you don't love
*Freedom* : Set out to be your own boss, many people discover they don't own a business they just have a job.  That freedom slowly disappears and your working way more when your meant to have freedom
*Want to have a larger impact than they could have on their own: *If you are working on your own you can only work a certain amount of hours a week and impact so many people. So they grow a bigger business to impact more people
*Having a meaningful job:* Even though these 4 things are amazing, the problem which happens is that say you might have gone into your business wanting meaning but becoming your own boss has become a bit of a nightmare as there is so much to do and not enough money to hire the help you need. You feel a bit underpaid and overworked. Although your work is still meaningful and does make a difference for people, that fulfillment that you get from having a meaningful job starts to deteriorate because you may start thinking... Is this actually worth the sacrifice?

*What's the solution:*
The number one thing that fixes these things is *more money*. Not more money at the top line (revenue) coming in, but more at the bottom line (net profit). Money is not the most important thing in life but cash flow and net profit are some of the most important things to keep your business alive and prevent you from going nuts.
As we know in the #BAMSQUAD there is a huge amount of other things you'll need to have a successful studio right down to your leadership, resources, strategy, hiring people etc. You'll need to make sure you have really good net profit otherwise you won't solve those other problems that you either have now or are about to have as you grow. In this training i will reveal the the formula. It is the exact financial formula with the key numbers you need to focus on. I will show you the math, what numbers are important and I'll give you hundreds of ideas for each number of how you increase it and the order in which you should focus on these numbers. I'll be referencing things in the #BAMSQUAD library that you may have already come across or not yet come across which will help you with parts of this training.

………………
# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - The Studio Profit Formula
You can use this spreadsheet to play with all the numbers and see what the true effects will be in your business.
*We will focus on these areas in this video:*
Number of leads X conversion rate = Students
Students X lifetime value = Revenue
Revenue X margin = Net profit
![image342.png](2. Business Backbones_images/image342.png)

Download the attached spreadsheet and have a go.

# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - Number of Leads
In this video we will cover the number of leads you have, tracking & some really awesome ideas on how to improve that number.

*Here are some awesome ideas on how to improve your number of leads: *
Ask your customers how they heard about you and double down on those marketing strategies
Learn how to master Ads on Google
Describe how you’re different on your website, without trashing your competition
Set up a referral system for students with bonuses
Revamp and re-launch an old successful promotion
Use facebook adverts to target people on their  birthday with a special offer for your studio
Use look-a-like targeting on facebook based on your current student list
Be a guest speaker at your local school's music class
Run a stall at a local school or community fair. Collect names and contact details through competition entries and follow up each up manually with an offer. You can also do the same at any reasonably priced trade shows for families in your area
Run promotions with local businesses and give away a product to each business' customers
Take requests to perform people's favourite songs in community facebook groups
Build a great website. Perfect the SEO so you rank number one on all the searches related to your lesson offerings (check out *module 5* for the SEO for music schools course)
Be a guest speaker at local schools
Install building signage
Use emotional angles in your marketing to promote lessons e.g. wish you could play your favourite song on the piano
Signage on your vehicle with a call to action
Run in-studio promotions through a video screen, posters, flyers etc, up-sell to current students / families
Store a file of advert ideas that caught your attention for inspiration for future campaign ideas. You can also research competitor adverts in the Facebook ad library
Run a student concert in a local shopping mall
Set up a referral system with a local music store
Give away water based temporary tattoos with your logo
Run an open house/day for the community
Invite people of influence to visit your studio and post on social media
Share photos and videos of students succeeding in lessons on social media, along with a compelling offer
Run one of large events e.g ukulele and wine evening, adult choir, teen rock band with free pizza. Have a special one Tim offer up-sell to a 6-8week program at the end of the night
Run a concert at a ten pin bowling alley on a busy Saturday afternoon / evening
Run special seasonal campaigns with a special offer. e.g Easter, Christmas, Black Friday, your studios birthday etc
Run viral competitions to attract leads. Only give away lesson packages (with some bonuses, otherwise you’ll get crap leads)
Give away branded bumper stickers for students/parents cars
In-studio promotions via video screen, posters, flyers etc
Run ‘bring a friend’ events at your studio
Have any staff link your studio's Facebook page to their personal pages with their job title e.g. music tutor, administrator
Use free strategies through facebook to attract customers
Launch a quiz funnel. e.g Which instrument should your child learn. Offer a voucher / compelling offer at the end of the sequence
Get interviewed in the local paper
Run competitions to attract leads. e.g colouring competitions or a talent quest
School newsletter ads
Create campaigns with a sign up gift, e.g free movie ticket or pizza
Reach out to these companies to see whether they’ll give you a discount on the prizes
Mother/daughter or father/son nights
Run campaigns for a special one-of deal for duo lessons to strengthen that special bond
Ask students to deliver fliers to houses on their street
Ask students/parents to do a post on social media about you and tag your business page
Sign up for the free online directories and link to your site
Show your lesson options for sale on your Facebook page
Target your competitor's customers with your Google ads
Get interviewed in the local paper
Set up a referral system for students with bonuses
Gift lesson vouchers to school raffle packages and fundraiser nights
Make sure you've got your google my business account optimised. Ask the BAMSQUAD if you need reviews
Make sure you’ve got at least a 4-star rating on Google
Use success stories of students in advert campaigns
Paid advertising on Facebook
Stop making weak offers like 10% off, or first lesson half-price
Up-skill in your copy writing abilities
Offer to do a free masterclass or workshop at a local school, church or music store
Run stalls at trade shows related to families in your area
Run online lessons and target small towns that don't have great lesson options
Run a massive end of school party event for kids with live band
Target facebook adverts to just show to people at the local schools in your area
Show your lesson options for sale on your facebook page
Get your students/parents to review you on yelp or any other similar sites that you consider important to rank on
Run a student concert in the local mall and market your studio
Improve your website's SEO (search engine optimisation)
Identify parents of influence and ask them how they would recommend you promote your business
Have someone interview you on video about your studio. Use on your website/social media.
Create a scholarship prize for young musicians, promote as a big competition with applications
Cross promotions with local businesses
Have posters on-site with special offers/up-sells
Make sure you have brochures on site for people to give to friends
Set up a referral system with a local music store
Provide incentives for tutors to actively encourage students' siblings to sign up for lessons
Create lead magnets that parents would want (e.g 10 best activities for kids in the area) and send them follow up emails about lesson options. Include an offer for your lessons
Get your website ranking on the first page of google for all instrument lesson options you offer. (*SEO course in module #5)*
Set up lead pages for each specific instrument you teach - hyper target that for Google
Branded refrigerator magnets
Run local talent quests
Connect with local youth workers, youth pastors, music directors, community orchestras etc
Put a sign up a friend offer in new students welcome packs
Give away branded stickers
Get signage on your vehicle
Give prizes at recitals/concerts if students bring a friend
Highlight ‘student of the week’ videos on social media
Perform/speak at local networking groups. Give promotional material out. Have it filmed and use for social media too
School newsletter ads
In-store and sidewalk signage
Work with your team to create before / After video clips of students. Use these as marketing clips
You can also create ‘reacts’ clips with the student. Ask them what goes through your mind seeing yourself play like this 1 year ago? What would you tell people who want to play like you etc
Use these videos on IG and any other networks with young people. Make a compelling offer at the end
Run a competition where your students have to send in pictures of their lunchbox with your studio sticker on it
Have them tag it on IG etc. Give away a lunch shout e.g pizzas to their whole class at lunch time
Build an epic Youtube Ad targeted at parents with a special offer to learn with you. E.g one month half price
Keep a swipe file of great advert / marketing Ideas you’ve seen. Set up a shortcut on your phone to be able to quickly screen record a video ad. Google how to do this with your phone model
School concerts are the #1 way to get students in bulk. Check out *module 5* for the full training on how you can approach this in many different ways
Run Facebook competitions where parents have to tag other parents to win a prize. Make sure the prize revolves around lessons (with other bonuses). E.g Buddy lessons. Tag a parent who’s child would like to win a buddy lesson package. 2 x $100 music lesson vouchers, 2 large pizzas and 2 movie tickets
Post in community fb groups with one off offers. e.g. ‘We’d like to give away a $100 music lesson voucher to a child that would love to learn [list instruments you teach], message us at [tag your business page] if your child or a child you know would like this
Give away as many as you’re happy to give away. For every other lead apologise that the $100 vouchers have been snatched up but would they like a $30 or $50 voucher? Then walk them through the booking process and give them your info packs about lessons etc
Showcase videos of your tutors on your website and social media. Sometimes it can be as simple as saying 'Steve has 3 spaces available for guitar lessons, message us asap if you want to snatch one before they go'
Run a massive end of school party event for kids with live band
Have a system to collect testimonials and share them
Capture leads of everyone that visits your website with lead magnets
Have an updates / Vlog / News section on your website where you can write little posts about highlights at your studio. This helps with your google ranking because it’s a site that’s continually being improved with fresh material
Review all the facebook marketing ideas in *module 5*. There’s copy ideas, headline ideas, videos, photos and stacks of creative tricks and tactics
Review all the google marketing copy ideas in *module 5*. There’s stacks of stuff to help you with the various types of google adverts. You should start with Search based ads first
Invest time into getting great video testimonials from students. Check out our resources on how to get them to respond with the answers you need

*Leverage these videos by loading them as:*
Reviews into youtube eg: Goodtime Music Academy Review
Video posts on facebook
Video ads on facebook
Short 60sec videos on instagram
Still photo from the video with the best text quote from the interview

# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - Conversion Rate
Below are some great ideas that will improve your Conversion Rate.
Implement a follow-up process for all enquiries. Check out the template in *module 3*
Set a goal for how fast you’ll reply to all customer enquiries
Make your terms and conditions easy to understand. Provide a summary of the most important ones at the top
Develop templates for sales emails and FAQs
Understand the Questions people ask and update your info pack / automated emails to better explain the answers to those questions
Offer free trial lessons and measure the conversion rate
Test different offers in your marketing. E.g test which performs better out of 2 for 1, Free trial, or nothing
Get your students/parents to review you on Google
Let people know that when they choose to learn with you, they are supporting kids that wouldn’t otherwise have the opportunity to learn
Send 9 word email to old leads (Hi Name, Are you still interested in _______ Lessons?)
Offer more options of lesson times. e.g weekends, daytime, Late nights
Create down sells for those that say lessons are too expensive - e.g Fortnightly, or just a one month pass or even one off lessons they can book individually once a month.  Sometimes some money is better than no money. You can up-sell them as they get hooked, or move them on if someone else wants their spot
Test and measure all marketing ideas to see whether they should be run all year round, once a year or not at all
Train your tutors in the process to sign up new students or how to follow through a lead properly so it’s not just handed to you or your admin on a scrap piece of paper with a phone number. Teach them what to record and what to say
Offer more lesson types. One-on-one, groups, intro classes, band classes etc
Get your students/parents to review you on Facebook. - This helps with social proof
Get your students/parents to review you on Google - This helps with social proof
Ask ex students to come back
Offer a 15min studio tour/ meet and greet
Create a monthly newsletter to your leads list. Create FOMO and Always have a new compelling offer for the month
Train the entire team in sales and customer service. Role play scenarios and test their ability to navigate enquiries from all customer avatars. e.g Parent of a child learning for the first time, an adult wanting to pick up an instrument again after not playing for years
Ensure all your offers have scarcity built in
Ensure your info packs and website have social proof / testimonials built in
Make it easier for customers to sign up. Ask for feedback on how they found the process, - could it be improved in any way
Stay in touch with your waiting list regularly and let them know about all new spots opening that might suit them
Survey people who don’t sign up as to why
Run short term introductory programmes that don't require permanent commitment
Measure your conversion rate of leads to students
Have a tagline for your studio that focuses on the student, not you
Take credit cards, bank deposit & eftpos - NOT cheques
Make phone contact with enquiries within 24-48hrs
Set a goal for how fast you’ll reply to all customer enquiries
Re-target leads that didn’t sign up for lessons after visiting your website
Have a strong ‘About Us’ section on your website - share your passion for what you do
Get your students/parents to review you on Facebook
Don’t be cryptic on your website, list your prices and the benefits of your services
Send a monthly newsletter to your leads
Add value instead of discounting. How can you make your lesson offer more appealing than just one lesson. Look at how we do this in *Module 4*
Try sign up your student's siblings to lessons
Form a waiting list system
Go through your sign up process yourself with fresh eyes, is it easy? What could be improved? Ask a friend (preferably a parent) to do this for you too
Train your tutors in the process to sign up new students
Provide incentives for tutors to actively encourage their students' siblings to sign up for lessons too
Train your team how to run an amazing first lesson and convert all students to regular lessons if it’s a free trial or short term sign up
Report back to your team on the reasons people don’t sign up regularly. Discuss what you can do to try fix each objection to lower the amount of people that don’t convert for that reason in the future
Review the copy writing materials in the BAM library to learn how to write in a way that’s more captivating
Add a chatbot to your website that links to FB messenger, then people can chat direct with you when you’re online. This also allows you to follow up directly to their FB messenger which is 5X more effective that email
When your google reviews rankings are looking great, you should link to this from your website homepage so people can read the reviews
Set an auto-responder on your social media for when you receive a private message
Use gmail plug ins like boomerang to send back any emails you didn’t get a reply to
Follow leads right through until you get an answer. Don’t give up after 2 emails
Create behind the scenes videos about how your lessons work, what people can expect and how you’re always tailoring the experience for each individual student
Send a short personal email to all waiting list leads ‘Hi Name, Are you still interested in [insert instrument] Lessons?

# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - Students
Now you have got your number of leads x your conversion rate which = students! Let's talk about how to track your students!
*Ideas on tracking students:*
You can count the number of students or export the number of active students from your studio management software
You will also want to add students into our cashflow course found in *Module 2. *In this module there is a section where you can enter the number of students you have and the number you want to be at. E.g - Currently have 45 students and aiming for 100 in the next 12 months. This awesome tool will also calculate loads of other numbers and stats for you automatically

………………..
# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - Lifetime Value
Ideas that will improve your Lifetime Value.
Get into a routine of trying to Up-sell students. This could be from 30min to 45 / 60-min lessons, or taking a second lesson time each week
Inform current students about the other lesson types you offer and actively try get them into classes that would benefit them. E.g a singer into piano classes, a drummer into band classes, a guitarist into singer-songwriter classes etc
Develop exclusive higher priced one-off workshops for students. Encourage your teachers to develop 1-2hr workshops that you can sell exclusively to 5-10 students. Run these outside of normal lesson times and come up with a deal that inspires teachers to want to do this more regularly
Sell branded t-shirts and other Merchandise. Sell these with limited time launches as opposed to being available all year round. Use shoplift and build in easy up-sells / add to carts. e.g if they want one t-shirt for $25, offer them an upgrade to 3 x t-shirts in 3 colours for $60
Track your teachers retention rates, look for trends and work on improving these rates. Identify what teachers with the best rates are doing and pair them with those with the worst rates and have them sit in on a lesson or two to watch for professional development
Inform customers of other products or lesson types that might be beneficial to them for a season, or as regular lessons. Remember the value ladder. Saying they should get 4 lessons can lead into getting them hooked and continuing forever. One example might be - a student needing to increase there lessons as they prepare for an upcoming performance or exam
Measure retention rates for the whole studio each month/year and try beat your previous period. (*This is done automatically for you in module 2 )*
Have a retention strategy in place that the team understands. How are you improving customer experience, customer engagement and making learning with you more sticky and hard to quit
Create awesome bonuses and resources for students that go away if they quit. e.g. video library of tutorials, event/performance opportunities etc
Hire tutors to teach the days/times you don’t want to in order to increase your chargeable hours each week
Stop working in your business teaching or doing admin and hire someone so you can work on growing your business
Sell merchandise, drinks and snacks at your student concerts as fundraisers towards scholarship programs for kids that can’t afford lessons
Create a discount for families signing up for 3 or more classes and promote this to families. Reach out to every family taking 2 lessons and try up-sell them to 3 lessons.
Highlight student of the month in your newsletter, make your students heroes
Offer parents to donate towards another child learning that cannot afford lessons
Up-sell resources, merchandise or instruments to customers while they’re excited and in impulse purchase mode. Build this into your induction process
Run programmes during school holidays. Survey parents to see what they would prefer. e.g short workshops or day programmes
Sell tickets to your student concerts
Hire a cinema for a fundraiser towards a charitable programme in your studio. Pick a family friendly movie
Provide a shopping list for starting each new instrument - stock those items yourself
Be the most encouraging music teacher in town. Train your team how to be the same. Discuss situations where it was challenging to be encouraging and help come up with strategies for how to handle it better in future
Ensure you (and any staff) always bring your A-game to lessons, your reputation hangs on it. Make professional development and weekly catch ups part of your culture. Build a culture of continual improvement
Create certificates and gamification that relate to the curriculum you sell, so you sell more books
Create lessons programmes to offer in local schools during hours teachers usually struggle to get work
Share before and after videos of students showing how far they’ve progressed in a year
Create a student development pipeline so students can see the awesome opportunities that await them as they progress
Suggest the most expensive lessons first to leads and learn how to down-sell if necessary. Teach your team the same
Create reward systems with certificates and prizes as students progress
Implement a system for transferring students from one teacher to another more smoothly. E.g a joint lesson with both teachers before the first lesson with the new teacher happens
Always be looking to make your concerts/recitals as fun as possible, but also with new musically challenging performances that every student can be proud of
Ensure your team is checking with parents to see how their child is talking about lessons at home? Do they enjoy it? Do they feel they’re being pushed too hard, not hard enough? What other helpful info could the parent offer?
Create monthly or quarterly challenges at your studio. E.g Record your first Youtube cover video, or practice 30-days In a row and get it signed off by a parent
Create an info pack for everything the new student needs to look for when purchasing a new instrument. End with offering a link to the instrument you sell which meets the criteria. Explain how it’ll save them time looking around if they buy from you direct
Ensure your waiting room area has some entertaining things to do. Focus on things that will build friendships. e.g two player games
Train your team how to teach students with different behavioural management problems or diagnosed conditions on the spectrum. e.g adhd, autism. Etc
Understanding best practices and tactics will help retain these students and make more meaningful progress in lessons
Include the opportunity for songwriting and original compositions for students. Host a competition or concert of original material students can work towards
Run themed concerts e.g Dress like a superhero and give out prizes. Get loads of photos to share on social media etc
Charge extra for early arrivals and late pick ups for any holiday program / summer camps you run. We would run programs 9am-3pm, then charge for additional time either side
Implement an instrument Hireage program. Watch the videos about this in module 8, you’ll be impressed with how easy and profitable this can be
Look out for timetable gaps and reach out to students either side to see if they’d like to claim that spot and double their lesson time. Works best for one-on-one lessons
Run an annual survey to all students. What’s one thing you’re doing well and what’s one thing you can improve. Review all the improvements you can make and make plans to implement the best ideas
Review your whole student on-boarding process. Poor on-boarding is the #1 reason for a high churn rate
Personalise lesson content for each student where possible and drip feed out the content so you keep people coming back for more
Provide monthly newsletter highlights to improve your communication and remind people of all the great stuff you’re offering and how hard you’re working behind the scenes for them
Set targets for how fast you’ll reply to emails
When you have tricky time consuming emails, sometimes it’s better to just send an audio or video email so it’s faster and easier for the person to grasp the context and feel appreciated. We used but have since switched to  which is less than half the price for the same features.
Go above and beyond if you or your team makes a big stuff up. I once drove 30mins at 8pm at night to drop off a box of chocolates to a family that was mucked around and apologised in person at their door. You don’t have to do that, but you can do something. Most customers will usually be pretty cool after a personal apology
Send the ‘We miss you’ email campaign to ex-students once a year. See if there’s any interest in them coming back and offer them a deal to do so
Give your students experiences your competitors aren’t. Look to implement things they love. A great example is doing video recordings of songs they’ve learned so they have something professional to share and that you can share on social media also
Keep your email list tidy. Address all emails to the customers name so it feels personal
Make your lessons more than music. Starting the lesson with rating how your week was out of 10 and asking them about what’s on their mind etc builds that mentoring relationship that not every teacher places importance on. Quitting lessons is then not about quitting lessons, it’s quitting a friend and mentor. That’s a lot harder
Talk to your team about how you want to come across in all communications. I used to tell my team to always add smiley faces and ensure everything is always framed positively
Have a strategic innovation goal each quarter/year. What new thing will you work hard to implement that makes your customer experience that much better. Train everyone involved in how to implement this so the idea is implemented well
