# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - Revenue
So, now we have our number of leads X conversion rate = students. To work out revenue is your number of students X lifetime value = Revenue.
*How to track revenue:*
The easiest way to track this is in your accounting software. At the end of each month you'll punch in all of your numbers into your *cashflow* forecast. You will see the exact amount of revenue there and it should match your accounting software.
In the *cashflow* spreadsheet you will see a breakdown of all that revenue, we will then hopefully see your spreadsheet go up over time. There are also loads of graphs in there to show you how it's trending. All the charts in the *cashflow* spreadsheet are showing you the effects of all the hard work you are doing.

………….
# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - Margin
Below covers How to calculate your profit margin & some ideas on how to improve it.
 *How to calculate your margin:*
Let’s recap how to calculate your profit margin, from the video
Firstly, you’ll need to access your accounting software and write down what your Gross Revenue was for a full year. Gross Revenue is the sum of all money generated by your music school, before taxes or deductions
Then, access your accounting records and write down your Net Profit for a full year. Your Net Profit is the leftover profit after all expenses, including your salary as a business owner
To calculate your Profit Margin, simply divide your Net Profit by your Gross Revenue. Then multiply by 100 to get your answer. You can round this to 1 decimal place
The formula looks like this: profit margin = Net profit / Gross revenue x 100
E.g. 
Gross revenue: $123,758 
Net profit: $14,763
So,
Profit margin = $123,758 / $14,763  x100
Profit margin = 11.9%
This means, that on top of your yearly salary as the business owner, your music school would be bringing in an extra 11.9% in bonus profit

*Ideas that will improve your margin:*
Make plans to move from one-on-one lessons into group classes
Go through your expenses and try to reduce every possible cost
Set yourself sales targets and try to beat them every month
Help every person understand how their role affects the bottom line and give them KPI’s in their jobs that move the needle. (Yes, including you)
Do a monthly review of your finances
Keep overheads to a minimum. Avoid emotional or vanity spending
Review moving all essential subscriptions to annual plans to save money. Look at the cash flow implications as to whether you can afford to do this
Get an accountant
Work with your accountant to identify all the areas that you can reduce taxation expenses
Implement a bad debt expense for any unpaid bills, so that you can reduce your taxable income. Ensure any unpaid debts  for lessons given are loaded here by you or your team
Review your insurance every year. Don’t over insure (especially depreciating assets like cars).
Review every single cost. Do I need it, is the return it brings work more than the cost. Don’t cut costs on the things that have brought savings in manual labour e.g subscriptions, professional development (e.g BAMSQUAD membership), technologies, apps
Implement and Enforce direct debit payments
Charge more for those not on these plans or not prepaying in advance
Implement and Enforce late fees
Develop better negotiation skills for lowering studio expenses
Ask for deals whenever you can. Many times people are actually empowered by their business to be able to give you 10-20% off. You have nothing to lose, so give it a shot. (Don’t ask us for a BAM discount, we’re already under-priced for you ;)
Outsource what you can, before committing to employing staff
If your teachers can legally be contractors, it’s always cheaper. Milk that as long as you can
Review your phone bills for a better plan every year. Play one company off against the other
Approach philanthropists for donations towards lesson programmes for underprivileged kids
Ensure your accountant is active in helping you grow your business, not just filling in your taxes
If you have to fire your accountant and get a better one, do it. It’s worth the money and reduction in stress
Develop jobs that the business needs, not that the person needs
Does everyone have a clear job description where every task plays an important role that leads to increasing the bottom line
Hire the right person, not the cheapest. The right person will make you more money, the cheapest person will cost you more money. It’s your choice
Sell equipment / instruments / furniture in your studio that you’re not using anymore
Get sponsorships for your studio
Make transitional plans to eliminate projects or lesson types that aren’t bringing the return you need
Pay more for a quality service if it will save you time or money (applies to staff too). Sometimes spending more saves you more
Set a budget and review every month. Use the cashflow course in *module 2 *
Offer more lesson types based on the demand/enquiries you receive
Understand your cost to acquire a new student and track all marketing methods. Double down on what works, and tweak what doesn’t. If it still doesn’t work, pause it for now
Charge more for one-off lessons
Increase your prices. Form a routine for this. For us the price went up once a year on the first day of Term 2 on our school calendar (usually around time for our tax year to start April 1st). Pick your date and make it a regular thing that parents know happens and are reminded of well in advance
Invest in technology to speed up productivity
Get rid of customers that cause stress or pay late
Build a relationship with a business banking manager and only work with them
Review how much you’re spending on investing in teachers beyond their pay. E.g thank you presents, food, professional development etc
If you’re not investing in this area it’s hard to build really loyal staff that go the extra mile. The best asset to increasing your profit is great people
In *module 8* you can learn about how we built a charitable trust to help cover overhead costs while making a big difference with many more students
Ask your networks whenever you need to pay for something that needs fixing. The amount of times a builder, plumber, electrician or carpet layer came and did a little job for free or cheap was crazy
Look for concert venues where you can actually be paid to hold the event. Bars / Cafes would pay us $1000+ to host a concert at their venue for a Saturday afternoon
We also did this at a 10-pin bowling alley in exchange for vouchers and free food, which could then use for big social media promotions
Reach out to businesses to ask whether they would sponsor one child to learn for a month / year. Provide them with a web link / video and pdf about your charitable work
If this child is put into spaces in existing group lessons then the donation towards their fees becomes pure profit. Learn more about doing this ethically in *module 8*
Try limit your perishables with holiday programmes / summer camps, but invest in resources, games etc that can be used over and over
Build an online portal for your students to ask you questions and access videos that supplement lessons. Put your lesson prices up when you release this and have it as a free service with the lessons. (Cancel their access if they quit lessons)
Build a relationship with a single music store as a partnership where you can get a discount on any purchases as well as giving your students discounts there. We still bought loads of second hand stuff when we found deals, but when it came to buying new we were loyal to one store
Ask other local businesses for vouchers as prizes for students in exchange for promotion to your list and social media. You can also give them vouchers for lessons in exchange (e.g. 2-4-1 vouchers). This can cut down on prize purchases if you can get enough exciting prizes
Ensure that if you’re being charged a credit card fee that you’ve factored that into your pricing. It’s best to increase your prices and have the fee included wherever possible
Ensure you’re keeping an accurate record of the assets you’ve purchased and your accountant is depreciating these every year to lower your tax bill. More on this in the cashflow course in* module 2*
Quarterly Review all staff hours and what’s done in those hours. Does everyone need to be working the number of hours they work or the number of weeks in a year they work
Create a system for missed lessons, catch ups etc that is weighted in your favour. Check out *module 3* for great strategies on this.
Have a no refund policy, but a company credit policy (except for circumstances you decide where it’s better to refund)
Review your design fees. Can this be done cheaper in house with a canna subscription. You might like to just pay a professional to set you up with your templates and brand guidelines in a way that you can build the bulk of designs yourself going forward
Alternatively online design sites like fiver, freelancer and up work etc can get you access to much cheaper designers. It’s essential you have your brand guidelines and well written briefs if you want good results though
Review your website domain costs. Some providers will charge differences of $30-50 in some cases. It may be worth moving, but do your research and read reviews first
Build templates for all your systems. e.g hiring someone and creating contracts etc. The man hours saved will be astronomical
Review everything that takes up your time and your teams time. Identify what systems and templates you need to save time. Reach out to the BAMSQUAD before building anything from scratch
If you have an eftpos / point of sale machine, review your contract every year and get multiple quotes for a new contract price. Ultimately you need to move to a credit card only direct debit system where there is no need for POS systems
Create a broken gear area with a clipboard for staff to report what’s broken. Wherever possible, attempt to fix everything you can instead of replacing it.
The clipboard process will reveal any patterns of staff who are regularly having gear broken under their watch. Discuss this with them and how you can improve things going forward. Sometimes it’s as simple as providing better storage solutions in their teaching room. e.g. hooks for headphones / leads
Apply for a fuel card as a business and run your petrol through it. We were able to save 5%, which doesn’t sound like a lot but with 7 company vehicles it added to over $700 per year. For you it may only be $100, but that’s $100 more in your pocket
Review your google cloud storage costs are you on the right plan or are you paying for more storage than you actually need right now
Review any additional team emails you’re paying for that are no longer active. e.g. old staff emails
Google charges us about $5 per person, per month for their business accounts.
Make sure your accountant is claiming a portion of your home rent / mortgage payments towards a home office. This can greatly reduce your tax costs
Look at your interest expense on any accounts that have an overdraft facility. How can you ensure money is moved around your accounts to reduce the amount and the length that you’re in overdraft for. Ultimately your goal is to build an emergency fund and remove your overdraft completely as these are usually at very high interest rates
Sign up with an office supplies company for a discount card / loyalty program. We were able to get a business rewards program that gave us 7% discounts on all purchases.
Create a lockup checklist for the last person to leave the studio each night. Ensure that everything is turned off to reduce power costs
Look at heating options for each room in your office / studio rooms. Are you using the most cost effective heating solution? Would it be better to invest in a better heater that will reduce power costs over the next year?
Transition to energy efficient light bulbs to save on power. If you’re renting, you can actually keep the old bulbs to replace when you go and take the new ones with you, or sell them
Have tutors email students PDFs to print at home instead of at the studio. If you have a customer portal where you can load these in stead that’s even better
If you have staff, then look into a cheap payroll service. Payroll fees for processing the pay for even two people is cheaper than your hourly rate for doing this yourself manually. This frees you up to do more profitable work instead
Bulk purchase your cleaning supplies including toilet paper, hand towels etc
Build a relationship with a local mechanic where you get discounted repairs in exchange for promotional deals to your students / social media etc
Review what skills you’ve been having to contract out. E.g website building, Design, cleaning etc.. How can you develop yourself or someone internally to help reduce this cost
Ensure you have a dunning system on automatic payments. A dunning management system is a solution that automatically retries a failed payment or expired credit card and sends a drip of renewal notifications to customers whenever a charge to their credit card is declined
Ensure your digital marketing campaigns have excluded audiences loaded in of your current students so your adverts are not showing to them and costing you money. If you have a customer portal that allows you to track traffic, then you can also load the portal page as an exclusion so anyone that’s been on that page (students/parents) will not be shown your adverts
Consider removing unprofitable lesson options. We once decided to remove both saxophone and violin lessons (about 4 years apart) from our lesson offerings. There was so much demand for more popular instruments and they were always struggling to grow at the rate we needed. We made the call to cut them. People were disappointed, one mum even sent me hate mail.  But we never looked back. It was the right decision for us
Run the maths on a price increase and how many people would have to quit over it for you to actually lose money. You’ll be surprised at the maths and feel much more comfortable about raising the price each year
Give your staff targets to meet that relate to the bottom line. Yes even teachers. We measured every teacher's retention every quarter and gave a prize for the person with the top percentage. Conversations were had very quickly with those not performing
Explain to your team the only 2 main ways to earn more money as a business is to increase sales and reduce expenses. Explain how the little things matter. Turning lights / amps off when not in use. Using one paper towel in the bathroom instead of 3 etc… it all adds up
Diversify your income into related services and products that can be sold beyond your students. Check out *module 8* for stacks of ideas and examples
Every year you should privately rank your team from best to worst. Give extra attention, bonuses and rewards to the top 20%. You want them around for as long as possible. Work with the middle 70% to develop them so they can get to the top 20%.  Remove the bottom 10% in order to raise the bar. Fire them, don’t renew their contracts or encourage them out the door. Better staff means a better performing business, it’s that simple. You can do it with grace and in a loving way

………
# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - Net Profit
In this video we will discuss how to calculate the profit value of each student & What you can do with your net profit.
*What is a student worth to you as a music school owner?*
Let’s recap how to calculate the value per customer, from the video. Firstly, you’ll need to know what your Profit Margin is. We covered this in the last video. The formula looks like this: Profit Margin = Net profit / Gross Revenue x 100
Let’s say these are your figures:
Gross Revenue: $123,758
Net Profit: $14,763
Profit Margin = 11.9%
To calculate your Value per Customer, simply divide your Net Profit by the number of students you currently have in your music school
*The formula looks like this: Value per customer = Net profit / Number of students*
E.g.
Net Profit: $14,763
Number of Students: 83

Value per Customer = $14,763 / 83
Value per Customer = $177.87
This means that for every student you’ve had, you know they’re worth about $178 to your music school.
 From this data, you can then see what your profit increase would look like if you added more students to your music school
In this example, let’s say you have a goal of getting to 100 students. That’s 17 more students
Simply multiply 17 students x the “Value per customer” which is $177.87.
That’s $3,023.75 extra that you’ll profit per year if you acquire 17 more students

*What Can You Do With Your Net Profit?*
There are 3 things you can do with your net profit. Have a think about these options and how you'd like to distribute the profit that you build in your own music school
If you can, try to do all three options
*Take the money for yourself: *Think about how much time and effort you put into keeping your music school going. Supporting your staff and students. It's important that you pay yourself well and try to increase your salary every year, so you can feel like your time is valued. You don't want to burn out. Remember if the boss isn't happy, no one is happy
*Reinvest the money into your business:*  As you put money back into the business, you're able to implement new strategies which will in turn grow your school and support your students too. By reinvesting into your business, you're reinvesting into your take-home profit. You're also ensuring that your retention rate is high and students continue to learn with your school
*Give it away:* This is where your charitable focus will fit in. You can think about whether you'd like to give a percentage of your net profit to charity. Or perhaps aim to reach a specific number every year. A lot of music schools choose a music-related charitable focus. So not only does giving back help boost the way your school is viewed in the community, but you're helping a lot of people access music in a meaningful way

……………..
# STUDIO PROFIT FORMULA - Conclusion
*Some concluding thoughts of this course:*
*Don't get overwhelmed :* There have been loads of ideas given and there will be many more. This can be overwhelming, we just want you to be aware of the formula.
*Reinvest back into your business*: You want to spend money investing in the things that are going to move those numbers the most
